# Software building blocks for a self-hosted personal AI agent app (as of October 2026): multi-provider models, memory, MCP/connectors, in-app editors/viewers, chat UI kits & desktop shells, personalized feed/ideas/goals

Research date: 2026-10-18. Scope: software building blocks for a self-hosted personal AI agent app (Apple Silicon desktop + self-hosted Linux server, shared React UI). Method: six parallel domain researchers plus a cross-cutting precedents pass, all restricted to primary sources (official docs, GitHub/npm/PyPI registries, vendor blogs, arXiv) fetched on the research date; the shared web-search budget was exhausted early, so most facts come from direct fetches and anything unverifiable is listed under Gaps rather than asserted. Versions, licenses and maintenance status are stated per library; AGPL/GPL/source-available/commercial terms are flagged inline. The document is organised as: an executive synthesis (this section), then the six domains (Models, Memory, MCP, Editors, UI/Shells, Feed/Ideas/Goals), then a precedents section on how OpenClaw, Hermes Agent and Khoj are assembled. Each `##` section follows the Takeaway / Cited Findings / Inferences / Gaps template; section headings are prefixed with their domain so the report writer can locate material quickly.

## Executive synthesis: decision-ready picks and trade-offs across all six domains (read first)

### Takeaway
A TypeScript core on Vercel AI SDK v7 (own model router on top), an OpenClaw-style markdown + SQLite memory (sqlite-vec/FTS5, provenance-tagged, nightly consolidation), an MCP host targeting spec 2026-07-28 with per-tool allow/ask/deny and `srt`/container sandboxing, licence-clean viewers (MDXEditor or CodeMirror 6, Univer, PDF.js 6, mammoth/docx-preview + Gotenberg, Shiki 4, sandboxed-iframe artifacts), assistant-ui on shadcn v4/Tailwind v4 with Radix-Colors-style OKLCH scales, and an Electron (or Swift + Electron-free CDP sidecar) shell around a long-running local gateway is the configuration the evidence supports. For the feed and proactivity layer, the market has retreated from autonomous feeds (ChatGPT Pulse retired June 2026, OpenClaw removed inferred commitments in v2026.8.1) toward user-declared recurring briefs plus confirmed suggestions, and the 2026 research literature says the same: gate proactive items on calibrated acceptance probability and batch them into scheduled windows.

### Cited Findings
**Models and providers**
- Vercel AI SDK is on v7 (`ai@7.0.143`, Oct 16, 2026; v7.0 released June 25, 2026; v6.0.x still maintained), Apache-2.0, ESM-only on Node 22+, with `ToolLoopAgent` (default `stopWhen: isStepCount(20)`), `toolApproval`, `runtimeContext` and an `@ai-sdk/mcp` client — [Source](https://github.com/vercel/ai/releases), [Source](https://vercel.com/blog/ai-sdk-7), [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/reference/ai-sdk-core/tool-loop-agent); its most-upvoted open issue (#2636) is the absence of built-in retry/fallback across providers — [Source](https://github.com/vercel/ai/issues?q=is%3Aissue+state%3Aopen+sort%3Areactions-%2B1-desc)
- LiteLLM v1.106.0 (Oct 18, 2026) is MIT except the separately licensed `enterprise/` directory, Python >=3.10 only — [Source](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/releases), [Source](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/blob/main/LICENSE); OpenRouter now always includes usage/cost in responses and charges 5% on BYOK traffic — [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/use-cases/usage-accounting), [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/use-cases/byok)
- Current Claude IDs are `claude-fable-5-1` ($10/$50 per MTok), `claude-opus-5-5` ($4/$20), `claude-sonnet-5-5` ($2/$10), `claude-haiku-5-5` (from $0.10/$0.50), all 1M context / 128K output with adaptive thinking; extended-thinking `budget_tokens` is not accepted on current models — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/overview); the `memory_20250818` tool needs no beta header and works on all Claude 4+ models — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/memory-tool); on-demand server-side compaction uses beta `compact-2026-09-04` — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/compaction-on-demand); `computer_toolset_20260801` is GA — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/computer-use-tool)
- OpenAI's flagship models are `gpt-6-astra` ($10/$50), `gpt-6.1-sol` ($2/$10) and `gpt-6-luna` ($0.10/$0.50) with 1.05M context on the Responses API — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models); Gemini 4 Argon went GA on Oct 13, 2026 and the Interactions API (Dec 11, 2025) is primary while `generateContent` is "legacy" but supported — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/changelog), [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/interactions)
- Ollama v0.40.6 (MIT) runs an MLX runner on Apple Silicon (announced June 11, 2026) and exposes an Anthropic-compatible `/v1/messages` with tools and thinking (no `tool_choice`/`cache_control`) — [Source](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/releases), [Source](https://ollama.com/blog), [Source](https://docs.ollama.com/api/anthropic-compatibility); LM Studio's terms (Aug 23, 2026) license it "solely for Your personal and / or internal business purposes" (proprietary) — [Source](https://lmstudio.ai/terms); the mlx-lm server "is not recommended for production" — [Source](https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/blob/main/mlx_lm/SERVER.md)
- Usable local tool-calling models in 2026: Qwen3.8-27B (Apache-2.0; 18 GB on Ollama) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B), [Source](https://ollama.com/library/qwen3.8); Gemma 4 31B (Apache-2.0 tag; tau2 avg 76.9%) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-4-31b-it); gpt-oss-20b (Apache-2.0; 14 GB) — [Source](https://ollama.com/library/gpt-oss); GLM-4.7-Flash 30B-A3B (MIT; tau2 79.5) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/zai-org/GLM-4.7-Flash); Llama 4 has not been updated since May 2025 and carries a custom community license — [Source](https://huggingface.co/meta-llama), [Source](https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Llama-4-Scout-17B-16E-Instruct)

**Memory**
- Claude.ai memory is now per-"Topic" and editable in Settings > Memory, updated in real time rather than nightly — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work); Claude Code auto memory is a `MEMORY.md` index (first 200 lines / 25 KB loaded) plus topic files with `type`/`modified` frontmatter — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory)
- OpenClaw (MIT, 392.4k stars, release 2026.10.5 on Oct 18, 2026) injects `AGENTS.md`/`SOUL.md`/`USER.md` every session under 20,000-char-per-file / 60,000-char total budgets (4,000 for `USER.md`), keeps `MEMORY.md` main-session-only and daily notes retrieval-only — [Source](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw), [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/agent-workspace); search is per-agent SQLite with FTS5/BM25 plus float64 BLOB vectors, sqlite-vec optional with automatic in-process cosine fallback, 400/80-token chunks — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-builtin); every index entry carries an origin class (`owner`/`agent`/`untrusted`/`system`) and network-tainted turns never become `owner` — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-architecture); nightly "dreaming" (`0 3 * * *`) is the only writer to `MEMORY.md` — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/dreaming)
- mem0 (Apache-2.0, `mem0ai` 2.2.1, Sep 25, 2026) now does "single-pass ADD-only extraction" and states its 2026 benchmark scores "reflect Mem0's managed platform" — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mem0ai/mem0/main/README.md), [Source](https://pypi.org/project/mem0ai/); Letta has moved to git-backed MemFS markdown files and its legacy Docker server is "no longer an actively maintained or supported" surface — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/concepts/memfs/index.md), [Source](https://docs.letta.com/guides/selfhosting); Zep Community Edition is discontinued while Graphiti (`graphiti-core` 0.31.1, Apache-2.0) continues — [Source](https://github.com/getzep/zep), [Source](https://pypi.org/project/graphiti-core/); Cognee 1.6.5 (Apache-2.0) runs keyless with local extraction/embeddings — [Source](https://pypi.org/project/cognee/), [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/topoteretes/cognee/main/README.md); Hindsight (MIT) claims LongMemEval SOTA "independently reproduced" — [Source](https://github.com/vectorize-io/hindsight)
- sqlite-vec was revived in March 2026 (v0.1.9 stable; ANN indexes in 0.1.10 alphas; MIT/Apache-2.0) — [Source](https://github.com/asg017/sqlite-vec/releases); LanceDB 0.40.0 (Apache-2.0) ships macOS arm64 and Linux x86_64/aarch64 wheels — [Source](https://pypi.org/project/lancedb/); local embedders: nomic-embed-text-v1.5 (768-d MRL, Apache-2.0, task prefixes required) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/nomic-ai/nomic-embed-text-v1.5), EmbeddingGemma-300m (768-d, Gemma license) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/google/embeddinggemma-300m), Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B (1024-d, 32K, Apache-2.0) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B); Apple's `NLContextualEmbedding` (macOS 14+) is documented for classification rather than similarity and the Foundation Models framework page mentions no embeddings API — [Source](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/naturallanguage/nlcontextualembedding.json), [Source](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/foundationmodels.json)
- Microsoft's AI Red Team memory-poisoning case study rose from 4/10 to over 80% success once the agent was told to check memory before acting — [Source](https://cdn-dynmedia-1.microsoft.com/is/content/microsoftcorp/microsoft/final/en-us/microsoft-brand/documents/Taxonomy-of-Failure-Mode-in-Agentic-AI-Systems-Whitepaper.pdf); LongMemEval (500 questions; knowledge updates, temporal reasoning, abstention) found a 30% accuracy drop for commercial assistants — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10813)

**MCP and connectors**
- The current MCP spec is 2026-07-28 (RC May 21, final July 28, 2026): sessions and `initialize` removed in favour of per-request `_meta` and `server/discover`, Multi Round-Trip Requests replace sampling/elicitation/roots, Tasks became an extension, and Roots/Sampling/Logging/HTTP+SSE/Dynamic Client Registration are deprecated on a 12-month clock — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/changelog), [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2026-07-28/); MCP Apps (`io.modelcontextprotocol/ui`) became the first official extension on Jan 26, 2026 with Claude, Goose, VS Code and ChatGPT support — [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2026-01-26-mcp-apps/); MCP was donated to the Linux Foundation's Agentic AI Foundation on Dec 9, 2025 — [Source](https://www.anthropic.com/news/donating-the-model-context-protocol-and-establishing-of-the-agentic-ai-foundation)
- The official registry is still "in preview", metadata-only, with minimal moderation and namespace proof via GitHub or DNS/HTTP only — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/registry/about.md); the TypeScript SDK v2 is the stable line, split into `@modelcontextprotocol/client|server|core` (2.3.1) with v1 `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` at 1.32.1 — [Source](https://ts.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io/v2/), [Source](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/typescript-sdk/releases)
- Gmail `gmail.readonly`/`gmail.modify`/`gmail.compose` are restricted scopes and `gmail.send` is sensitive — [Source](https://developers.google.com/workspace/gmail/api/auth/scopes); verification is not required for personal use by "fewer than 100 users" or Internal Workspace apps, but unverified apps face a lifetime 100-new-user cap — [Source](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464323); External + Testing projects issue refresh tokens that expire in 7 days — [Source](https://developers.google.com/identity/protocols/oauth2); Google's own agent-facing tool is the `gws` CLI (Rust, Apache-2.0, "not an officially supported Google product", AES-256-GCM keyring token storage) rather than an MCP server — [Source](https://github.com/googleworkspace/cli)
- First-party remote MCP servers exist for Slack (`mcp.slack.com/mcp`; no DCR; Marketplace or internal apps only) — [Source](https://docs.slack.dev/ai/mcp-server/), Notion (`mcp.notion.com/mcp`, OAuth, all plans) — [Source](https://developers.notion.com/docs/get-started-with-mcp), Linear (`mcp.linear.app/mcp`, OAuth 2.1 with DCR or API key) — [Source](https://linear.app/docs/mcp) and GitHub (`api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/`, OAuth or PAT, toolsets, MIT) — [Source](https://github.com/github/github-mcp-server); Slack throttles `conversations.history` to 1 request/minute for non-Marketplace distributed apps since May 29, 2025 (internal apps exempt) — [Source](https://docs.slack.dev/changelog/2025/05/29/rate-limit-changes-for-non-marketplace-apps)
- The spec says clients "MUST consider tool annotations to be untrusted unless they come from trusted servers" — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools); Invariant Labs documented tool poisoning, rug pulls and cross-server shadowing — [Source](https://invariantlabs.ai/blog/mcp-security-notification-tool-poisoning-attacks); Anthropic's `srt` sandbox-runtime (Apache-2.0) wraps stdio servers with macOS Seatbelt / Linux bubblewrap+seccomp and a domain-allowlisted proxy — [Source](https://github.com/anthropic-experimental/sandbox-runtime); Docker MCP Gateway (MIT) adds `--verify-signatures`, `--block-secrets`, `--log-calls` — [Source](https://github.com/docker/mcp-gateway); an OWASP MCP Top 10 (Beta) exists — [Source](https://owasp.org/www-project-mcp-top-10/); Anthropic's Software Directory Policy (Apr 15, 2026) requires annotations, OAuth with CA certificates, <=64-char tool names and no hidden instructions — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/13145358-anthropic-software-directory-policy); `.mcpb` bundles store `sensitive` user_config in the OS keychain — [Source](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/mcpb/blob/main/MANIFEST.md), [Source](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/desktop-extensions)

**Editors and viewers**
- MDXEditor 4.3.3 (MIT) is the only Markdown editor with a first-party rich/source/diff toggle — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdxeditor/editor/latest), [Source](https://mdxeditor.dev/editor/docs/diff-source); BlockNote's `@blocknote/xl-*` packages are "GPL-3.0 OR PROPRIETARY" and its export is `blocksToMarkdownLossy` — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@blocknote/xl-multi-column/latest), [Source](https://www.blocknotejs.org/docs/features/export/markdown); TipTap 3.32.0 core and the new `@tiptap/markdown` are MIT but the latter is an "early release" — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@tiptap/core/latest), [Source](https://tiptap.dev/docs/editor/markdown)
- npm `xlsx` is frozen at 0.18.5 with an unfixed High-severity advisory ("A non-vulnerable version cannot be found via npm"); SheetJS CE 0.20.3 must be installed from cdn.sheetjs.com — [Source](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-4r6h-8v6p-xvw6), [Source](https://docs.sheetjs.com/docs/getting-started/installation/nodejs/); Univer 1.1.0 (Apache-2.0, Oct 17, 2026) keeps formulas in OSS while xlsx import/export, charts and pivots are Pro — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/dream-num/univer/releases/latest), [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/dream-num/univer/dev/README.md); Handsontable requires a licence key even for non-commercial use and HyperFormula is GPL-3.0-only — [Source](https://handsontable.com/docs/javascript-data-grid/software-license/), [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/hyperformula/latest)
- pdfjs-dist is 6.4.299 (Apache-2.0) and react-pdf 11 requires React 19 and Chrome 125 / Safari 18 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/pdfjs-dist/latest), [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/wojtekmaj/react-pdf/releases/latest); EmbedPDF's core is Apache-2.0 (PDFium fork; only `cloudpdf/server` is fair-source) — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/embedpdf/embed-pdf-viewer/main/LICENSE)
- DOCX: mammoth 1.13.0 (BSD-2) and docx-preview 0.4.1 (Apache-2.0) are maintained — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/mammoth/latest), [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/docx-preview/latest); no maintained permissive PPTX renderer exists (PPTXjs last pushed 2022, PPTX2HTML 2017) so conversion goes through LibreOffice headless or Gotenberg v8.38.1 (MIT) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/meshesha/PPTXjs), [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/g21589/PPTX2HTML), [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/gotenberg/gotenberg/releases/latest); Collabora Online is MPL-2.0 and ONLYOFFICE Document Server is AGPL-3.0 — [Source](https://github.com/CollaboraOnline/online/blob/master/COPYING), [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/ONLYOFFICE/DocumentServer)
- Monaco 0.57.0 is 101.7 MB unpacked and its workers fail on `file://` pages — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/monaco-editor/latest), [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/monaco-editor/main/README.md); Shiki 4.5.0 offers a JavaScript RegExp engine that avoids the WASM download — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/shiki/latest), [Source](https://shiki.style/guide/regex-engines); Streamdown 2.7.0 (Apache-2.0) is a sanitising streaming Markdown renderer — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/streamdown/latest)
- MDN warns that combining `allow-scripts` with `allow-same-origin` lets a same-origin embedded document remove its own sandbox — [Source](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/iframe); Claude Artifacts run on a separate `claudeusercontent.com` origin with a CSP that blocks external `connect-src` — [Source](https://simonwillison.net/2024/Oct/21/claude-artifacts/); iframe-resizer 5.5.9 is GPL-3.0 with paid commercial tiers — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/iframe-resizer/latest), [Source](https://iframe-resizer.com/pricing/); Sandpack (Apache-2.0) can self-host its bundler on a separate subdomain — [Source](https://sandpack.codesandbox.io/docs/guides/hosting-the-bundler); WebContainers require a commercial licence for for-profit production use — [Source](https://webcontainers.io/enterprise)

**Chat UI kits, theming, shells**
- `@assistant-ui/react` is at 0.15.29 (MIT), updated Oct 15, 2026, with `react-ai-sdk` and `react-langgraph` companions — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@assistant-ui/react/latest), [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@assistant-ui/react-ai-sdk/latest); Vercel AI Elements' standalone registry is deprecated and "will be replaced by the shadcn/ui version of AI Elements" — [Source](https://github.com/vercel/ai-elements)

**Feed, ideas, goals**
- ChatGPT Pulse launched Sept 25, 2025 for Pro as 5-10 overnight cards built from chats, memory, Gmail and Calendar — [Source](https://techcrunch.com/2025/09/25/openai-launches-chatgpt-pulse-to-proactively-write-you-morning-briefs/) — and on June 18, 2026 OpenAI said Pulse would shut within 14 days, pointing users to a relaunched "Scheduled tasks" feature for daily briefings — [Source](https://gigazine.net/gsc_news/en/20260619-chatgpt-scheduled-tasks/); Gemini added "Daily Brief" and the Ultra-only "Spark" proactive agent on May 19, 2026 — [Source](https://gemini.google/release-notes/)
- OpenClaw's automation docs state "The inferred commitments experiment was removed in v2026.8.1, so heartbeat no longer extracts follow-ups from conversations. Explicit automations are the replacement." — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation)
- Google Discover's user controls are follow topic, "Not interested in [Topic]", "Don't show content from [Source]" and like/save — [Source](https://support.google.com/websearch/answer/2819496); Readwise "Mastery" resurfaces items with 7/14/28-day half-lives once recall probability falls to 50% — [Source](https://docs.readwise.io/readwise/docs/faqs/reviewing-highlights); Particle clusters coverage into per-story cards with "Opposite Sides" — [Source](https://techcrunch.com/2024/11/12/particle-launches-an-ai-news-app-to-help-publishers-instead-of-just-stealing-their-work); Kagi News builds one non-personalised daily briefing from thousands of RSS feeds and open-sources its front end (MIT) and feed lists — [Source](https://blog.kagi.com/kagi-news), [Source](https://github.com/kagisearch/kite-public)
- Bing Search/News APIs were retired on Aug 11, 2025 — [Source](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/bing/search-apis/); paid replacements price at Brave $5/1k (with $5 free monthly credit) — [Source](https://brave.com/search/api/), Exa $4-15/1k — [Source](https://exa.ai/pricing), Tavily 1,000 free credits/month — [Source](https://docs.tavily.com/documentation/api-credits); Miniflux (Apache-2.0) and Karakeep (AGPL-3.0, LLM tagging, RSS ingest) are the self-hosted feed and saved-items backends — [Source](https://github.com/miniflux/v2), [Source](https://github.com/karakeep-app/karakeep)
- Ranking literature: LLM zero-shot rankers show position and popularity bias — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.08845); LinUCB gave a 12.5% click lift on Yahoo! Front Page — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1003.0146); sleep-time compute cuts test-time compute ~5x when queries are predictable — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13171)
- Proactivity research: ProactiveBench (6,790 events, F1 66.47%) — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12361); PRISM intervenes only when calibrated acceptance probability exceeds an asymmetric-cost threshold (-22.78% false alarms) — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01532); ProAgentBench (28,000+ real events; memory improves timing prediction) — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04482); a unified silent/ask/assist/act framework warns "offline classification performance does not predict real deployment benefit" — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03727); human agreement on where to intervene is near chance (Krippendorff's alpha = +0.047) — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04296)
- Skill induction: Agent Workflow Memory (+24.6% Mind2Web, +51.1% WebArena relative) — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07429); Agent Skill Induction (+23.5% vs static agent) — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06821); SkillWeaver (+31.8% WebArena) — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07079); shipped precedents are Devin's daily "Dreaming" consolidation — [Source](https://docs.devin.ai/product-guides/memory.md) and OpenClaw's "Skill Workshop" where agents create and update their own versioned skills — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/skills); Claude Code offers cloud routines (cron >= 1 h, API fire) and local Desktop scheduled tasks (1-minute granularity, self-rescheduling) — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/routines), [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/desktop-scheduled-tasks)

### Inferences
- Model layer: build on AI SDK v7 but own the router. The SDK gives normalized tool calling, UI message streams and cache/reasoning usage fields, yet has no cross-provider fallback; OpenClaw and Goose both implement their own primary/fallback model refs with cooldowns (Models section 6), and `prepareStep`'s `model` override is the natural seam to do the same. Store provider-native assistant turns opaquely because Claude, OpenAI and Gemini all require signed/encrypted reasoning to be round-tripped verbatim.
- Cost accounting: reuse LiteLLM's `model_prices_and_context_window.json` as the price table (no need to run LiteLLM) and normalize to the AI SDK v7 usage shape (`cacheReadTokens`, `cacheWriteTokens`, `reasoningTokens`); OpenRouter is the shortcut when breadth matters more than data-locality.
- Local inference: target Ollama first (MIT, MLX on Apple Silicon, OpenAI- and Anthropic-compatible endpoints, same binary on Linux), with llama.cpp `llama-server` as the portable fallback and LM Studio as an optional user-chosen backend (proprietary terms, but personal use is allowed). Default local agent models for a 32-64 GB Mac: Qwen3.8-27B or Gemma 4 26B-A4B/31B; gpt-oss-20b or GLM-4.7-Flash for 16-32 GB; treat Llama 4 as legacy.
- Memory: copy OpenClaw's layering rather than adopting a framework. Always-injected persona/user/rules files with hard character budgets, a curated `MEMORY.md` that only a nightly consolidation job writes, daily notes indexed but not injected, a single SQLite file with FTS5 + sqlite-vec (brute-force fallback is fine at personal scale), provenance tags on every entry, and a memory editor UI with pause/reset/incognito. Adopt the `memory_20250818` command set (view/create/str_replace/insert/delete/rename) as the internal memory-tool protocol so Claude can use it natively and other models can use it through the same handler. Benchmark on LongMemEval-style tasks; discount vendor LoCoMo numbers.
- MCP host: implement 2026-07-28 semantics with a 2025-11-25 fallback using TypeScript SDK v2; do not build on Roots/Sampling/Logging; use CIMD for the host's OAuth identity. Three trust tiers (bundled / namespace-verified / unknown), per-tool allow/ask/deny keyed `mcp__<server>__<tool>`, approvals bound to a hash of `tools/list` so description changes revoke them, full-argument confirmation dialogs, stdio servers under `srt` or a container with no network by default, per-server secrets injected from the OS keychain, and an append-only audit log.
- Google Workspace: for a single-user self-hosted app, keep the GCP consent screen External + Testing (no verification, no CASA) and build 7-day re-auth into the UI, or publish unverified and live with the warning screen and the lifetime 100-user cap; request `gmail.readonly` + `gmail.send` + `drive.file` as the minimum pair; use Pub/Sub pull subscriptions for Gmail push and renew `watch` daily. Wrapping the `gws` CLI as a stdio MCP server is the fastest route to Google-maintained API coverage.
- Viewers: MDXEditor (or CodeMirror 6 as the canonical source editor with an optional WYSIWYG view) for Markdown; SheetJS CE from the CDN tarball + TanStack Table for viewing and Univer for editing spreadsheets; PDF.js 6 (self-hosted worker) with EmbedPDF v2 for annotation needs; docx-preview/mammoth for DOCX and Gotenberg or `qlmanage` for PPTX; CodeMirror 6 + Shiki 4 (JS engine, fine-grained bundle) for code; Streamdown for streamed chat Markdown; artifacts in `sandbox="allow-scripts"` srcdoc iframes on a second origin with a meta CSP and a home-grown ResizeObserver/postMessage resizer; Sandpack with a self-hosted bundler for React artifacts. Avoid Handsontable/HyperFormula, BlockNote XL, iframe-resizer, ONLYOFFICE and WebContainers on licence grounds.
- UI: assistant-ui on shadcn CLI v4 (Base UI default, Radix via `radix-ui` still supported) and Tailwind v4.3; user-selectable accent colours via OKLCH CSS variables generated into 12-step light/dark scales with the open Radix palette algorithm (seed at step 9, APCA advisory), gated by WCAG 2 contrast. LibreChat (MIT) and Hugging Face chat-ui (Apache-2.0) are the licence-clean whole-app references; Open WebUI (branding clause) and LobeHub (community licence) are not.
- Shell: the embedded, agent-controllable browser requirement effectively forces a Chromium-based shell today. Electron 44 (WebContentsView + `webContents.debugger`/CDP, Playwright `connectOverCDP`) is the mature option; Electrobun's optional CEF and Tauri 3's alpha CEF runtime are the watch-list. If a native Swift shell is preferred for App Intents, Keychain, Accessibility and Foundation Models, pair it with a separate Chromium/Playwright process for browsing, exactly as OpenClaw pairs a Swift menu-bar app with a Node gateway. Either way, plan a Swift layer (native app, or node-swift/napi-rs addons plus a helper target).
- Architecture: one long-running Node/Bun gateway that serves the SPA and a typed WebSocket/SSE API (OpenClaw's TypeBox -> JSON Schema -> Swift pipeline is a concrete recipe), loopback-only by default, device pairing with signed challenges, Tailscale/SSH for remote access, passkeys only behind HTTPS, and the desktop shell as a thin supervised client (launchd). The same gateway runs unchanged on the Linux server.
- Feed and proactivity: ship a bounded daily brief (5-10 cards) assembled from an editable text "interest ledger" plus derived embeddings, sourced from RSS/HN/arXiv/Jetstream/saved items with paid search only for gaps, ranked by embedding recall x recency decay then an LLM reranker over a shuffled shortlist, with one or two bandit exploration slots, mandatory citations and Particle-style multi-perspective clusters, and Discover-style feedback controls written straight back to the ledger. Treat goal extraction and skill ideation as nightly jobs that emit candidates for one-tap confirmation (AWM/ASI-style mining of repeated tool sequences into SKILL.md drafts, verified by replay), delivered in scheduled windows rather than real-time interrupts, and gated by an acceptance-probability threshold learned from thumbs/accept-reject logs.

### Gaps
- The per-turn web-search budget shared by all researchers was exhausted early; coverage therefore leans on direct fetches of known primary URLs, and a few items (OpenAI help/blog pages, The Verge, Reddit API terms, Apryse licensing, OpenAI canvas isolation) returned 403/404 and are flagged unverified in their sections.
- npm's HTML pages returned 403, so versions were read from `registry.npmjs.org` JSON and GitHub's API; shipped (gzip) bundle sizes were not measured for any editor or viewer.
- Some vendor-reported numbers (Hermes Agent star counts, ClawHub skill counts, model benchmark scores from vendor cards) come from single sources and should be treated as approximate.
- Several facts in the Models section (OpenAI model release dates, Gemini context windows for 3.8/3.6 Flash) and the UI section (ChatGPT macOS, Cursor and LM Studio shell stacks, Tauri CEF Apple Silicon support, Apple OS minimums for Foundation Models/App Intents) could not be confirmed from primary pages.
- No dedicated literature pass was possible on conversational goal mining from dialogue logs; ProactiveBench/ProAgentBench cover task prediction from activity streams rather than goal extraction from chat.

Research date: 2026-10-18. All facts below come from primary sources fetched on that date; years on GitHub release pages are omitted by GitHub and were taken from asset timestamps or page footers. Web search quota was exhausted mid-task, so everything after the first two searches was gathered by fetching official docs/repos directly.

## Models — 1. Vercel AI SDK

### Takeaway
The AI SDK is now on **v7** (`ai@7.0.x`, released June 25, 2026; v6 is still maintained as `6.0.x`), Apache-2.0, ESM-only on Node 22+, and ships a first-class `ToolLoopAgent` (default `stopWhen: isStepCount(20)`), `toolApproval` (human-in-the-loop), `@ai-sdk/mcp` client, and a normalized `usage` object with cache-read/cache-write/reasoning sub-counts. It is the most complete TypeScript abstraction for a multi-provider agent, but it still lacks built-in retry/fallback across providers (the single most-upvoted open issue) and Ollama is community-maintained.

### Cited Findings
- Current versions: `ai@7.0.143` (Oct 16) and `ai@6.0.309` (Oct 16) are the two maintained lines; `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible` is at 3.0.71 (v7 line) / 2.0.85 (v6 line) — [Source](https://github.com/vercel/ai/releases)
- AI SDK 7 release date June 25, 2026; "over 16 million weekly downloads"; headline features `ToolLoopAgent`, experimental `HarnessAgent` (runs Claude Code / Codex / Pi harnesses behind the same `Agent` interface), `toolApproval`, typed `runtimeContext`, `@ai-sdk/otel`, `@ai-sdk/tui` (`runAgentTUI`) — [Source](https://vercel.com/blog/ai-sdk-7)
- AI SDK 6 shipped first as a beta (Oct 2025) adding the agent abstraction and tool-execution approval — [Source](https://infoq.com/news/2025/10/vercel-ship-ai); v6 docs still carry an "AI SDK 6 Beta" label — [Source](https://v6.ai-sdk.dev/docs/foundations)
- v7 breaking changes: Node 22+ required, all packages ESM-only; `stepCountIs` → `isStepCount`; `system` → `instructions`; `experimental_prepareStep/activeTools/output` lose the prefix; `onFinish` → `onEnd`, `onStepFinish` → `onStepEnd`; `fullStream` → `stream`; `needsApproval` deprecated in favor of `toolApproval`; `usage.cachedInputTokens`/`usage.reasoningTokens` removed in favor of `inputTokenDetails.cacheReadTokens` / `outputTokenDetails.reasoningTokens`; `result.usage` now sums all steps (`totalUsage` deprecated); Anthropic `providerMetadata.anthropic.cacheCreationInputTokens` removed (use `usage.inputTokenDetails.cacheWriteTokens`); codemod `npx @ai-sdk/codemod v7` — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/migration-guides/migration-guide-7-0)
- License: Apache License 2.0 ("Copyright 2023 Vercel, Inc.") — [Source](https://github.com/vercel/ai/blob/main/LICENSE); repo 27.4k stars, "The AI Toolkit for TypeScript", Node.js 22+ — [Source](https://github.com/vercel/ai)
- Provider packages (official): `@ai-sdk/openai`, `@ai-sdk/anthropic`, `@ai-sdk/google`, `@ai-sdk/google-vertex`, `@ai-sdk/azure`, `@ai-sdk/amazon-bedrock`, `@ai-sdk/mistral`, `@ai-sdk/deepseek`, `@ai-sdk/groq`, `@ai-sdk/xai`, `@ai-sdk/cerebras`, `@ai-sdk/togetherai`, `@ai-sdk/fireworks`, `@ai-sdk/deepinfra`, `@ai-sdk/perplexity`, `@ai-sdk/cohere`, `@ai-sdk/baseten` + audio providers; community: `ollama-ai-provider`, `@openrouter/ai-sdk-provider`, `ai-sdk-provider-claude-code`, `ai-sdk-provider-codex-cli`, `ai-sdk-provider-gemini-cli`, `zhipu-ai-provider`, etc.; the capability table lists `gpt-6-astra/luna/sol`, `gpt-5.6`, `claude-opus-4-8`, `gemini-3.8-flash`, `deepseek-v4-flash/pro`, `gemma-4-31b` (Cerebras), `gpt-oss-120b` — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/foundations/providers-and-models)
- `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible`: `createOpenAICompatible({ name, baseURL, apiKey, headers, includeUsage, supportsStructuredOutputs, supportsMultiPartToolContent, metadataExtractor, transformRequestBody })`; exposes `chatModel`, `completionModel`, `embeddingModel`, `imageModel`; LM Studio has a dedicated page; tool streaming and reasoning extraction are listed as supported — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/providers/openai-compatible-providers)
- Tool API: `tool({ description, inputSchema, execute, strict, contextSchema, inputExamples, onInputStart/onInputDelta/onInputAvailable, toModelOutput })`; `dynamicTool()` for runtime-unknown schemas with `toolCall.dynamic` flag; `repairToolCall` (not `experimental_`) with `NoSuchToolError`/`InvalidToolInputError`; `stopWhen` built-ins `isStepCount(n)`, `hasToolCall(...)`, `isLoopFinished()`; `prepareStep` can override `model`, `toolChoice`, `activeTools`, `messages`, `instructions`, `providerOptions`; provider-executed tools ignore `toolApproval`; `inputExamples` are used natively only by Anthropic — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/ai-sdk-core/tools-and-tool-calling)
- Agent classes: `ToolLoopAgent` (model + tools loop; `generate()`/`stream()`), `HarnessAgent`; `runtimeContext` (shared) and `toolsContext` (per-tool, typed by `contextSchema`) — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/agents/overview); `ToolLoopAgent` options include `instructions`, `tools`, `activeTools`, `toolOrder`, `toolChoice`, `toolCallConcurrency`, `stopWhen` (default `isStepCount(20)`), `prepareStep`, `toolApproval` (callback or per-tool `'approved' | 'denied' | 'user-approval'`), `output`, `providerOptions`, `maxRetries` (default 2), lifecycle `onStart/onStepStart/onToolExecutionStart/onToolExecutionEnd/onStepEnd/onEnd` — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/reference/ai-sdk-core/tool-loop-agent)
- Loop control: `ToolLoopAgent` warns if the default 20-step limit cuts a loop; `pruneMessages` (e.g. `reasoning: 'all'`, `toolCalls: 'before-last-3-messages'`) and `prepareStep`-returned `messages` are the documented history-compaction hooks; "forced tool calling" pattern uses `toolChoice: 'required'` plus a `done` tool without `execute` — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/agents/loop-control)
- Usage shape (v7): `usage: { inputTokens, inputTokenDetails: { noCacheTokens, cacheReadTokens, cacheWriteTokens }, outputTokens, outputTokenDetails: { textTokens, reasoningTokens }, totalTokens, raw }`; `providerMetadata` moved to `finalStep` — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/reference/ai-sdk-core/generate-text)
- UI Message Stream protocol: SSE `data:` JSON parts with header `x-vercel-ai-ui-message-stream: v1`; part types `start`, `start-step`/`finish-step`/`reset-step`, `text-start/delta/end`, `reasoning-start/delta/end`, `reasoning-file`, `source-url`/`source-document`, `file`, `tool-input-start` (with `inputEncoding` json|text), `tool-input-delta`, `tool-input-available`, `tool-approval-request`/`tool-approval-response`, `tool-output-available`, `tool-output-denied`, `custom` (`{provider}.{type}`), `data-*` custom data parts, `error`, `abort`, `finish`, `[DONE]`; text-stream protocol is the plain alternative — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/ai-sdk-ui/stream-protocol)
- Anthropic provider specifics: `providerOptions.anthropic.thinking: { type: 'adaptive' }` (Sonnet/Opus 4.6+), `effort: low|medium|high|xhigh|max`, `display: 'summarized'|'updates'`, `cacheControl: { type: 'ephemeral', ttl: '1h' }`; provider-defined tools `anthropic.tools.webSearch_20260318`, `webFetch_20260318`, `codeExecution_20260120`, `computerToolset_20260801`, `textEditor_20250728`, `bash_20250124`, `memory_20250818`, `toolSearchBm25_20251119`/`toolSearchRegex_20251119`, `advisor_20260301`; `contextManagement.edits` supports `clear_tool_uses_20250919`, `clear_thinking_20251015`, `compact_20260112`; separate `compaction` option for on-demand summarization; `providerMetadata.anthropic` carries `contextManagement.appliedEdits`, `stopDetails`, `iterations` — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/providers/ai-sdk-providers/anthropic)
- MCP client: `createMCPClient` from `@ai-sdk/mcp`; transports `http` (Streamable HTTP, recommended), `sse`, `stdio` (local only), custom; OAuth via `authProvider` with dynamic client registration; `tools()` with schema discovery or explicit `schemas`; `listResources`/`readResource`, `experimental_listPrompts`, `complete`, elicitation via `onElicitationRequest`; v7 changed MCP `redirect` default from `'follow'` to `'error'`; the lightweight client does not do session persistence, resumable streams or notifications — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/ai-sdk-core/mcp-tools), [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/migration-guides/migration-guide-7-0)
- Known pain points (top open issues by reactions): #2636 "retry strategies and fallbacks" (no built-in provider fallback), #7180 convert model messages to UI messages, #1896 programmatic access to model capabilities/provider listings, #9743 `prepareStep` should allow updating the tools sent to the LLM, #4842 custom retry callback / backoff, #5418 Gemini logprobs, #6723 OpenAI Responses `background` — [Source](https://github.com/vercel/ai/issues?q=is%3Aissue+state%3Aopen+sort%3Areactions-%2B1-desc)

### Inferences
- For a self-hosted TS agent, v7 is the sensible target: v6 is on the same cadence but v7 is where `toolApproval`, `runtimeContext`, and the normalized cache/reasoning usage fields live. Pin a 7.0.x and run the codemod when migrating v5/v6 code.
- The "no fallback/retry across providers" gap means the app must own a model-router layer (OpenClaw and Goose both do this themselves; see §6). The `prepareStep` `model` override is the natural seam to implement per-step fallback.
- Ollama via `ollama-ai-provider` is community code; using `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible` against Ollama's `/v1` (or LM Studio/llama.cpp) keeps you on an official package at the cost of Ollama-native features (`think`, `/api/show` capabilities).
- Apple Silicon / Linux: pure TypeScript, runs anywhere Node 22+ runs (both).

### Gaps
- npmjs.com returned 403, so weekly-download and exact `@ai-sdk/anthropic`/`@ai-sdk/openai` v7-line versions were not verified from npm (only `ai` and `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible` versions from the GitHub releases page).
- The reaction counts on the issues page may be comment counts (page did not label them); the ranking order is by reactions but the numbers are unverified.

## Models — 2. LiteLLM and OpenRouter

### Takeaway
LiteLLM (v1.106.0, Oct 18, 2026; MIT core with a separately licensed `enterprise/` directory; Python ≥3.10) remains the broadest Python translation layer and a self-hostable proxy with spend logs, but it is Python-only and its enterprise features (SSO beyond 5 users, audit logs, SCIM, spend reports) need a `LITELLM_LICENSE` key. OpenRouter is an OpenAI-Chat-shaped hosted router over "400+" models with always-on usage/cost accounting, provider routing preferences, ZDR filtering, and BYOK at a 5% fee.

### Cited Findings
LiteLLM
- Latest: v1.106.0 (Oct 18, "Latest"), v1.107.0-rc.1 (Oct 18, pre-release); release notes mention Responses API spend-log rows per WebSocket turn, stdio MCP servers disabled by default (breaking), per-model budget windows, `agent_access_default_deny` — [Source](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/releases)
- PyPI: version 1.106.0 released Oct 18, 2026; license MIT (SPDX); `Requires-Python <3.15, >=3.10` — [Source](https://pypi.org/project/litellm/)
- LICENSE header: "Portions of this software are licensed as follows… All content that resides under the 'enterprise/' directory… is licensed under the license defined in 'enterprise/LICENSE'… Content outside… is available under the MIT license" — [Source](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/blob/main/LICENSE); README: "Features under the LiteLLM Commercial License", 61.5k stars, "100+ LLM providers", Python SDK vs AI Gateway (Proxy Server) — [Source](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm)
- Enterprise vs OSS: OSS gateway/SDK include virtual keys, budgets, rate limits, spend tracking, Admin UI, load balancing, MCP gateway, 100+ providers; Enterprise (license key `LITELLM_LICENSE`, 30-day trial) adds SSO (free up to 5 users), SCIM, JWT auth, RBAC, audit logs, spend report generation, tag/per-model budgets, secret-manager integrations, guardrail integrations — [Source](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/enterprise)
- Cost tracking: automatic for models in `model_prices_and_context_window.json`; every response carries `x-litellm-response-cost`; `LiteLLM_SpendLogs` table (hashed key, user, team, tags, model group, spend, token counts); `/spend/logs` (capped 10k rows) and `/spend/logs/v2` (paged, 1,000 rows/page); `/global/spend/report` is Enterprise; zero-cost requests increment `litellm_zero_cost_requests_total` — [Source](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/proxy/cost_tracking)
- SDK helpers: `response._hidden_params["response_cost"]` on all calls; `completion_cost()`, `cost_per_token()`, `token_counter()` (model tokenizer, tiktoken fallback), `get_max_tokens()`, `litellm.model_cost` dict from the community-maintained JSON, `register_model()`, `LITELLM_LOCAL_MODEL_COST_MAP=True` for offline pricing — [Source](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/completion/token_usage)
- Usage normalization: "OpenAI compatible usage object across all providers" (`prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens`, `total_tokens`); streaming usage via `stream_options={"include_usage": True}` final chunk; proxy `always_include_stream_usage`; endpoint bridging (e.g. `/chat/completions` → `/responses`) driven by the model's `mode` in the price map — [Source](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/completion/usage)

OpenRouter
- API shape: `POST /api/v1/chat/completions` ("very similar to the OpenAI Chat API"), `GET /api/v1/generation?id=`; OpenRouter-specific request fields `models[]`, `route: 'fallback'`, `provider` (ProviderPreferences), `plugins`; `finish_reason` normalized to `tool_calls|stop|length|content_filter|error` with raw value in `native_finish_reason`; unsupported params are ignored; usage includes `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`, `prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens`, `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens`, `cost`, `cost_details.upstream_inference_cost`, `is_byok`, `server_tool_use.web_search_requests`; token counts use the model's native tokenizer — [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/api-reference/overview)
- Usage accounting is now always included ("`usage: {include: true}`… deprecated and have no effect"); in streaming it arrives in the final SSE chunk; `upstream_inference_cost` is populated only for BYOK — [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/use-cases/usage-accounting)
- `GET /api/v1/generation` returns `native_tokens_prompt`, `native_tokens_completion`, `native_tokens_reasoning`, `native_tokens_cached`, `total_cost`, `upstream_inference_cost`, `cache_discount`, `is_byok`, `latency`, `generation_time`, `finish_reason`/`native_finish_reason`, `streamed`, `cancelled` — [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/api-reference/get-a-generation)
- Provider routing: `provider.{order, allow_fallbacks, require_parameters, data_collection: allow|deny, zdr, enforce_distillable_text, only, ignore, quantizations, sort: price|throughput|latency (or {by, partition}), preferred_min_throughput, preferred_max_latency, max_price}`; `:nitro` (throughput + priority tier) and `:floor` (price + flex tier) suffixes; default load balancing weights stable providers by inverse square of price; BYOK endpoints are prioritized automatically unless `order` is set — [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/features/provider-routing)
- BYOK: "5% of what the same model/provider would cost normally on OpenRouter", deducted from credits; pay-as-you-go includes a $25,000/month list-price allowance; BYOK spend excluded from budgets unless "Include BYOK spend" is on — [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/use-cases/byok)
- Data policy: account-level toggle to block providers that may train (separate for paid/free models); per-provider retention shown as "Zero retention"/"Retained for N days"; per-request restriction via provider preferences — [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/features/privacy-and-logging)
- Models endpoint: "400+ models and providers"; `pricing.{prompt, completion, request, image, web_search, internal_reasoning, input_cache_read, input_cache_write, overrides}`, `context_length`, `supported_parameters` (e.g. `tools`, `structured_outputs`), `top_provider.{context_length, max_completion_tokens, is_moderated}`; `:free` is a catalog variant, `:nitro` a routing variant — [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/overview/models)

### Inferences
- LiteLLM is the right choice only if the app is Python; for a TypeScript app it is at most a sidecar proxy (adds a hop, a Postgres for spend logs, and operational surface). Its real asset for any stack is `model_prices_and_context_window.json` (see §5).
- OpenRouter is the cheapest way to get a very wide model catalog plus normalized cost accounting without maintaining price tables, but it is a hosted dependency; BYOK keeps provider billing direct at a 5% toll and preserves OpenRouter's normalized `usage`.
- Both run on Apple Silicon and Linux (LiteLLM is pure Python; OpenRouter is remote).

### Gaps
- Could not fetch `enterprise/LICENSE` (404 at the expected path), so the exact enterprise license text is unverified beyond the README's "LiteLLM Commercial License".
- OpenRouter's Responses-API-compatible endpoint: three candidate doc URLs returned 404; existence/status unverified.
- OpenRouter exact model count beyond "400+" not verified (the live `/api/v1/models` JSON was not fetched).

## Models — 3. Direct provider APIs

### Takeaway
As of Oct 2026 the frontier lineups are: Anthropic Claude Fable 5.1 / Opus 5.5 / Sonnet 5.5 / Haiku 5.5 (all 1M context, 128K output, adaptive thinking always-on, dateless pinned IDs); OpenAI GPT-6 Astra / GPT-6.1 Sol / GPT-6 Luna (1.05M context) on the Responses API; Google Gemini 4 Argon (GA Oct 13, 2026) and Gemini 3.8 Flash on the Interactions API (generateContent is "legacy" but supported). Memory tool (`memory_20250818`), context editing, server-side compaction (two betas), strict structured outputs, and the free `count_tokens` endpoint are all available on Claude; the agent-relevant difference is that Claude keeps tool state in content blocks with signed thinking, OpenAI in typed output items with `previous_response_id`/Conversations, and Gemini in Interaction steps with `previous_interaction_id`.

### Cited Findings
Anthropic (Claude Messages API)
- Current lineup and IDs: `claude-fable-5-1` ($10/$50 per MTok), `claude-opus-5-5` ($4/$20), `claude-sonnet-5-5` ($2/$10), `claude-haiku-5-5` (from $0.10/$0.50); all 1M context, 128K max output, adaptive thinking ("always on" for Fable 5.1/Opus 5.5), default effort `high` (Fable/Sonnet) or `medium` (Opus 5.5/Haiku 5.5); "Every Claude model ID is a pinned snapshot, including the dateless IDs used from the 4.6 generation on"; extended thinking (`type: enabled` + `budget_tokens`) "deprecated on Claude Opus 4.6 and Claude Sonnet 4.6 and not accepted on later models"; the tokenizer introduced with Opus 4.7 fits ~555k words per 1M tokens vs ~750k before; legacy still available: Fable 5, Opus 5, Opus 4.8/4.7/4.6/4.5, Sonnet 5, Sonnet 4.6, Haiku 4.5; Models API returns `capabilities` (`thinking.types.disabled`, `server_tools`, `code_execution`) and a `line` field — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/overview)
- Older names in the question (Sonnet 4.5 / Haiku 4.5 / Opus 4.1): Opus 4.1 and Sonnet 4 are "retired, except on Bedrock and Google Cloud"; Haiku 4.5 and Sonnet 4.5 still appear in tool-pricing tables — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/overview)
- Thinking: `thinking: {type: "adaptive", display: "summarized"|"omitted"|"updates"(beta header `thinking-display-updates-2026-08-18`)}`; `display` defaults to `"omitted"` on 5.x models (empty `thinking` text, signature still returned); Sonnet 5.5 rejects `disabled` and offers `{type: "between_tools"}`; Haiku 5.5 and Opus 5 accept `disabled` only at effort ≤ high; thinking blocks carry a `signature` that must be passed back unchanged ("Modified thinking blocks are rejected with a 400 error"); interleaved thinking is automatic with adaptive thinking; "progress updates" between tool calls arrive as separate signed `thinking` blocks on Fable 5.1/Opus 5.5/Sonnet 5.5; streaming emits `thinking_delta` then one `signature_delta` per block — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/thinking)
- Effort: `output_config.effort` ∈ `low|medium|high|xhigh|max`, GA, no beta header; per-message effort via empty `role: "system"` message with `output_config` needs beta `mid-conversation-output-config-2026-07-01` and preserves the prompt cache; changing top-level effort between requests invalidates the cache — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/effort)
- Tool use: client tools return `stop_reason: "tool_use"` with `tool_use` blocks; results go back as `tool_result` blocks in a `user` turn; `tool_choice: {type: "auto", disable_parallel_tool_use: true}`; server tools (`web_search`, `web_fetch`, `code_execution`, `tool_search`, advisor, MCP connector) run on Anthropic infra; tool-use system prompt costs 286 tokens on Opus 5.5/Sonnet 5.5/Haiku 5.5 — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/overview)
- Tool reference (type strings / beta headers): `web_search_20260318|20260209|20250305` (none), `web_fetch_20260318|…` (none), `code_execution_20260521|20260120|20250825` (none), `advisor_20260301` (`advisor-tool-2026-03-01`), `tool_search_tool_regex_20251119` / `tool_search_tool_bm25_20251119` (none), `mcp_toolset` (`mcp-client-2025-11-20`), `memory_20250818` (none), `bash_20250124` (none), `text_editor_20250728|20250124` (none), `computer_toolset_20260801` (none) / `computer_20251124` (`computer-use-2025-11-24`) / `computer_20250124` (`computer-use-2025-01-24`), `browser_toolset_20260801` (none); optional tool properties `cache_control`, `strict`, `defer_loading` (cache-preserving), `allowed_callers` (`direct` / `code_execution_20260120`), `input_examples`, `eager_input_streaming` — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/tool-reference)
- Computer use: `computer_toolset_20260801` is GA on the Claude API (no beta header), a client toolset of 17 member tools (`screenshot`, `left_click`, `type`, `key`, `zoom`…) each returned as `tool_use` with `toolset_name: "computer"`; `computer_20251124`/`computer_20250124` return an error on 5.5+ models on the Claude API; quick start pairs it with `text_editor_20250728` and `bash_20250124` — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/computer-use-tool)
- Memory tool: `{"type": "memory_20250818", "name": "memory"}`, "available on all Claude 4 and later models", **no beta header required**; client-side commands `view`, `create`, `str_replace`, `insert`, `delete`, `rename` under `/memories`; API auto-injects a memory protocol system prompt; SDK helpers `BetaLocalFilesystemMemoryTool` (Python/TS), `betaMemoryTool` (TS), `BetaAbstractMemoryTool` (Python/C#); pairs with context editing and with server-side compaction; path-traversal protection is the implementer's responsibility — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/memory-tool)
- Context editing (beta header `context-management-2025-06-27`): `clear_tool_uses_20250919` (`trigger` default 100,000 input tokens, `keep` 3 tool uses, `clear_at_least`, `exclude_tools`, `clear_tool_inputs`) and `clear_thinking_20251015` (`keep: {type: "thinking_turns", value: N}` or `"all"`); response reports `context_management.applied_edits` (also in final `message_delta`); `count_tokens` accepts `context_management` and returns `original_input_tokens`; client-side SDK `compaction_control` is deprecated (removed in Python v1.0) in favor of server-side compaction — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/context-editing)
- Compaction (server-side summary): two betas. On demand: header `compact-2026-09-04`, request `"compaction": {"type": "summarize", "instructions"?}`, returns a single signed `compaction` block with `stop_reason: "compaction"`, which must be placed first in `messages` on later requests; billed via `usage.iterations[{type: "compaction", …}]`; cannot be combined with `context_management`; SDK tool runners expose `compact_before_next_turn()` — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/compaction-on-demand). Threshold: edit `compact_20260112` under `context_management.edits` with header `compact-2026-01-12`, `trigger` default 150,000 input tokens (min 50,000), `pause_after_compaction`, `instructions`; the API drops content before the returned block; usage again in `usage.iterations` — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/compaction-threshold); Anthropic recommends on-demand "wherever it is available" — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/compaction)
- Prompt caching: `cache_control: {type: "ephemeral"}` (5 min, write 1.25x) or `{…, "ttl": "1h"}` (write 2x), no beta header mentioned; reads 0.1x (0.025x on Fable 5.1, 0.05x on Opus 5.5/Sonnet 5.5); usage fields `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation.ephemeral_5m_input_tokens` / `ephemeral_1h_input_tokens`; min cacheable 512 tokens on 5.5-generation models (4,096 on Opus 4.6/4.5/Haiku 4.5); up to 4 breakpoints, 20-block lookback — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching)
- Structured outputs: GA; `output_config.format: {type: "json_schema", schema}` and strict tool use `strict: true`; old `output_format` needs beta `structured-outputs-2025-11-13`; limits: no recursive schemas, `additionalProperties: false` required, 20 strict tools/request; grammar compile cached 24h — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/structured-outputs)
- Token counting: `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens`, GA, "free to use" with separate RPM limits (5,000/10,000/20,000 by tier), accepts system/tools/images/PDFs (base64 only) and thinking blocks; rejects server tools and MCP connector; Claude 4.7+ tokenizer yields "approximately 30 percent more tokens" — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/token-counting)
- Streaming: `message_start` (usage input tokens) → `content_block_start`/`content_block_delta` (`text_delta`, `input_json_delta` with `partial_json`, `thinking_delta`, `signature_delta`) /`content_block_stop` → `message_delta` (stop_reason + *cumulative* usage) → `message_stop`; `ping` and `error` (e.g. `overloaded_error`) events may appear; tool input is streamed one complete key/value at a time — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/streaming)

OpenAI (Responses API)
- Current flagship models: `gpt-6-astra` (1.05M ctx, 128K out, $10/$50, effort low→max), `gpt-6.1-sol` (1.05M, $2/$10), `gpt-6-luna` (1.05M, $0.10/$0.50, effort none→max); specialized GPT-5.6 Cyber, GPT-Image-2.5, GPT-Realtime-2.1 — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models); GPT-5.1 launched Nov 12, 2025 and has since been superseded (GPT-5.2 Dec 11, 2025 per the τ³ leaderboard dates below) — [Source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GPT-5.1)
- Function tools: `{type: "function", name, description, parameters, strict}`; `function_call` output items (`call_id`, `arguments`) answered with `function_call_output` items; reasoning items returned alongside tool calls must be passed back; `namespace` tool grouping; `tool_search` deferred tools (gpt-5.4+); streaming via `response.output_item.added` → `response.function_call_arguments.delta` → `…done` → `response.output_item.done`; `custom` tools with Lark/regex grammars (LLGuidance); examples use `gpt-6-astra`, GPT-6.1 Sol "requires the Responses API for tool calling" — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/function-calling)
- Built-in tools: `web_search`, `file_search`, `tool_search`, `mcp` (remote, `server_label`/`server_url`/`require_approval`), shell, image generation, computer use, skills, programmatic tool calling — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/tools); create-request `tools[].type` enum includes `function, file_search, computer, computer_use_preview, web_search, web_search_preview, mcp, code_interpreter, image_generation, local_shell, shell, custom, namespace, tool_search, apply_patch, programmatic_tool_calling`; `include` supports `reasoning.encrypted_content`, `message.output_text.logprobs`, etc.; `context_management: [{type: "compaction", compact_threshold}]`; `background` — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/api-reference/responses/create)
- Conversation state: `previous_response_id` chains turns (all chained input tokens still billed); Conversations API object passed as `conversation`; `store` defaults to 30-day retention, `store: false` disables; reasoning items are encrypted by default and `reasoning.context: "all_turns"` carries reasoning across turns; server-side compaction and a standalone `/responses/compact` endpoint exist — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/conversation-state), [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/compaction)
- Reasoning: `reasoning.effort` ∈ `none|minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh|max` (model-dependent; Astra rejects `none`), `reasoning.summary` `auto|concise|detailed`, separate `reasoning.mode` `standard|pro` on GPT-5.6/6; stateless mode returns `encrypted_content` on reasoning items by default; reasoning tokens billed as output and reported in `usage.output_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/reasoning)
- Prompt caching: automatic; min 1,024 tokens on GPT-5.6+; `usage.input_tokens_details.cached_tokens` and (GPT-5.6+) `cache_write_tokens`; reads 0.1x (0.05x on GPT-6.1 Sol), writes 1.25x on GPT-5.6+; `prompt_cache_key`; `prompt_cache_options.ttl: "30m"` on GPT-5.6+ (older `prompt_cache_retention: "in_memory"|"24h"`) — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-caching)

Google (Gemini API)
- Models: `gemini-4-argon` (flagship, Stable, "unified 1M context"), `gemini-3.8-flash`, `gemini-3.6-flash`, `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`, `gemini-3.1-pro-preview`, `gemini-3-flash-preview`; Gemini 2.5 Pro/Flash/Flash-Lite access "limited to users who have used these models before" — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/models); dates: Gemini 4 Argon GA Oct 13, 2026; 3.8 Flash GA Sep 2, 2026; 3.6 Flash + 3.5 Flash-Lite GA Jul 21, 2026; 3.1 Pro Preview Feb 19, 2026; 3 Flash Preview Dec 17, 2025; Interactions API launched Dec 11, 2025; 3 Pro Preview Nov 18, 2025; no generateContent deprecation listed — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/changelog)
- Pricing (paid, per 1M): Argon $2/$10 through Nov 30, 2026 then $4/$20; 3.8 Flash and 3.6 Flash $0.75/$3.75 through Dec 31, 2026 then $1.50/$7.50; 3.5 Flash-Lite $0.30/$2.50; cached input ≈ one-tenth of input; Argon not on the free tier — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing)
- Interactions API: `POST …/v1beta/interactions`; generateContent is "legacy" but "fully supported"; `store=true` default (55-day paid / 1-day free log retention), `previous_interaction_id` for server-side history (tools/system/generation_config must be re-sent each turn); steps model (`function_call`, `function_result`, thoughts, `model_output`); SDKs `google-genai` / `@google/genai` ≥ 2.3.0; limitations: no remote MCP on Gemini 3, no Batch API, no explicit caching — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/interactions)
- Function calling (Interactions): tools `{type: "function", name, description, parameters}`; `function_call` steps with `name`, `arguments`, `id` answered by `function_result` steps with `call_id`; `tool_choice` in `generation_config`: `auto|any|none|validated` plus `allowed_tools`; parallel and compositional calls; stateless history must include thought steps unchanged; remote `mcp_server` tools (Streamable HTTP only); streaming tool calls arrive as `step.start`/`step.delta`; recommended 10–20 active tools — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/function-calling)
- Thinking: `thinking_level` (`minimal|low|medium|high`, model-dependent) and `thinking_summaries`; defaults: Argon/3.8/3.6 Flash medium, 3.5 Flash-Lite minimal, 3.1 Pro Preview high; every thought step carries an encrypted `signature` that must be resent unchanged in stateless mode; thinking tokens in `usage.total_thought_tokens` and billed as output — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/thinking)
- Caching: implicit caching on by default for 2.5+ (min 4,096 tokens on Argon/3.8/3.6/3.1 Pro, 2,048 on 2.5); explicit caching "not supported in the Interactions API, or for Gemini 4 Argon and onward"; hits visible in `usage.total_cached_tokens` — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/caching)
- Token counting: `POST /v1beta/models/{model}:countTokens` counts input only (text, images, video, audio, tools); Interactions usage fields `total_input_tokens`, `total_output_tokens`, `total_thought_tokens`, `total_cached_tokens`, `total_tool_use_tokens`, `total_tokens` — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/tokens); generateContent `usageMetadata` has `promptTokenCount`, `cachedContentTokenCount`, `candidatesTokenCount`, `toolUsePromptTokenCount`, `thoughtsTokenCount`, `totalTokenCount` — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/api/generate-content)

### Inferences
- The three vendors have converged on "signed/encrypted reasoning that must be round-tripped verbatim" (Claude `signature`, OpenAI `encrypted_content`, Gemini thought `signature`). An abstraction that drops or rewrites assistant history will break tool loops on all three; store provider-native assistant turns opaquely.
- Claude's `memory_20250818` + on-demand compaction (`compact-2026-09-04`) is the most complete server-assisted long-session story for a personal agent; OpenAI's `context_management: compaction` and Gemini's `previous_interaction_id` cover the same need differently (OpenAI/Gemini keep state server-side by default, which matters for a privacy-focused self-hosted app: set `store: false`).
- Price-wise the mid tiers are now near-identical ($2/$10 Sonnet 5.5, $2/$10 GPT-6.1 Sol, $2/$10 Argon promo), so routing can be capability-driven rather than cost-driven at that tier.

### Gaps
- OpenAI's Responses create reference is 506K characters; only the first 100K were read, so `text.verbosity`, `max_tool_calls`, `service_tier`, `truncation` details were not re-verified from the reference (they were covered only indirectly).
- Gemini generateContent `Part` fields (`functionCall`, `functionResponse`, `thoughtSignature`) and `toolConfig.functionCallingConfig` enum were not confirmed from the REST reference (page truncated); the thought-signatures page redirected to the Interactions-centric thinking guide.
- Exact context windows for Gemini 3.8/3.6 Flash were not shown on the models index page.
- GPT-5.x → GPT-6 release dates were not fetched from an official OpenAI changelog (search quota exhausted); the models page shows no dates.

## Models — 4. Local inference on Apple Silicon and Linux

### Takeaway
All four local runtimes expose OpenAI-compatible tool calling today: Ollama v0.40.6 (MIT; MLX runner on Apple Silicon since mid-2026, llama.cpp/GGUF elsewhere; `/v1/chat/completions`, non-stateful `/v1/responses`, and an Anthropic-compatible `/v1/messages`), LM Studio 0.4.x/"Bionic" (proprietary, "personal and/or internal business" use allowed; OpenAI + Anthropic + Responses endpoints; MLX and llama.cpp engines; `lms`/`llmster` headless), llama.cpp b11723 (MIT; `--jinja` default, `--reasoning-format`, GBNF/json_schema, router mode), mlx-lm 0.32.2 (Apple-only; server parses tool calls and splits reasoning but is "not recommended for production"), vLLM 0.32.0 (Apache-2.0; Linux GPU; ~30 tool-call parsers; Apple Silicon only as experimental CPU build). The models that are realistically usable for tool-calling agents on a 32–64 GB Mac are Qwen3.5 9B/27B/35B-A3B, Qwen3.8-27B, Gemma 4 12B/26B-A4B/31B, gpt-oss-20b, GLM-4.7-Flash (30B-A3B), Mistral Small 4 (119B-A6.5B needs ~64 GB+), Nemotron 3.5 Lightning (30B-A3B) and Muse Glimmer 30B; Llama 4 (Apr 2025) is stale and Kimi K2.5/K3, DeepSeek V4.x and GLM-5.x are datacenter-scale.

### Cited Findings
Runtimes
- Ollama releases: v0.40.6 (Latest, Oct 16), v0.40.7 pre-release (Oct 18: reasoning effort on Responses requests, MLX model additions), v0.40.5 (Oct 14: Nemotron 3.5 Super, fixes to Responses conversations with custom tool calls), v0.40.4 (Oct 12: "Decision models are supported by the MLX runner on Apple silicon", speculative decoding fixes, multimodal embeddings via llama-server) — [Source](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/releases); license MIT — [Source](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/blob/main/LICENSE)
- Ollama MLX/engine history: "Ollama's highest performance on Apple Silicon yet with MLX" (Jun 11, 2026), "Faster Gemma 4 on MLX with multi-token prediction" in Ollama 0.31 (Jun 29, 2026), "Improved performance and model support with GGUF" via llama.cpp in Ollama 0.30 (Jun 5, 2026), Claude Desktop support via the Anthropic-compatible API (Aug 25, 2026), Nemotron 3.5 Lightning (Aug 11, 2026), Muse Glimmer "accelerated by the MLX engine" (Aug 10, 2026), per-token pricing for cloud plans (Aug 31, 2026) — [Source](https://ollama.com/blog)
- Ollama OpenAI compatibility: `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/completions`, `/v1/embeddings`, `/v1/models`, `/v1/responses` ("added in Ollama v0.13.3… Only the non-stateful flavor is supported"); tools supported but `tool_choice` is not; `reasoning_effort`/`reasoning.effort` and Ollama `think` field; vision via base64 only; `stream_options.include_usage` supported — [Source](https://docs.ollama.com/api/openai-compatibility)
- Ollama Anthropic compatibility: `/v1/messages` with messages, streaming, system prompts, tools + tool results, thinking, `output_config.effort`, base64 images; unsupported: `tool_choice`, `metadata`, `count_tokens`, `cache_control`, Batches, citations, PDF documents, streaming `error` events; Claude Code works with `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://localhost:11434` — [Source](https://docs.ollama.com/api/anthropic-compatibility)
- Ollama native tool calling: `tools` on `/api/chat`, `message.tool_calls`, `role: "tool"` with `tool_name`; streaming tool calls across chunks; parallel calls — [Source](https://docs.ollama.com/capabilities/tool-calling); the library lists 256 models tagged `tools` — [Source](https://ollama.com/search?c=tools)
- LM Studio developer surface: OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions, Completions, Responses, Embeddings, Models, Structured Output, Tool Use; an Anthropic-compatible Messages endpoint; LM Studio REST API (`/api/v1/chat` on port 1234, stateful chats); tool calling + MCP via API; `llmster` headless daemon for servers/CI ("not dependent on the LM Studio GUI"); `lms daemon up`, `lms get`, `lms server start --port 1234`, `lms chat`; SDKs `@lmstudio/sdk` and `lmstudio` (Python) — [Source](https://lmstudio.ai/docs/developer)
- LM Studio terms (Version: August 23, 2026): license is "solely for Your personal and / or internal business purposes"; employer is the Customer for work use; no sublicensing/SaaS redistribution; paid features billed separately — [Source](https://lmstudio.ai/terms)
- LM Studio timeline: 0.4.0 (Jan 28, 2026: server deployment, parallel requests with continuous batching, REST API endpoint); 0.3.39 (Jan 15, 2026: Open Responses); Anthropic-compatible API usable from Claude Code (Jan 30, 2026); Responses API first in 0.3.29 (Oct 6, 2025); mlx-engine v1.8.5 with KV-cache checkpointing (Jun 5, 2026); "Introducing LM Studio Bionic" (Jul 16, 2026); Splash Engine for Apple Silicon (Sep 18, 2026) — [Source](https://lmstudio.ai/blog); Bionic is "LM Studio's agent for open models… Powered by the LM Studio runtime, with MLX and llama.cpp under the hood" — [Source](https://lmstudio.ai/), [Source](https://lmstudio.ai/bionic); Splash is a third-party (Inco AI) open-source Apple Silicon engine installed as an experimental runtime, requiring "LM Studio Bionic 1.1.5 or newer", M3+, macOS 26.4+, ≥36 GB; 74 tok/s on Qwen3.8-27B on a 48 GB M5 Pro — [Source](https://lmstudio.ai/blog/splash-engine)
- llama.cpp: latest tags b11723/b11722 (Oct 19), b11721 (Oct 18) — [Source](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/releases); MIT — [Source](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/LICENSE); llama-server: `--jinja` "enabled by default", `--reasoning-format none|deepseek|deepseek-legacy` (default `auto`) routes thoughts to `message.reasoning_content`, `reasoning_effort` request field, `response_format` `json_object`/`json_schema`, `--grammar`/`--grammar-file` GBNF, `parallel_tool_calls` on some models, built-in tools via `--tools`/`--agent` and `--mcp-servers-config`, router/multi-model mode via `--models-dir`/`--models-preset` (`--models-max` 4), feature list mentions Responses and Anthropic Messages-compatible routes — [Source](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/tools/server/README.md); native tool-call formats: Llama 3.x, Functionary v3, Hermes 2/3, Qwen 2.5, Mistral Nemo, Firefunction v2, Command R7B, DeepSeek R1 (WIP), GPT-OSS (Harmony); generic handler otherwise ("may consume more tokens"); "Extreme KV quantization (e.g. `-ctk q4_0`) can substantially degrade tool calling" — [Source](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/docs/function-calling.md)
- mlx-lm: v0.32.2 (Oct 16), v0.32.0 (Oct 15: tool call parser for MiniCPM5, fixes to Qwen3 Coder argument parsing, Qwen 3.5 parallel head, Muse-Spark-Open-816B); Gemma 4 + Gemma 4 tool-call parser in v0.31.2 (Apr 7); gpt-oss sharding v0.30.4 (Jan 19) — [Source](https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/releases); `mlx_lm.server` source reads `tools`, warns `if tools and not tokenizer.has_tool_calling`, parses tool blocks with `tokenizer.tool_parser` into `choice["tool_calls"]` with `finish_reason = "tool_calls"`, and returns reasoning separately as `choice["reasoning"]`; routes `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/completions`, `/v1/models`, `/health` — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/main/mlx_lm/server.py); SERVER.md: "The MLX LM server is not recommended for production as it only implements basic security checks"; `--kv-bits` forces one-request-at-a-time — [Source](https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/blob/main/mlx_lm/SERVER.md)
- vLLM: v0.32.0 (Oct 18; drops Python 3.10; CORS no longer `*`), v0.31.0 (Oct 5; DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash perf), v0.30.0 (Sep 22; DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash and GLM-5.3-Flash support); CUDA 13 default images, ROCm, XPU, CPU wheels x86_64/arm64/macOS — [Source](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/releases); Apache-2.0 — [Source](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/blob/main/LICENSE); tool calling: `--enable-auto-tool-choice --tool-call-parser <name>`, `--tool-strict-level`, parsers `hermes, mistral, llama3_json, llama4_pythonic, pythonic, granite, granite4, internlm, jamba, xlam, deepseek_v3, deepseek_v31, openai (gpt-oss), kimi_k2, hunyuan_a13b, cohere_command3/command4, longcat, glm45, glm47, functiongemma, qwen3_xml, mimo, step3p5, olmo3, gigachat3, apertus, beam, hf`; `tool_choice` named/auto/none/required — [Source](https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/features/tool_calling.html); Apple Silicon: "experimental support for macOS with Apple Silicon", CPU backend only, build from source, FP32/FP16; no Metal backend (points to community `vllm-metal` using MLX); Linux CPU x86 (AVX512) and ARM AArch64 (NEON); Python 3.11–3.14 — [Source](https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/getting_started/installation/cpu.html)

Models (existence, license, size, Apple Silicon footprint)
- Qwen3.5 (Mar–Apr 2026): `Qwen3.5-4B`, `-9B`, `-27B`, `-35B-A3B`, `-122B-A10B`, `-397B-A17B` (plus FP8/GPTQ-Int4 variants) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/models?search=Qwen3.5); Ollama downloads: 0.8b 1.2–1.3 GB, 2b ~3 GB, 4b 3.3–4 GB, 9b 6.6–7.6 GB, 27b 17–20 GB, 35b 22 GB, 122b 81 GB, all 256K context, tagged vision/tools/thinking, `-mlx` tags available — [Source](https://ollama.com/library/qwen3.5)
- Qwen3.8 (Aug 2026): `Qwen3.8-27B` (dense, Apache-2.0, 262,144 ctx native / 1M with YaRN, hybrid Gated DeltaNet + Gated Attention; Terminal-Bench 2.1 73.0, SWE-bench Pro 61.7) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B); `Qwen3.8-Flash-Next` (MoE 125B total / 6B active, 512 experts, license `qwen-community-1.0`, Toolathlon Verified 73.5) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next); `Qwen3.8-2.4T-A95B` flagship (Aug 12) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/Qwen); Ollama `qwen3.8:27b` 18 GB, 256K, vision/tools/thinking, `27b-mlx` — [Source](https://ollama.com/library/qwen3.8); Qwen3-Next was not visible in current listings (see Gaps)
- Gemma 4 exists: sizes E2B, E4B, 12B, 26B-A4B (MoE, ~3.8B active), 31B dense; `gemma-4-31b-it` is Apache-2.0 (license tag), 30.7B params, 256K context, "Native support for structured tool use", Tau2 avg 76.9% — [Source](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-4-31b-it); Gemma docs: "built-in function-calling support", 128K (small) / 256K (medium) — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemma/docs/core); Ollama sizes e2b 4.6–7.5 GB, e4b 6.6–9.5 GB, 12b 7.7–8 GB, 26b 16–19 GB, 31b 19–20 GB, `-mlx` tags — [Source](https://ollama.com/library/gemma4)
- Gemma 3 / 3n: still listed as earlier generations — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemma/docs/core); BFCL (Dec 2025) scores Gemma-3-27b-it 29.47%, 12b 30.43%, 4b 19.62% (prompt mode) — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/HuanzhiMao/BFCL-Result/main/2025-12-16/score/data_overall.csv)
- Llama 4: Scout 109B/17B-active (16E) and Maverick 402B/17B-active (128E), last updated May 22, 2025, no Llama 4.x/5 listed — [Source](https://huggingface.co/meta-llama); Scout released April 5, 2025, 10M context, "Llama 4 Community License Agreement" (700M-MAU clause, "Built with Llama" attribution) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Llama-4-Scout-17B-16E-Instruct)
- gpt-oss: Apache 2.0; 120b = 117B total / 5.1B active ("single 80GB GPU"), 20b = 21B / 3.6B active ("within 16GB"); MXFP4 MoE weights; native function calling/browsing/python/structured outputs; must use the harmony format; reasoning low/medium/high; paper Aug 2025 — [Source](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-120b); Ollama 20b 14 GB, 120b 65 GB, 128K ctx, tools+thinking — [Source](https://ollama.com/library/gpt-oss)
- DeepSeek: V4-Flash 291B and V4-Pro 1.6T (Jun 22, 2026), V4-Flash-0731 304B, V4-Pro-0813 1.7T, V4.1-Flash 763B (Oct 2026) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai); V4.1-Flash: MIT, 552B backbone, 8B active (prefill)/16B (decode), 1M context, Terminal-Bench 2.1 90.6, DeepSWE v1.1 74.2 — [Source](https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash); V3.2-Exp scored 56.73%/54.12% on BFCL (Dec 2025) — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/HuanzhiMao/BFCL-Result/main/2025-12-16/score/data_overall.csv)
- Mistral: Mistral Small 4 (v26.03, Apache 2.0, "Hybrid model unifying instruct, reasoning, and coding"), Mistral Medium 3.5 (Modified MIT), Mistral Large 3 (v25.12, Apache 2.0), Mistral Large 4 (v26.10), Ministral 3 14B/8B/3B (Apache 2.0); Devstral 2 / Devstral Small 2 and Magistral Small/Medium 1.2 are deprecated (retire 3/31/2026 and 7/31/2026); Mistral Small 3.x deprecated — [Source](https://docs.mistral.ai/getting-started/models/models_overview/); `Mistral-Small-4-119B-2603`: Apache 2.0, 119B total / 6.5B active (128 experts, 4 active), 256k context, "native function calling and JSON output", `reasoning_effort` none|high, vLLM recommended, llama.cpp via GGUF — [Source](https://huggingface.co/mistralai/Mistral-Small-4-119B-2603)
- GLM: GLM-5 754B (Aug), GLM-5.2 753B (Sep), GLM-5.3 753B and GLM-5.3-Flash 321B (Oct) — [Source](https://huggingface.co/zai-org); GLM-5.3-Flash: MIT, 320B total / 18B active, `reasoning_effort` low|high|max, vLLM/SGLang — [Source](https://huggingface.co/zai-org/GLM-5.3-Flash); GLM-4.7-Flash: MIT, 30B-A3B, vLLM `glm47` parser, τ²-Bench 79.5, SWE-bench Verified 59.2 — [Source](https://huggingface.co/zai-org/GLM-4.7-Flash); GLM-4.6 ranked #4 on BFCL at 72.38% (FC thinking) — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/HuanzhiMao/BFCL-Result/main/2025-12-16/score/data_overall.csv)
- Kimi: Kimi-K3 2.8T (Sep 2026), K2.7-Code 1T, K2.6 1T, K2.5 1T — [Source](https://huggingface.co/moonshotai); Kimi-K2.5: Modified MIT, 1T / 32B active, native INT4, 256K, interleaved thinking + multi-step tool calls, SWE-bench Verified 76.8 — [Source](https://huggingface.co/moonshotai/Kimi-K2.5); Kimi-K2-Instruct 59.06% BFCL — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/HuanzhiMao/BFCL-Result/main/2025-12-16/score/data_overall.csv)
- Other 2026 local-agent models: NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Lightning "open 30B mixture-of-experts (MoE) model with 3B active parameters", 1M context, tools+thinking, 19–25 GB — [Source](https://ollama.com/library/nemotron-3.5-lightning); Muse Glimmer (Meta) 30B, Apache 2.0, "built for always-on local agents", vision/tools/thinking, 18 GB, 128K — [Source](https://ollama.com/library/muse-glimmer)
