- Benchmarks: BFCL is at V4 ("holistic agentic evaluation"), last updated 2026-04-12, eval package `bfcl-eval==2025.12.17` — [Source](https://gorilla.cs.berkeley.edu/leaderboard.html); the Dec 16, 2025 overall table: #1 Claude-Opus-4-5 77.47%, #2 Claude-Sonnet-4-5 73.24%, #3 Gemini-3-Pro-Preview 72.51%, #4 GLM-4.6 72.38%, #11 Kimi-K2-Instruct 59.06%, #14 DeepSeek-V3.2-Exp 56.73%, #23 Qwen3-235B-A22B-2507 52.15%, Qwen3-32B 48.71%, Qwen3-30B-A3B-2507 41.39%, Qwen3-8B 42.57%, Llama-4-Maverick 37.29%, Llama-4-Scout 28.13%, Mistral-small-2506 37.15%; gpt-oss not listed — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/HuanzhiMao/BFCL-Result/main/2025-12-16/score/data_overall.csv); τ²-bench repo is MIT, v1.0.1 grading update July 2026, domains retail/airline/telecom (+ mock, banking_knowledge) — [Source](https://github.com/sierra-research/tau2-bench); τ²-bench text leaderboard top 3 (Pass^1): Qwen3.5-397B-A17B 87.9%, Gemini 3.0 Pro 85.4%, Claude Opus 4.5 85.3% — [Source](https://taubench.com); τ³-Banking (Pass^1): Qwen 3.8 Max 55.2%, Claude Opus 5 48.7%, Grok 4.5 47.9%, GPT-5.6-sol 46.9%, Muse Spark 1.1 40.5%, Kimi K3 37.1%, GLM-5.2 37.1% — [Source](http://taubench.com/leaderboard/)

### Inferences
- RAM at 4-bit on Apple Silicon (inferred from Ollama download sizes plus ~20–30% headroom for KV cache/activations): 16 GB → Qwen3.5-9B / Gemma 4 E4B–12B / gpt-oss-20b (14 GB file, tight); 32 GB → Qwen3.5-27B or Qwen3.8-27B (17–20 GB), Gemma 4 31B (19–20 GB), Gemma 4 26B-A4B, Nemotron 3.5 Lightning, Muse Glimmer; 48–64 GB → Qwen3.5-35B-A3B comfortably, long contexts on 27–31B; 96–128 GB → gpt-oss-120b (65 GB), Qwen3.5-122B-A10B (81 GB), Mistral Small 4 (119B at ~4-bit ≈ 60–70 GB, not measured). DeepSeek V4.x, GLM-5.x, Kimi K2.5/K3 are out of reach locally.
- Among small models, the MoE "3B-active" class (Qwen3.5-35B-A3B, GLM-4.7-Flash 30B-A3B, Nemotron 3.5 Lightning, Gemma 4 26B-A4B) gives the best tokens/sec for agent loops on a Mac; dense 27–31B gives better quality per GB. GLM-4.7-Flash's τ² 79.5 and Gemma 4 31B's τ² 76.9 are the only vendor-reported τ² numbers for ≤32B-class models found; treat them as self-reported.
- BFCL's public CSV lags (Dec 2025) and omits Qwen3.5/3.8, Gemma 4 and gpt-oss; use it only to confirm that function-calling quality drops steeply below ~8B.
- Ollama is the lowest-friction single runtime that covers both Apple Silicon (MLX) and Linux (llama.cpp/CUDA/ROCm) with one API, and it uniquely offers an Anthropic-compatible endpoint; LM Studio is Apple/desktop-first and its daemon mode is fine for a personal Linux box, but its EULA (not open source) should be noted; vLLM is the Linux GPU server choice.

### Gaps
- Qwen3-Next (80B-A3B, 2025) and Qwen3.6 did not appear in the Qwen org or search listings fetched; existence as separate current releases unverified (Splash blog references `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B`, so a Qwen3.6 line exists but was not verified on HF).
- Gemma 3n status and Gemma 4 exact release date (tech report dated Jul 2, 2026; Ollama/mlx-lm support from April 2026) not pinned to an announcement.
- Licenses for Qwen3.5 (per-model), GLM-5.x, Kimi K3, DeepSeek V4 Flash/Pro, and Nemotron 3.5 Lightning were not visible on the pages fetched.
- Current LM Studio version number was not displayed anywhere fetched (only "latest", 0.4.0 from Jan 2026, and "Bionic 1.1.5" as a minimum); whether "Bionic" replaces the 0.4.x line is unclear.
- gpt-oss newer versions: none found; harmony-format requirement persists.
- BFCL V4 live table is JS-rendered; only the Dec 2025 CSV was readable.

## Models — 5. Normalizing tool calls, streams and token accounting across providers

### Takeaway
A provider-neutral core needs five normalizations: (1) message history as provider-native opaque assistant turns (Claude content blocks with signed `thinking`/`tool_use`, OpenAI Responses output items incl. encrypted `reasoning`, Gemini steps with signed thoughts) plus a lossy canonical view for the UI; (2) a stream event vocabulary like the AI SDK's `text-/reasoning-/tool-input-*` parts; (3) a usage record with `input`, `cacheRead`, `cacheWrite`, `output`, `reasoning` (all three vendors now expose each, under different names); (4) a price table (LiteLLM's JSON or OpenRouter `/models`) keyed by provider/model with cache-read/cache-write/reasoning rates; (5) token counting via provider endpoints (Anthropic and Gemini are free/cheap; OpenAI has only tiktoken offline).

### Cited Findings
- Claude: tool calls are `tool_use` content blocks (`id`, `name`, `input`) in the assistant turn, results are `tool_result` blocks (`tool_use_id`, `content`, `is_error`) in the next user turn — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/overview); `thinking` blocks carry `signature` and "the sequence of consecutive `thinking` blocks must match what the model generated… you can't rearrange, edit, or partially drop them" — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/thinking); streaming deltas `text_delta`, `input_json_delta` (`partial_json`), `thinking_delta`, `signature_delta`; usage in `message_start` and cumulative in `message_delta` — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/streaming)
- OpenAI Responses: `function_call` items (`call_id`, `name`, `arguments`) and `function_call_output` items; "reasoning items returned with tool calls must be passed back"; arguments stream via `response.function_call_arguments.delta/done`; Chat Completions remains the `tool_calls`-array shape and defaults to non-strict — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/function-calling); reasoning items carry `encrypted_content` in stateless mode — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/reasoning)
- Gemini: `function_call` steps (`name`, `arguments`, `id`) answered by `function_result` steps (`call_id`, `result[]`, may include images); thought steps have encrypted `signature`s that "should NOT" be removed or modified; streaming via `step.start`/`step.delta` — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/function-calling), [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/thinking)
- Usage field names: Anthropic `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` (+ `cache_creation.ephemeral_5m/1h_input_tokens`), compaction `usage.iterations[]` — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching), [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/compaction-on-demand); OpenAI `usage.input_tokens_details.cached_tokens`, `cache_write_tokens` (GPT-5.6+), `usage.output_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` — [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-caching), [Source](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/reasoning); Gemini `total_input_tokens`, `total_output_tokens`, `total_thought_tokens`, `total_cached_tokens`, `total_tool_use_tokens` (Interactions) / `promptTokenCount`, `cachedContentTokenCount`, `candidatesTokenCount`, `thoughtsTokenCount`, `toolUsePromptTokenCount` (generateContent) — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/tokens), [Source](https://ai.google.dev/api/generate-content); OpenRouter `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`/`cache_write_tokens`, `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens`, `cost`, `cost_details` — [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/api-reference/overview)
- Already-normalized shapes: AI SDK v7 `usage.inputTokenDetails.{noCacheTokens, cacheReadTokens, cacheWriteTokens}`, `outputTokenDetails.{textTokens, reasoningTokens}`, `raw` — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/reference/ai-sdk-core/generate-text); AI SDK UI stream parts (`tool-input-start/delta/available`, `reasoning-*`, `data-*`) — [Source](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/ai-sdk-ui/stream-protocol); LiteLLM "OpenAI compatible usage object across all providers" with `response_cost` in `_hidden_params` — [Source](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/completion/usage), [Source](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/completion/token_usage); OpenClaw's `@openclaw/ai` exposes `toolcall_delta` events with cumulative `partialJson` and keeps only parsed `arguments` on completed calls — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openclaw/openclaw/main/packages/ai/README.md)
- Cost tables: LiteLLM `model_prices_and_context_window.json` ("community-maintained") drives `litellm.model_cost` with `max_tokens`, `input_cost_per_token`, `output_cost_per_token`; `LITELLM_LOCAL_MODEL_COST_MAP` for offline — [Source](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/completion/token_usage); OpenRouter `/api/v1/models` `pricing` includes `input_cache_read`, `input_cache_write`, `internal_reasoning`, `web_search`, `overrides` — [Source](https://openrouter.ai/docs/overview/models); Goose fetches model metadata live from models.dev with a bundled fallback and applies long-context price tiers to cost estimates — [Source](https://github.com/block/goose/releases)
- Tokenizers/counting: Anthropic `count_tokens` is free and counts under the tokenizer of the `model` passed (4.7+ ≈ +30%) — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/token-counting); Gemini `countTokens` REST/SDK counts input only — [Source](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/tokens); tiktoken is "a fast BPE tokeniser for use with OpenAI's models", MIT, encodings `o200k_base`/`cl100k_base` — [Source](https://github.com/openai/tiktoken); LiteLLM `token_counter()` uses model tokenizers (Anthropic, Cohere, Llama2, OpenAI) and "falls back to tiktoken" — [Source](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/completion/token_usage); Claude's `clear_at_least` exists specifically to decide whether clearing is worth a prompt-cache invalidation — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/context-editing)

### Inferences
- Canonical cost record: `{provider, model, inputTokens, cacheReadTokens, cacheWriteTokens(5m|1h), outputTokens, reasoningTokens, serverToolUnits, iterations[]}`; compute cost = Σ(tokens × rate) with per-provider cache multipliers (Anthropic 1.25x/2x write, 0.1x read; OpenAI 1.25x write, 0.1x/0.05x read; Gemini ~0.1x read + storage) and treat OpenRouter `usage.cost` as authoritative when routed through it.
- Store the raw provider usage object (`raw`) alongside the normalized one; both AI SDK and LiteLLM already do this and it is the only way to reconcile billing later (e.g. Anthropic compaction iterations or OpenAI chained `previous_response_id` re-billing).
- For local models, "cost" should be zero but token counts still matter for context management; llama.cpp/mlx-lm/Ollama return OpenAI-style `usage` so the same record works.

### Gaps
- LiteLLM's documentation of `prompt_tokens_details`/`completion_tokens_details` and Anthropic cache fields in its normalized response was not found on the pages fetched (the usage page lists only the three basic fields).
- tiktoken's latest version and whether it ships an encoding for GPT-6 models were not verified.

## Models — 6. How existing open-source personal agents abstract providers

### Takeaway
OpenClaw (MIT, Node 24/26, 392k stars, release 2026.10.5) owns its provider layer (`@openclaw/ai` workspace package built on vendor SDKs `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `openai`, `@google/genai`) with `provider/model` refs, `agents.defaults.model.{primary,fallbacks}`, auth-profile rotation with cooldowns, and turn-local model failover; Goose (Apache-2.0, Rust, v1.55.0) has a per-provider Rust module tree with live models.dev pricing, declarative custom providers and cost estimation; Open Interpreter is now a Rust rewrite "based on Codex" (0.0.56) while the LiteLLM-based Python package stalled at 0.4.3 (Oct 2024). Among UI shells, Cherry Studio's `@cherrystudio/ai-core` is built on the Vercel AI SDK, LibreChat is MIT with Responses-API support, Jan bundles llama.cpp + MLX, Open WebUI and LobeChat carry source-available-style license riders.

### Cited Findings
- OpenClaw: MIT; Node 24.16+ or 26.1+; macOS/Linux/Windows/WSL2 (+ iOS/Android apps); 392k stars; "Any OS. Any Platform."; credits Mario Zechner for "pi" — [Source](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw); latest release 2026.10.5 (Oct 18, 2026) adds Gemini 4 Argon as default Google model, llama.cpp router model limits/tools, managed Ollama/LM Studio embedding readiness; 2026.10.10-beta.1 adds per-run model fallback overrides, context-overflow recovery → fallbacks, custom model aliases, prioritized private vLLM — [Source](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/releases); `package.json` version 2026.10.5, `engines.node ">=24.16.0 <25 || >=26.1.0"`, deps `@anthropic-ai/sdk 0.133.0`, `openai 7.31.0`, `@google/genai 2.28.0`, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk 1.32.1` (+ `@modelcontextprotocol/client|core|server 2.3.1`), `@agentclientprotocol/sdk 1.7.0`, `@openclaw/ai workspace:*`; no `ai`/`@ai-sdk/*`/`@mariozechner/pi-ai` dependency — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openclaw/openclaw/main/package.json); `@openclaw/ai` = "Reusable model API contracts, provider adapters, and streaming primitives from OpenClaw"; provider ids, credentials, catalogs, retries and failover stay in the host app; subpaths `anthropic`, `google-model-family`, `openai-completions-compat`, `openai-responses-payload-policy`, `event-stream`; bundled Mistral adapter (Apache-2.0) — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openclaw/openclaw/main/packages/ai/README.md)
- OpenClaw providers: hosted OpenAI, Anthropic, OpenRouter, Google Gemini/Vertex, Z.AI, Vercel AI Gateway, Kilo, MiniMax, NVIDIA, xAI, OpenCode, Moonshot/Kimi Coding, Volcano/BytePlus, Synthetic; local llama.cpp, LM Studio, Ollama, vLLM, SGLang, LiteLLM proxies; `models.providers` for custom base URLs; refs `provider/model` — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/model-providers); model selection: `agents.defaults.model.primary` + `fallbacks` in order, `agents.defaults.models` aliases, `modelPolicy.allow`, `utilityModel`/`decisionModel`/`imageModel`/`pdfModel`, per-agent overrides, `/model <ref> -s|-a|-g`; custom providers written to `~/.openclaw/agents/<agentId>/agent/models.json`; `models.mode merge|replace`; per-model `input`, reasoning, context window, token limits, `cost` block — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/models); failover = (1) auth-profile rotation within provider, (2) next entry in `agents.defaults.model.fallbacks`; profiles store API keys/OAuth in per-agent SQLite with `auth.profiles`/`auth.order` metadata; OAuth tried before API keys, then LRU; cooldowns 30s → 1m → 5m cap, billing failures disable 10 min; failover errors: auth, rate limit, overload, timeout, billing, `model_not_found`, unrecoverable context overflow; "Fallback execution is turn-local"; user `/model` picks are strict (no silent fallback) — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/model-failover); `models.providers.*.localService` starts local model servers on demand after a health check; hosted catalog refreshed from `catalog.openclaw.ai/models/v2/catalog.json` every six hours — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/config-runtime)
- Goose: "a general-purpose AI agent that runs on your machine", Apache-2.0, built in Rust, desktop app + CLI + API for macOS/Linux/Windows, "15+" providers incl. Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, Ollama, OpenRouter, Azure, Bedrock, plus Claude/ChatGPT/Gemini subscriptions via ACP, 70+ MCP extensions, 55.4k stars — [Source](https://github.com/block/goose); providers module files: `base.rs`, `provider_registry.rs`, `anthropic_def.rs`, `openai_def.rs`, `google_def.rs`, `ollama_def.rs`, `ollama_cloud.rs`, `openrouter_def.rs`, `bedrock.rs`, `azure.rs`, `gcpvertexai.rs`, `databricks_def.rs`, `litellm.rs`, `local_inference.rs`, `githubcopilot.rs`, `claude_code.rs`/`claude_acp.rs`, `codex.rs`/`codex_acp.rs`, `gemini_cli.rs`, `custom_provider_config.rs`, `toolshim.rs`, `canonical_cost.rs`, `usage_estimator.rs`, `formats/`, `inventory/` — [Source](https://github.com/block/goose/tree/main/crates/goose/src/providers); releases v1.55.0 (Oct 16: declarative AI gateway provider, OpenAI Responses preferred with live reasoning metadata, adaptive thinking for Sonnet/Haiku 5.5, long-context price tiers in cost estimates, removed background subagents/orchestrator), v1.54.0 (Oct 8: cache-write tokens from Responses usage), v1.53.0 (Oct 2: model metadata live from models.dev with bundled fallback, Opus 5.5 / GPT-6 Sol/Luna, rmcp 3.4.1) — [Source](https://github.com/block/goose/releases)
- Open Interpreter: Apache-2.0, 68.6k stars, "This is the new Rust version of Open Interpreter, based on Codex"; OpenAI-compatible providers via `interpreter --chat-completions`; community fork of the Python project at endolith/open-interpreter — [Source](https://github.com/OpenInterpreter/open-interpreter); releases 0.0.56 (Oct 7, 2026: gpt-6.1-sol, claude-sonnet-5-5 metadata), 0.0.55, 0.0.54 (Sep 30: bundles Codex 0.156.1 client, ChatGPT OAuth, per-run `model_provider` config) — [Source](https://github.com/OpenInterpreter/open-interpreter/releases); Python package `open-interpreter` 0.4.3 uploaded 2024-10-26, `Requires-Python >=3.9,<4`, depends on `litellm<2.0.0,>=1.41.26` — [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/open-interpreter/0.4.3/json)
- Cherry Studio: Community Edition AGPL-3.0 with a separate commercial license (bd@cherry-ai.com), Enterprise Edition sold separately; Windows/Mac/Linux + mobile; local models via Ollama and LM Studio; 52.7k stars — [Source](https://github.com/CherryHQ/cherry-studio); latest v2.1.7 (Oct 19, 2026) — [Source](https://github.com/CherryHQ/cherry-studio/releases); `@cherrystudio/ai-core` is "a unified AI provider interface package built on the Vercel AI SDK" (requires `ai` + `@ai-sdk/*`), with a provider registry (OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, OpenAI-compatible, xAI, Azure, DeepSeek + `registerProvider`), plugin hooks (`onRequestStart`, `transformParams`, `transformResult`) and `streamText`/`generateObject` wrappers; sibling packages `ai-sdk-provider`, `provider-registry` — [Source](https://github.com/CherryHQ/cherry-studio/blob/main/packages/aiCore/README.md), [Source](https://github.com/CherryHQ/cherry-studio/tree/main/packages)
- Jan: Apache 2.0, 45.0k stars, bundled llama.cpp engine, remote providers (OpenAI, Anthropic, Mistral, Groq, MiniMax…), OpenAI-compatible local server at `localhost:1337`, MCP, macOS 13.6+/Windows/Linux — [Source](https://github.com/janhq/jan); v0.8.6 (Oct 9, 2026); v0.8.5 ships llama.cpp 0.5.0 (b11146) in-process worker with CUDA 13/Vulkan, conversation prompt caching on by default; v0.8.4 added an OpenAI-compatible gateway, Responses API support, keyring-stored keys and Gemini key rotation; v0.8.2 HIP/ROCm and MLX models in the local API server; v0.8.0 `/v1/orchestrations` server-side MCP tool execution — [Source](https://github.com/janhq/jan/releases)
- Open WebUI: 156.1k stars; Ollama + any OpenAI-compatible API (LM Studio, Groq, Mistral, OpenRouter, vLLM examples), native function calling, MCP/MCPO/OpenAPI tool servers; "Open WebUI License with an additional requirement to preserve the 'Open WebUI' branding" — [Source](https://github.com/open-webui/open-webui); LICENSE: BSD-style terms plus clause 4 forbidding removal of branding except deployments with ≤50 users in a rolling 30 days, written permission, or an enterprise license — [Source](https://github.com/open-webui/open-webui/blob/main/LICENSE); v0.12.3 (Oct 16): Anthropic-SDK `x-api-key` audit attribution, tool-search `call_tool`, compaction between tool rounds — [Source](https://github.com/open-webui/open-webui/releases)
- LibreChat: MIT, v0.8.8, 45.9k stars; providers Anthropic, Bedrock, OpenAI, Azure, Google, Vertex, OpenAI Responses API, Ollama, Mistral, Groq, OpenRouter, custom OpenAI-compatible endpoints via `librechat.yaml`; Agents with Skills/Subagents and MCP tools — [Source](https://github.com/danny-avila/LibreChat)
- LobeChat: 83.3k stars, "LobeHub Community License"; "over 10,000 tools and MCP-compatible plugins"; Docker self-host — [Source](https://github.com/lobehub/lobe-chat); the license is "based on Apache License 2.0" and permits unmodified commercial use "including as a frontend and backend service", but "a commercial license must be obtained from the producer if you want to develop and distribute a derivative work" — [Source](https://github.com/lobehub/lobe-chat/blob/main/LICENSE); v2.2.20 (Oct 18, 2026) adds Claude Haiku 5.5, lets Codex/Pi use any tool-capable model, keeps stdio MCP sessions alive — [Source](https://github.com/lobehub/lobe-chat/releases)

### Inferences
- The two most mature "personal agent" codebases (OpenClaw, Goose) both rejected a third-party abstraction and built thin per-provider adapters over vendor SDKs plus a model catalog with pricing (OpenClaw's hosted catalog v2; Goose via models.dev). The reusable patterns are: `provider/model` string refs, primary + ordered fallbacks, auth-profile rotation with cooldowns, strict (no-fallback) user-pinned selections, and per-model metadata (context window, max tokens, reasoning, input modalities, cost).
- Cherry Studio shows the opposite path (Vercel AI SDK underneath with a registry and plugin middleware) works for a desktop multi-provider client; for a TypeScript app, "AI SDK core + own router/catalog" is the pragmatic middle.
- LiteLLM-centric designs (old Open Interpreter) have been abandoned by their own project; LiteLLM is now mostly used as an optional *proxy target* (Goose `litellm.rs`, OpenClaw "local proxies such as LiteLLM").
- Licensing: OpenClaw (MIT), Goose/Jan/Open Interpreter (Apache-2.0), LibreChat (MIT) are clean to borrow from; Cherry Studio is AGPL-3.0, Open WebUI and LobeChat have non-OSI riders.

### Gaps
- Goose's provider and lead/worker documentation pages returned 404 at every path tried; lead/worker env vars and the custom-provider JSON format were not verified (only the Rust module names and release notes).
- OpenClaw's `models.providers.<id>.api` enum (e.g. `openai-completions`, `anthropic-messages`) and the exact per-model schema were not reachable (config pages moved; the docs recommend `openclaw config schema` / `config.schema.lookup`).
- Whether `@openclaw/ai` descends from `@mariozechner/pi-ai` is not stated on the README (the main README only thanks "pi").
- LibreChat's internal provider SDK (`@librechat/agents` / LangChain) and LobeChat's `model-runtime` package were not confirmed from source.
- Cherry Studio's current Vercel AI SDK major (v6 vs v7) is not stated in the aiCore README.

Research date: 2026-10-18. Method: WebFetch of primary sources (official docs, GitHub, PyPI, arXiv). The shared WebSearch budget ran out after the first four searches, so everything below is from direct fetches of known URLs; items that could not be fetched are listed under Gaps rather than filled from memory.

## Memory — 1. First-party memory designs (Claude, ChatGPT, Gemini, Copilot)

### Takeaway
Every first-party assistant has converged on the same two-layer shape: (a) explicit, user-visible memory items the user can edit/delete/pause, plus (b) implicit recall over past chats, with an incognito/temporary-chat escape hatch. Anthropic is the only vendor that also ships the mechanism as a developer primitive: a client-side, file-based `memory_20250818` tool whose storage you own, paired with server-side context editing and compaction.

### Cited Findings
**Claude.ai memory (consumer)**
- Current design stores memory as individual "Topics": "In Settings > Memory, open 'Topics,' select one, then use the edit icon or 'Delete.'" The legacy design kept a single memory summary edited via Settings > Capabilities > "View and edit memory" (Manage memory modal, pencil icon adds custom instructions). — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work)
- Current memory "reflects changes to your conversations as they happen"; the legacy synthesis was "updated every 24 hours." — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work)
- Each project has its own memory; inside a project Claude uses project memory plus non-project account memory, never other projects' memory; a per-project "Use account memory" toggle fully isolates it. — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work)
- Controls: Pause (keeps memory, stops using/creating), Reset ("permanently deletes all memories, including project memories... this cannot be undone"), per-chat memory off before the first message, Incognito chats (ghost icon; not saved to memory or history; all plans). — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work)
- Plans: past-chat search on Pro/Max/Team/Enterprise; memory on by default for Free/Pro/Max; Team/Enterprise owners enable and members opt in; not available to HIPAA, public-sector, or custom-retention orgs. Citations to past chats link back to the original conversation. — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work)
- Memory import/export is "experimental" (import from other assistants, export for backup/migration); a legacy memory export option remains until September 9, 2026. — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work)

**Claude Code: CLAUDE.md + auto memory**
- Two complementary systems, both loaded every session: CLAUDE.md (you write; instructions) and auto memory (Claude writes; "learnings and patterns"); auto memory loads "first 200 lines or 25KB" of its index. — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory)
- Storage: `~/.claude/projects/<project>/memory/` containing `MEMORY.md` (index, one line per memory) plus topic files such as `user_role.md`, `feedback_testing.md`; derived from the git repo so all worktrees share one directory; machine-local, never synced. Memory files carry a `type` frontmatter field (kinds include `user` and `feedback`) and Claude Code stamps a `modified` ISO-8601 frontmatter field on write (v2.1.214+). — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory)
- Over-limit handling: if `MEMORY.md` exceeds 200 lines/25KB the write succeeds but Claude Code returns an error telling Claude to rewrite the index "because everything past the limit is dropped on the next load." CLAUDE.md files load in full up to 4 MiB; guidance is to target under 200 lines. — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory)
- Controls: `/memory` toggle writes `autoMemoryEnabled` to `~/.claude/settings.json`; `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=1`; `autoMemoryDirectory` setting; memory files are excluded from the `cleanupPeriodDays` transcript sweep. Subagents may keep separate auto memory. — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory)

**Anthropic API memory tool, context editing, compaction (as of Oct 2026)**
- Tool spec is still `{"type": "memory_20250818", "name": "memory"}`; "available on all Claude 4 and later models"; the tool itself "doesn't require a beta header" (SDK helpers live in the beta namespace). Client-side: Claude only requests ops; your handler executes them under the `/memories` prefix. — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/memory-tool)
- Six commands: `view` (optional `view_range`), `create` (`file_text`), `str_replace` (`old_str`, optional `new_str`), `insert` (`insert_line`, `insert_text`), `delete`, `rename` (`old_path`, `new_path`), each with specified return strings and error strings. — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/memory-tool)
- The API auto-injects a system instruction: "IMPORTANT: ALWAYS VIEW YOUR MEMORY DIRECTORY BEFORE DOING ANYTHING ELSE... ASSUME INTERRUPTION: Your context window might be reset at any moment." — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/memory-tool)
- SDK helpers: `BetaAbstractMemoryTool` (Python, C#), `betaMemoryTool` (TypeScript), `BetaMemoryToolHandler` (Java), plus a ready-made `BetaLocalFilesystemMemoryTool` in Python/TS. Security section mandates path-traversal protection (`/memories/../../secrets.env`), size caps, expiration of stale files, and stripping sensitive data. — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/memory-tool)
- Context editing: beta header `context-management-2025-06-27`; strategies `clear_tool_uses_20250919` (defaults: trigger 100,000 input tokens, keep 3 tool uses; `clear_at_least`, `exclude_tools`, `clear_tool_inputs`) and `clear_thinking_20251015` (`keep` = N thinking turns or `"all"`); "available on all supported Claude models." SDK-side `compaction_control` is deprecated (removed in Python v1.0). — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/context-editing)
- Compaction (server-side summary): on-demand compaction uses beta header `compact-2026-09-04` and a top-level `compaction` parameter (keeps recent turns, can run in background; recommended). Threshold compaction uses header `compact-2026-01-12` with edit `{"type": "compact_20260112"}` in `context_management.edits`; default trigger 150,000 input tokens (min 50,000); `pause_after_compaction`; `instructions`; returns a `compaction` content block and `stop_reason: "compaction"`. — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/compaction), [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/compaction-threshold)
- Anthropic's guidance: "compaction keeps the active context small without client-side bookkeeping, and memory preserves the information that must survive summarization." — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/memory-tool)

**ChatGPT memory**
- Wikipedia (citing The Verge, Apr 11 2025, "ChatGPT will now remember your old conversations"): memory is an optional feature where users can have ChatGPT save specific information, and a separate option lets ChatGPT recall past conversations. — [Source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ChatGPT)

**Gemini**
- Aug 13 2025: Gemini app "can reference past conversations" and this is on by default; setting path Settings > "Personal context" > "Your past chats with Gemini"; initially 2.5 Pro, select countries, consumer accounts 18+. Temporary Chats are not used for personalization or training and are kept up to 72 hours. "Gemini Apps Activity" renamed "Keep Activity" (effective Sept 2 2025). — [Source](https://blog.google/products/gemini/temporary-chats-privacy-controls/)
- Google help overview: personalization draws on "Memory of your past Gemini chats," content from connected Google apps, and your instructions; "These features are not available to everyone at this time"; personal accounts only (no work/school/supervised). — [Source](https://support.google.com/gemini/answer/15637730)
- "Personal Intelligence" (Jan 2026, beta): opt-in connection of Gmail, Google Photos, YouTube and Search history; runs on Gemini 3 Pro/Flash; US-only for Google AI Pro/Ultra, personal accounts 18+; explicit facts live in "Saved Info" (gemini.google.com/saved-info); users can disconnect apps and delete individual memories; Photos excluded in Illinois/Texas. (Secondary source.) — [Source](https://www.datacamp.com/blog/gemini-personal-intelligence)

**Microsoft Copilot**
- Apr 4 2025 (Mustafa Suleyman): Copilot Memory remembers details such as favorite foods and a nephew's birthday; opt-in, reviewable in a user dashboard, user chooses which types of information are kept or opts out entirely. — [Source](https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2025/04/04/your-ai-companion/)

### Inferences
- The consumer pattern to copy for a personal agent: topic-level memory items with edit/delete, a pause switch, a hard reset, a per-chat incognito mode, and citations from recalled facts back to the source conversation (Claude does all five).
- Claude's move from a nightly-regenerated summary to real-time topic items (and Claude Code's `modified` timestamps) suggests Anthropic found provenance/recency metadata and immediate writes more useful than batch synthesis; a self-hosted design can keep a nightly consolidation pass but should write facts immediately.
- The `memory_20250818` tool is a good wire protocol to adopt internally even for non-Anthropic models: six commands, line-numbered views, and a `/memories` prefix map cleanly onto a markdown directory on disk.

### Gaps
- ChatGPT: every OpenAI primary source (help.openai.com Memory FAQ, openai.com "Memory and new controls" post, "memory-faq") returned HTTP 403 and The Verge is blocked for this agent; Wikipedia's body section on memory was not in the fetched slices. The specific UI names ("Saved memories", "Reference chat history", Settings > Personalization > Memory > "Manage memories", Temporary Chat exclusions, EEA/UK/CH rollout exclusions, Free-tier lightweight memory) are from prior knowledge and were NOT verified here.
- Gemini: the official Personal Intelligence announcement (blog.google) and Android Authority article returned 404/403; the January 2026 details above rest on DataCamp (secondary). The Google help subpages ("Get personalization based on your past Gemini chats", "Manage what Gemini remembers") were not fetched, so current defaults and region/age rules are unverified. A third-party guide surfaced by search claims memory requires 18+, is unavailable in EEA/UK/Switzerland/Nigeria, and needs both the Memory toggle and Keep Activity on; not verified.
- Claude Code auto memory: only two of the four `type` values (`user`, `feedback`) were visible in the fetched text.
- The full list of Microsoft Copilot memory controls (per-memory deletion, consumer vs M365 behaviour) was not researched beyond the launch post.

## Memory — 2. OpenClaw's markdown memory

### Takeaway
OpenClaw (MIT, 392k stars, release 2026.10.5 on 2026-10-18) is the most fully worked-out "plain markdown + SQLite" memory design available to copy: always-injected persona/user/rules files with hard character budgets, a curated `MEMORY.md`, daily `memory/YYYY-MM-DD.md` notes that are indexed but not injected, hybrid FTS5+vector search in a per-agent SQLite file (sqlite-vec optional, pure-cosine fallback), a pre-compaction "memory flush," and a nightly "dreaming" cron (light/REM/deep) that promotes only trusted, repeatedly-recalled candidates into `MEMORY.md`.

### Cited Findings
- Project: MIT license; 392.4k stars; "The AI that really does things. Any OS. Any Platform. The lobster way."; npm install requires Node 24.16+ or 26.1+ (26 recommended); installers for macOS, Linux, Windows; "State, memory, and credentials live on your hardware." — [Source](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw)
- Releases: `openclaw 2026.10.5` (18 Oct 2026, latest) adds memory diagnostics explaining skipped trigger recall / rejected embedding responses and reports readiness of managed Ollama and LM Studio embedding setups; `2026.10.10-beta.1` (15 Oct 2026) adds optional memory-embedding setup to local setup and non-interactive llama.cpp embedding-only setup. — [Source](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/releases)
- Workspace (default `~/.openclaw/workspace`): `AGENTS.md` (operating instructions and memory-use rules, every session), `SOUL.md` (persona, tone, boundaries, every session), `USER.md` (optional user model, every session), `IDENTITY.md` (name/vibe/emoji, set in bootstrap ritual), `BOOTSTRAP.md` (one-time first-run ritual), `BOOT.md` (optional startup checklist), `MEMORY.md` (curated long-term memory, "Loaded only in the main, private session"), `memory/YYYY-MM-DD.md` (daily logs), `skills/`. There is no standalone `TOOLS.md`; tool conventions live in an `AGENTS.md` `## Tools` section. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/agent-workspace)
- Budgets: `agents.defaults.bootstrapMaxChars` default 20000 per file; `agents.defaults.bootstrapTotalMaxChars` default 60000; `USER.md` has a separate 4,000-character cap; oversized files are truncated in the prompt (file on disk intact); missing optional files are omitted. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/agent-workspace)
- What goes where: `USER.md` = stable preferences/communication style/relationships/active-project context "written as directives with observed-date and active/superseded metadata"; `MEMORY.md` = "compact, curated layer of durable non-profile facts and standing decisions," not a log; `memory/YYYY-MM-DD.md` = detailed daily notes, "indexed for search but not injected into every turn"; today's and yesterday's notes load on a bare `/new` or `/reset`; slugged `memory/YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>.md` files are also picked up; imports from Codex/Claude Code/Hermes go to `memory/imports/<tool>/` and are not merged. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory)
- `USER.md` entry format: a metadata comment then one imperative directive, e.g. `<!-- observed: 2026-07-27 | status: active -->` / `- Prefer concise progress updates during implementation work.`; supersede in place (mark old entry `superseded`, keep it beside the replacement) because "append-only contradictory history can cause systems to follow an original preference the user has since changed." Multi-user gateways keep `users/<id>/USER.md`, editable by the agent only via the `personal_instructions` tool for the authenticated requester. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/user-model)
- `SOUL.md` holds voice: tone, opinions, brevity, humor, boundaries; "Short beats long. Sharp beats vague."; `AGENTS.md` holds operating rules. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/soul)
- Builtin search engine: per-agent SQLite at `~/.openclaw/agents/<agentId>/agent/openclaw-agent.sqlite` (also holds sessions/transcripts); keyword = FTS5 with BM25; vectors stored as little-endian float64 BLOBs; "sqlite-vec: optional acceleration for in-database vector queries"; "If sqlite-vec fails to load, OpenClaw falls back to in-process cosine similarity automatically"; hybrid results get MMR by default; chunks default 400 tokens with 80-token overlap; file changes trigger a debounced reindex (1.5 s). Indexed: `MEMORY.md`, root `USER.md`, `memory/*.md`, session transcripts (if `sessions` in `memory.search.sources`), `memory.search.extraPaths`. Default provider is OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small` when a key is set; local GGUF via `memory.search.local.modelPath`. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-builtin)
- Providers: `memory.search.provider` accepts `openai`, `gemini`, `voyage`, `mistral`, `bedrock`, `local`, `ollama`, `omlx`, `lmstudio`, `github-copilot`, `openai-compatible`, `deepinfra`, `none`; `local` installs a managed llama.cpp `llama-server` and downloads a ~0.3 GB GGUF via `@openclaw/llama-cpp-provider`; EmbeddingGemma task prefixes are applied automatically; `auto`/`local` fall back to keyword-only on failure, explicit remote providers do not. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-search)
- Ranking: final score = "hybrid relevance x recency decay x importance multiplier"; dated daily notes decay with a 30-day half-life while `MEMORY.md`/`USER.md` are evergreen; importance (1-10) is scored once at write time; MMR with lambda 0.7 over Jaccard token overlap; 200 candidates per leg (cap 400). Trigger recall injects up to three entries per turn whose trigger phrases score >= 0.65, and only from `MEMORY.md`/`USER.md`. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-search), [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-architecture)
- Memory tiers and provenance: bootstrap (human-written), curated core (`MEMORY.md`, `USER.md`, written by dreaming or direct user request), episodic (daily notes + transcripts, retrieved only), prospective (standing intents in SQLite + cron), review (`DREAMS.md`, never injected). Every index entry has an origin class `owner`, `agent`, `untrusted`, or `system` stored as SQLite columns; unknown provenance is never `owner`; "Taint from network-sourced tool results marks the rest of the turn as `untrusted`, until the next user message"; cron/heartbeat/sub-agent sessions produce no durable candidates; `openclaw memory forget` removes entries derived from chosen sessions. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-architecture)
- Memory flush before compaction: on by default (`agents.defaults.compaction.memoryFlush.enabled`), a silent turn asks the agent to save important context; during the flush `write` appends only to the daily note and replayed/copied text is rejected; can run on a different model (`agents.defaults.compaction.memoryFlush.model`, e.g. `ollama/qwen3:8b`). — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory)
- Dreaming: "the background memory consolidation system in `memory-core`," on by default, one cron job (default `dreaming.frequency` = `0 3 * * *`); three phases per sweep: light (stage recent material), REM (theme/reflection summaries), deep (score and promote; "the only phase that writes long-term memory, and it writes only to `MEMORY.md`"). Ranking weights: relevance 0.30, frequency 0.24, query diversity 0.15, recency 0.15, consolidation 0.10, conceptual richness 0.06; gates `minScore`, `minRecallCount`, `minUniqueQueries`; `phases.deep.maxPriorEntryLossFraction` 0.25; `phases.deep.maxPromotedSnippetTokens` 160; `untrusted`/`system` candidates removed before any prompt; results and counts go to `DREAMS.md` ("Dream Diary"). CLI: `openclaw memory promote [--apply]`, `promote-explain`, `rem-harness`, `rem-backfill --stage-short-term`, `session-backfill`; slash `/dreaming on|off|status`. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/dreaming)
- Tools: `memory_search` (hybrid; can include session transcript hits), `memory_get` (file or line range), `intent` (event-conditioned standing intents); CLI `openclaw memory status|search|index --force|reset`. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory)

### Inferences
- The design's core safety idea is that only `MEMORY.md`/`USER.md` (curated, trusted) are ever injected automatically; everything the model wrote during a session stays retrieval-only until a scored, provenance-filtered promotion pass. That is directly reusable.
- Numbers worth copying as defaults: 400/80-token chunks, 30-day half-life, 3 injected trigger entries, 4k-char user profile, 20k/60k bootstrap budgets, 03:00 nightly sweep.
- sqlite-vec being optional with a brute-force fallback indicates that at personal-agent scale (thousands of chunks) exact cosine over stored BLOBs is fast enough.

### Gaps
- Chunking token rules beyond "400 tokens / 80 overlap," the exact hybrid vector/text weights, and whether sqlite-vec is bundled by default or loaded opportunistically were not stated on the fetched pages.
- `DREAMS.md` format and the Dreams UI (gateway/doctor page) were not fetched.
- The llms.txt index was truncated at ~100k chars, so a dedicated `memory-core` plugin reference page may exist that I did not read.

## Memory — 3. Memory frameworks (mem0, Letta/MemGPT, Zep/Graphiti, Cognee, newer entrants)

### Takeaway
All four incumbents are Apache-2.0 and run on Apple Silicon and Linux, but their centres of gravity have shifted toward hosted platforms: mem0's headline 2026 numbers are platform-only and its OSS `add()` is now ADD-only; Letta has pivoted from a Postgres server to a git-backed "MemFS" harness (Letta Code) with the Docker server deprecated; Zep's community edition is discontinued (Graphiti remains the OSS core); cognee is the most self-contained local option (keyless default, embedded DBs, MCP). Hindsight (MIT) is the notable 2025-26 entrant with independently reproduced LongMemEval results.

### Cited Findings
**mem0**
- License Apache 2.0; 67.4k stars; PyPI `mem0ai` 2.2.1 released Sep 25, 2026 (Python >=3.10, <4.0); Python and TypeScript SDKs (`pip install mem0ai`, `npm install mem0ai`). — [Source](https://github.com/mem0ai/mem0), [Source](https://pypi.org/project/mem0ai/)
- README (April 2026 algorithm): "Single-pass ADD-only extraction -- one LLM call, no UPDATE/DELETE. Memories accumulate; nothing is overwritten."; "Multi-signal retrieval -- semantic, BM25 keyword, and entity matching scored in parallel and fused."; benchmark table LoCoMo 71.4 -> 92.5, LongMemEval 67.8 -> 94.4, BEAM(1M) 64.1, BEAM(10M) 48.6, ~7K tokens, p50 0.88-1.09 s; caveat: "Scores reflect Mem0's managed platform, which includes proprietary optimizations not available in the open-source SDK; open-source users should expect directionally similar gains but not identical numbers." — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mem0ai/mem0/main/README.md)
- `add()` docs: an LLM "pulls out key facts, decisions, or preferences"; "New memories are added without overwriting or deleting existing memories"; table says "ADD-only; memories accumulate" (Platform) and "ADD-only; you control storage" (OSS); `infer=True` default, `infer=False` stores raw payload; optional `expiration_date`. — [Source](https://docs.mem0.ai/core-concepts/memory-operations/add)
- Paper arXiv 2504.19413 (Chhikara, Khant, Aryan, Singh, Yadav; submitted Apr 28 2025): 26% relative improvement in LLM-as-a-Judge over OpenAI's memory on LoCoMo; graph variant ~2% higher; 91% lower p95 latency and >90% token savings vs full-context. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.19413)
- Paper body: two phases; extraction uses a rolling summary + last 10 messages; update phase retrieves 10 similar memories and the LLM picks ADD / UPDATE / DELETE / NOOP via tool call; Mem0g stores (source, relation, destination) triplets in Neo4j, a conflict detector marks outdated edges invalid rather than deleting them; LoCoMo = 10 conversations averaging ~26k tokens, ~200 questions each, adversarial category excluded; overall J: full-context 72.90, Mem0g 68.44, Mem0 66.88, Zep 65.99, OpenAI 52.90; p95 latency full-context ~17.1 s vs Mem0 1.44 s; store size Mem0 ~7k tokens vs Zep >600k. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413)
- Zep's rebuttal (Chalef & Rasmussen, May 6 2025): Mem0 assigned the user role to both speakers, appended timestamps to text instead of `created_at`, and ran searches sequentially; corrected Zep = 75.14% +/- 0.17 vs Mem0 Graph 68.44 / Mem0 Base 66.88 (full-context GPT-4o-mini 72.90); LoCoMo flaws: category 5 unusable, image-content questions unanswerable from BLIP captions, wrong speaker attribution, ambiguous answers, conversations (16-26k tokens) fit in context, no knowledge-update questions. — [Source](https://www.getzep.com/blog/lies-damn-lies-statistics-is-mem0-really-sota-in-agent-memory/)
- Graph memory today: entities extracted and linked by co-occurrence ("does not assign labeled edges"), built natively with no external graph DB; the earlier `enable_graph`/`graph_store` integration (Neo4j, Memgraph, Kuzu, Apache AGE, Neptune) "has been replaced" and `relations` is always empty. — [Source](https://docs.mem0.ai/open-source/features/graph-memory)
- Self-hosted stack: REST API (port 8888) + web dashboard (port 3000) via Docker Compose (`make up`, `make bootstrap`); Postgres with pgvector; `JWT_SECRET`, per-user `m0sk_` API keys, `/setup` wizard; bundled LLMs `openai`/`anthropic`/`gemini`, embedders `openai`/`gemini` (Ollama not mentioned; adding a provider requires editing `server/main.py` and rebuilding); dashboard Memories page browses/searches, deletion only via entity-level "cascade-delete." — [Source](https://docs.mem0.ai/open-source/setup)

**Letta / MemGPT**
- Repo `letta-ai/letta`: Apache-2.0; current source lives in `letta-ai/letta-code` ("agent harness, terminal UI, App Server, channels, and the runtime for the desktop and web apps"); `npm install -g @letta-ai/letta-code`; the `archive` branch holds "the retired Letta V1 API server." — [Source](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta)
- `letta-code`: Apache-2.0; "a stateful agent harness for creating agents that are more like people than tools"; MemFS "tracks all context, including memory blocks, in git"; `letta backend local`; 3.7k stars. PyPI `letta` 0.34.12 (Oct 12, 2026, Apache-2.0) is now described as "Letta Code: stateful agents in your terminal." — [Source](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta-code), [Source](https://pypi.org/project/letta/)
- MemGPT paper arXiv 2310.08560 (Packer, Wooders, Lin, Fang, Patil, Stoica, Gonzalez; Oct 2023): "virtual context management" inspired by OS paging between fast and slow memory; evaluated on document analysis and multi-session chat. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.08560)
- Memory blocks: fields `label`, `description` ("the main information used by the agent to determine how to read and write to that block"), `value` (setting it "completely replaces the entire block content"), `limit` (characters), `read_only`; conventional `persona` and `human` blocks; rendered in the prompt inside `<memory_blocks>` with `chars_current`/`chars_limit`; a block can be attached to many agents (`block_ids`), last-write-wins. — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory-blocks)
- Archival memory: "general-purpose vector DB," effectively unlimited, tools `archival_memory_insert` / `archival_memory_search` (tag filters), developer API `client.agents.passages.*`; cannot be pinned to context; contrasted with conversation search over past messages. — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/archival-memory)
- MemFS (current model): "a git repository that belongs to the agent"; memories are Markdown files with YAML frontmatter addressed by label (`persona` -> `persona.md`); root-level files load into the system prompt every turn, directories with their own `MEMORY.md` index stay out of context; agent-owned skills under `$MEMORY_DIR/skills`; every edit is a commit; keyword search via the MemFS Search mod, semantic/hybrid needs QMD. — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/concepts/memfs/index.md)
- Dreaming (replaces "sleep-time agents" in docs): background subagents "consolidate useful lessons" into memory; `createAgent` accepts `dreaming` with `trigger` in `off | step-count | compaction-event`, `behavior` in `reminder | auto-launch`, `stepCount` (example 25); CLI `/sleeptime`; "Agent reviews before applying" option. — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/agent-sdk/memory/index.md), [Source](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/sleep-time-agents)
- Sleep-time compute paper arXiv 2504.13171 (Lin, Snell, Wang, Packer, Wooders, Stoica, Gonzalez; Apr 17 2025): ~5x less test-time compute for the same accuracy on Stateful GSM-Symbolic/AIME; up to +13% / +18% accuracy; 2.5x lower average cost per query when amortized across related queries. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13171)
- Self-hosting: "The CLI runs agents in-process" (no account needed); App Server for central hosting; Docker Compose/Railway/Fly configs in a deployment repo; local state at `~/.letta/lc-local-backend`, each agent's MemFS at `~/.letta/lc-local-backend/memfs/<agent-id>/memory` (`LETTA_LOCAL_BACKEND_DIR` override); local providers Ollama, LM Studio, llama.cpp (`letta --backend local connect ollama`); self-hosted agents are not reachable from chat.letta.com and not auto-backed-up. The legacy `letta/letta:latest` Docker image (port 8283, embedded Postgres, pgvector) is "no longer an actively maintained or supported Letta product surface." — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/self-hosting/index.md), [Source](https://docs.letta.com/guides/selfhosting)
- ADE: original "deprecated and replaced with the new ADE inside chat.letta.com"; it exposed editable core memory blocks, the live context window, archival memory, tools, and a simulator. — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/guides/ade/overview)

**Zep & Graphiti**
- Graphiti: Apache-2.0; "a framework for building and querying temporal context graphs for AI agents"; explicit bi-temporal tracking, episodes as provenance, entity nodes with evolving summaries, edges as triplets with validity windows, superseded facts invalidated not deleted; backends Neo4j 5.26, FalkorDB 1.1.2, Amazon Neptune (+OpenSearch Serverless), Kuzu 0.11.2 (deprecated); MCP server in `mcp_server/` (Docker + Neo4j); OpenAI default with Anthropic/Gemini/Groq/Azure, Ollama via OpenAI-compatible endpoint (example `nomic-embed-text`). — [Source](https://github.com/getzep/graphiti)
- PyPI `graphiti-core` 0.31.1 released Oct 15, 2026 (Apache-2.0, Python >=3.10,<4). — [Source](https://pypi.org/project/graphiti-core/)
- Zep paper arXiv 2501.13956 (Rasmussen, Paliychuk, Beauvais, Ryan, Chalef; Jan 20 2025): DMR 94.8% vs MemGPT 93.4%; up to 18.5% accuracy gain on LongMemEval; 90% latency reduction. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.13956)
- Zep Community Edition: "Zep Community Edition is no longer supported"; code moved to a `legacy/` folder; the `getzep/zep` repo is now examples/integrations for Zep Cloud (managed, proprietary "Context Graph Engine"). — [Source](https://github.com/getzep/zep), [Source](https://github.com/getzep/graphiti)

**Cognee**
- Apache-2.0; 32.5k stars; v1.6.1 "Google Sync & Visualization" (Sep 24 2026), v1.6.0 keyless workflows (Sep 18 2026); PyPI `cognee` 1.6.5 (Oct 16 2026, Python >=3.10,<3.15). — [Source](https://github.com/topoteretes/cognee), [Source](https://pypi.org/project/cognee/)
- Operations `remember`, `recall`, `improve`, `forget`; "Runs locally for free -- no API key required": default path uses a local GLiNER extraction model and a local embedding model on CPU; Ollama guide; single-Postgres mode with PGVector (Postgres-as-graph-store "released as a demo feature"); SDKs Python, TypeScript (`@cognee/cognee-ts`), Rust (`cognee-rs`); `cognee-mcp` (port 8001 in Docker Compose) for Cursor/Cline/other MCP clients; "Runs on your own machine with embedded databases." — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/topoteretes/cognee/main/README.md), [Source](https://docs.cognee.ai/)

**Newer entrants**
- LangMem (LangChain): MIT; 1.7k stars; PyPI `langmem` 0.0.30 released Oct 27, 2025 ("Prebuilt utilities for memory management and retrieval"); background memory manager + in-conversation memory tools. No release in ~12 months. — [Source](https://github.com/langchain-ai/langmem), [Source](https://pypi.org/project/langmem/)
- Supermemory: MIT (plugins and MCP server open source); 31.3k stars; self-host via `npx supermemory local` on `http://localhost:6767` or Docker (amd64/arm64); offline text memory works with Ollama but URL ingestion uses a hosted reader; Enterprise tier exists. — [Source](https://github.com/supermemoryai/supermemory)
- Hindsight (Vectorize): MIT; 51.3k stars; retain / recall / reflect; four networks (world facts, experiences, observations, mental models); storage Postgres+pgvector, Oracle 23ai, or "Embedded DB (pg0)"; claims SOTA on LongMemEval as of Jan 2026, "independently reproduced" by Virginia Tech's Sanghani Center and The Washington Post. Paper arXiv 2512.12818 (Dec 14 2025): with an open 20B model accuracy rises 39% -> 83.6% over same-backbone full context; 91.4% LongMemEval and 89.61% LoCoMo with a larger backbone. — [Source](https://github.com/vectorize-io/hindsight), [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.12818)
- MemOS (MemTensor): Apache 2.0; 11.9k stars; "MemOS 2.0 Stardust"; claims LoCoMo 88.83 and LongMemEval 89.20, OpenClaw task completion 36.63% -> 50.87%, 35.24% token savings; papers arXiv 2507.03724 and 2505.22101; news through 2026-08-17. — [Source](https://github.com/MemTensor/MemOS)
- A-MEM: arXiv 2502.12110 (Xu et al.; Feb 2025, v11 Oct 8 2025, NeurIPS 2025): Zettelkasten-style notes with contextual descriptions, keywords, tags and links; "memory evolution" updates existing notes when new ones arrive. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12110)
- Mastra (TypeScript): core Apache-2.0 with `ee/` directories under the Mastra Enterprise License; 28.9k stars; memory = message history (`lastMessages`), working memory (template injected as a system message), semantic recall, "Observational Memory" (background agents compress older messages into an observation log; `observationalMemory: true`), memory processors; LibSQL default store; scoping by `resource` (user) vs `thread`. — [Source](https://github.com/mastra-ai/mastra), [Source](https://mastra.ai/docs/memory/overview)

### Inferences
- For a self-hosted personal agent, mem0's OSS path now offers ADD-only extraction plus hybrid retrieval with your own Postgres+pgvector; the UPDATE/DELETE/NOOP conflict resolution from the 2025 paper is no longer the default behaviour, so dedup/supersession must be built or configured separately.
- Letta's architecture (always-in-context labelled blocks with char limits + out-of-context searchable store + background consolidation) is still the best conceptual model; the new MemFS makes it "markdown files in a git repo," which converges with OpenClaw and Claude Code.
- The LoCoMo leaderboard is not decision-grade: vendor-run, small (10 conversations), with known gold-label flaws and scores now clustered in the 88-94% range. LongMemEval with independent reproduction (Hindsight) is the more credible yardstick.
- Graphiti is the only mature OSS temporal knowledge graph but requires a graph DB (Neo4j/FalkorDB); for a single user, OpenClaw-style markdown + sqlite is lighter and mem0's "native" entity linking shows graphs can be approximated without a graph database.

### Gaps
- mem0 OpenMemory MCP: `github.com/mem0ai/mem0/tree/main/openmemory` returns 404 and the docs index no longer names OpenMemory; it appears superseded by the bundled REST server + dashboard, but no deprecation notice was found. Its MCP tool names (`add_memories`, `search_memory`, etc.) were not verified.
- Letta: exact memory-editing tool names in the current harness (e.g. `memory_replace`/`memory_insert`/`memory_rethink`) and the on-disk format beyond "git repository" were not on the fetched pages; whether `letta` PyPI and `@letta-ai/letta-code` npm are the same artifact is unclear.
- Cognee: the "ECL (Extract, Cognify, Load)" terminology and the default embedded databases (SQLite / LanceDB / Kuzu or NetworkX) did not appear on the fetched README/docs pages; the docs llms.txt index did not name backends.
- ATANT (arXiv 2604.10981) reproduction-failure claims about LoCoMo and the "444 of 446 category-5 questions have empty gold" figure came from a search summary and were not fetched.
- A-MEM repo license, Supermemory and MemOS latest release versions, and whether Hindsight's code (not just the 20B model) is released were not verified.

## Memory — 4. Local embedding search (stores, models, Apple frameworks)

### Takeaway
For a self-hosted personal agent the pragmatic stack is SQLite + sqlite-vec (revived in March 2026, now v0.1.9 stable with ANN indexes in alpha) or LanceDB (embedded, Apache-2.0, native wheels for macOS arm64 and Linux), fed by a 100-600M-parameter local embedder served from Ollama's `/api/embed` (nomic-embed-text v1.5, bge-m3, EmbeddingGemma-300m, or Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B). Apple's on-device `NLEmbedding`/`NLContextualEmbedding` exist but are English-centric/undocumented in dimension and better suited to classification features than retrieval; the Foundation Models framework exposes no embeddings API.

### Cited Findings
**sqlite-vec**
- Dual-licensed Apache-2.0 and MIT; "sqlite-vec is a pre-v1, so expect breaking changes!"; Mozilla Builders is the main sponsor; `vec0` virtual tables; float, int8 and binary vectors; metadata, auxiliary and partition-key columns; bindings `npm install sqlite-vec`, `pip install sqlite-vec`, `gem`, Go, Rust (`cargo add sqlite-vec`), Datasette, rqlite; "runs anywhere SQLite runs (Linux/MacOS/Windows, in the browser with WASM, Raspberry Pis, etc.)". — [Source](https://github.com/asg017/sqlite-vec)
- Maintenance: v0.1.7 (17 Mar 2026) notes say the project was revived "after a long hiatus" (contributor vlasky maintained a fork meanwhile) and added real `DELETE` support plus distance-range constraints; v0.1.8 (30 Mar) fixed npm install; v0.1.9 (31 Mar 2026, latest stable) fixed DELETE with long metadata; v0.1.10-alpha.1..4 (31 Mar - 18 May 2026) introduce new ANN indexes (rescore, experimental IVF, DiskANN) and `ALTER TABLE RENAME`; 159 open issues / 57 PRs. — [Source](https://github.com/asg017/sqlite-vec/releases)
- PyPI: 0.1.9 (Mar 31 2026) stable, 0.1.10a4 (May 18 2026) pre-release. — [Source](https://pypi.org/project/sqlite-vec/)
- vec0 syntax: `contents_embedding float[1024]`; KNN via `WHERE embedding MATCH '[...]' AND k = 5`; metadata columns TEXT/INTEGER/FLOAT/BOOLEAN filterable with `= != > >= < <=` (max 16; "slower full scan," strings >12 chars inefficient); `partition key` columns (max 4) restrict the scan; auxiliary `+col` columns (max 16) are returned but not filterable. — [Source](https://alexgarcia.xyz/sqlite-vec/features/vec0.html)
- JavaScript: `sqliteVec.load(db)` works with `node:sqlite` (Node >= 23.5.0, open with `{ allowExtension: true }`), `better-sqlite3`, `node-sqlite3`, `bun:sqlite`, Deno `@db/sqlite`; Bun on macOS may need `Database.setCustomSQLite(...)` because Apple's SQLite disallows extensions; vectors bound as `Float32Array`. — [Source](https://alexgarcia.xyz/sqlite-vec/js.html)

**LanceDB and alternatives**
- LanceDB: Apache-2.0; "an open-source embedded retrieval library"; Python, TypeScript and Rust SDKs (+REST); Lance columnar format; vector search, full-text search, hybrid search, reranking, filtering, SQL; Enterprise is a separate distributed product (`db://` URIs). — [Source](https://github.com/lancedb/lancedb), [Source](https://docs.lancedb.com/)
- PyPI `lancedb` 0.40.0 (Oct 7 2026, Apache, Python >=3.10) ships abi3 wheels for `macosx_11_0_arm64`, `manylinux_2_28_x86_64`, `manylinux_2_28_aarch64`, `win_amd64`. — [Source](https://pypi.org/project/lancedb/)
- DuckDB `vss`: HNSW on `FLOAT[N]` arrays with `l2sq`/`cosine`/`ip`; persistence needs `SET hnsw_enable_experimental_persistence = true` and "WAL recovery for custom indexes is not yet implemented" (not recommended for production); index must fit in RAM; deletes only marked until `PRAGMA hnsw_compact_index`; "experimental." — [Source](https://duckdb.org/docs/current/core_extensions/vss.html)
- pgvector: PostgreSQL License; v0.8.7 per install commands; HNSW and IVFFlat; `vector` indexable to 2,000 dims (16,000 storage), `halfvec` 4,000, `bit` 64,000, `sparsevec`; Postgres 13+ (pg13-pg18 images). — [Source](https://github.com/pgvector/pgvector), [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pgvector/pgvector/master/LICENSE)
- Chroma: Apache-2.0; 29.5k stars; in-process `chromadb.Client()` with persistence or `chroma run --path`; Python and JS SDKs; tagged pypi/npm releases "on Mondays." — [Source](https://github.com/chroma-core/chroma)

**Local embedding models**
- nomic-embed-text-v1.5: ~0.1B params; 768 dims, Matryoshka to 512/256/128/64 (MTEB 62.28 at 768 -> 56.10 at 64); 8192-token context; Apache-2.0; requires task prefixes `search_document:`, `search_query:`, `clustering:`, `classification:`; sentence-transformers, Transformers, Transformers.js, ONNX, TEI. Ollama `nomic-embed-text` = 274 MB, v1.5 (`137m-v1.5-fp16`), 91.3M pulls, listed with a 2K context window. — [Source](https://huggingface.co/nomic-ai/nomic-embed-text-v1.5), [Source](https://ollama.com/library/nomic-embed-text)
- nomic-embed-text-v2-moe: 475M total / 305M active params; 768 -> 256 Matryoshka; 512-token context; ~100 languages; Apache-2.0; needs `trust_remote_code=True`; paper Feb 11 2025. — [Source](https://huggingface.co/nomic-ai/nomic-embed-text-v2-moe)
- BAAI/bge-m3: 1024 dims; 8192 tokens; dense + sparse + multi-vector (ColBERT); 100+ languages; MIT; Ollama `bge-m3` = 1.2 GB, 567M params, 7.8M pulls. — [Source](https://huggingface.co/BAAI/bge-m3), [Source](https://ollama.com/library/bge-m3)
- google/embeddinggemma-300m: ~300M params; 768 dims with MRL truncation to 512/256/128; 2,048-token context; 100+ languages; Gemma license (gated download); paper Sep 24 2025; documented for sentence-transformers. Ollama `embeddinggemma` = 622 MB, 2K context, 2.5M pulls. — [Source](https://huggingface.co/google/embeddinggemma-300m), [Source](https://ollama.com/library/embeddinggemma)
- Qwen3-Embedding: 0.6B / 4B / 8B; dims 1024 (user-defined 32-1024) / 2560 / 4096; 32K context; 100+ languages incl. code; Apache-2.0; MTEB multilingual 64.33 (0.6B); needs transformers >= 4.51.0, sentence-transformers >= 2.7.0; Ollama `qwen3-embedding` 0.6b 639 MB, 4b 2.5 GB, 8b 4.7 GB, 4.3M pulls. — [Source](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B), [Source](https://ollama.com/library/qwen3-embedding)
- all-MiniLM-L6-v2: 22.7M params; 384 dims; truncates past 256 word pieces; Apache-2.0; English; sentence-transformers/ONNX/OpenVINO. — [Source](https://huggingface.co/sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2)
- Snowflake arctic-embed-l-v2.0: Apache-2.0; 74 languages; paper arXiv 2412.04506; sentence-transformers (`trust_remote_code=True`), Transformers.js, ONNX, TEI. — [Source](https://huggingface.co/Snowflake/snowflake-arctic-embed-l-v2.0)
- Ollama embeddings API: `POST /api/embed` with `model`, `input` (string or array), `truncate` (default true), `dimensions`, `keep_alive`, `options`; returns `embeddings`, `prompt_eval_count`; OpenAI-compatible `/v1/embeddings` at `http://localhost:11434/v1/` supporting `model`, `input`, `encoding_format`, `dimensions` (no token arrays, no `user`). — [Source](https://docs.ollama.com/api/embed), [Source](https://docs.ollama.com/api/openai-compatibility)
- Transformers.js: Apache-2.0; runs on ONNX Runtime; WASM CPU by default, WebGPU opt-in (`device: 'webgpu'`); dtypes fp32/fp16/q8/q4; `feature-extraction` pipeline for embeddings; CDN example pins `@huggingface/transformers@4.3.1`; 16.4k stars. — [Source](https://github.com/huggingface/transformers.js), [Source](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers.js/index)
- mlx-embeddings (Apple Silicon via MLX): GPL-3.0 (copyleft, flag); BERT, XLM-RoBERTa, ModernBERT, Qwen3 and Qwen3-VL embedding architectures; 449 stars. — [Source](https://github.com/Blaizzy/mlx-embeddings)

**Apple frameworks**
- `NLContextualEmbedding` (iOS 17 / macOS 14 / visionOS 1 / watchOS 10): "A model that computes sequences of embedding vectors for natural language utterances"; properties `dimension`, `maximumSequenceLength`, `languages`, `scripts`, `hasAvailableAssets`, `modelIdentifier`, `revision`; `init?(language:)`, `init?(script:)`, `requestAssets(completionHandler:)` ("downloads them if available"), `load()`, `unload()`, `embeddingResult(for:language:)` returning per-token vectors; Apple recommends `NLEmbedding` for semantic similarity and `NLContextualEmbedding` for text classification/word tagging (Create ML `bertEmbedding`). — [Source](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/naturallanguage/nlcontextualembedding.json)
- WWDC23 "Explore Natural Language multilingual models": the contextual embeddings are BERT models trained with a masked-LM objective; "27 languages across three separate models, one each for Latin-script, Cyrillic, and Chinese/Japanese/Korean"; assets download on demand; inference runs on device. — [Source](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2023/10042/)
- `NLEmbedding` (iOS 13 / macOS 10.15): "A map of strings to vectors, which locates neighboring, similar strings"; `vector(for:)`, `distance(between:and:distanceType:)`, `neighbors(for:maximumCount:distanceType:)`; `sentenceEmbedding(for:)` requires iOS 14 / macOS 11; "Sentence embeddings are dynamic... Nearest-neighbor search therefore doesn't apply"; custom embeddings via Create ML `MLWordEmbedding` or `write(_:language:revision:to:)`; a single instance "isn't safe for concurrent use." — [Source](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/naturallanguage/nlembedding.json), [Source](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/naturallanguage/nlembedding/sentenceembedding(for:).json), [Source](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/naturallanguage/finding-similarities-between-pieces-of-text.json)
- Foundation Models framework (iOS 26 / macOS 26 / visionOS 26 / watchOS 27 per the page): "access to any large language model, like the on-device and Private Cloud Compute models designed for Apple Intelligence"; `SystemLanguageModel`, `LanguageModelSession`, `@Generable` guided generation, `Tool` calling; requires an Apple Intelligence-capable device; embeddings are not mentioned anywhere on the page. Apple's 2025 tech report describes the on-device model as "~3B-parameter" with KV-cache sharing and 2-bit quantization-aware training, plus LoRA adapters. — [Source](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/foundationmodels.json), [Source](https://machinelearning.apple.com/research/apple-foundation-models-tech-report-2025)

### Inferences
- Everything in this section runs on Apple Silicon and Linux: sqlite-vec/LanceDB/Chroma/pgvector ship arm64 macOS and Linux builds, and all listed embedders are available through Ollama (which runs on both) or sentence-transformers. Only mlx-embeddings is macOS-only (and GPL-3.0).
- For a single user, brute-force sqlite-vec (or OpenClaw's fallback of cosine over stored BLOBs) is adequate; ANN indexes matter only past ~10^5-10^6 chunks. If you want FTS + vectors + hybrid in one dependency, LanceDB is the simpler choice; if you want "one SQLite file," sqlite-vec + FTS5 is.
- Model choice heuristic from the specs: all-MiniLM for English-only speed (384-d), nomic-v1.5 or EmbeddingGemma for 768-d MRL-truncatable general use, bge-m3 or Qwen3-0.6B when multilingual/long-context matters (1024-d), with EmbeddingGemma's gated Gemma license and nomic's required prefixes as the gotchas.
- Apple's NL embeddings are useful for zero-dependency on-device similarity in a native macOS/iOS shell, but undocumented dimensions/languages and sentence-embedding availability per language make them a secondary path rather than the primary index.

### Gaps
- `NLContextualEmbedding` output dimension and per-language sentence-embedding availability are not stated in Apple's docs or the WWDC transcript (prior recollection: 512-d contextual, English-first sentence embeddings; unverified).
- Snowflake arctic-embed parameter counts/dimensions (recollection: l-v2.0 ~568M/1024-d, m-v2.0 ~305M/768-d) were not visible in the fetched card slices.
- npm metadata (versions, platform packages) for `sqlite-vec`, `@lancedb/lancedb` and `openclaw` could not be fetched (npmjs.com returned 403); Chroma's PersistentClient page returned 404.
- DuckDB's own license (MIT) and the Foundation Models on-device context window size (recollection: ~4,096 tokens) were not confirmed on fetched pages.
- EmbeddingGemma support in MLX/transformers.js/llama.cpp was not confirmed from the model card (only sentence-transformers is documented there).

## Memory — 5. Design patterns (persona layering, profiles, consolidation, editing UIs, benchmarks, safety)

### Takeaway
The field has converged on: a small always-injected layer (persona + user profile + curated facts, each with a hard budget), a large retrieval-only episodic layer, a background consolidation job that promotes/merges/supersedes with provenance filters, and a user-facing editor. The biggest unsolved risk is memory poisoning via indirect prompt injection, which has been demonstrated against ChatGPT, Gemini and a reference agent (4/10 -> >80% success once the agent is told to consult memory), so write-gating, provenance tagging and user review are first-class requirements.

### Cited Findings
**Soul/persona files and system-prompt layering**
- OpenClaw layers `AGENTS.md` (operating rules) + `SOUL.md` (voice) + `IDENTITY.md` + `USER.md` (user model, 4k cap) + `MEMORY.md` (main session only) under 20k-per-file / 60k-total char budgets, and injects daily notes only via search. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/agent-workspace)
- Letta renders labelled blocks (`persona`, `human`, ...) into the prompt inside `<memory_blocks>` with `chars_current`/`chars_limit` and a `description` that tells the agent how to use each block; MemFS root files load every turn while indexed subdirectories stay out of context. — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory-blocks), [Source](https://docs.letta.com/concepts/memfs/index.md)
- Claude Code layers CLAUDE.md (enterprise/project/user), path-scoped `.claude/rules/`, `AGENTS.md`, and the auto-memory `MEMORY.md` index (200 lines/25KB) with topic files read on demand. — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory)
- Anthropic's context-engineering guidance (Sep 29 2025): compaction "typically serves as the first lever," structured note-taking (e.g. a `NOTES.md`) persists state outside the window, sub-agents return 1,000-2,000-token summaries, and "just-in-time" retrieval keeps lightweight identifiers (paths, queries) and loads content at runtime; Claude Code uses a hybrid of up-front CLAUDE.md plus on-demand files. — [Source](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/effective-context-engineering-for-ai-agents)

**User profile representations**
- Free-text block: Letta `human` block (character-limited, replaced wholesale on `value` set); Claude's legacy single memory summary. — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory-blocks), [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work)
- Topic files with metadata: Claude.ai "Topics" editable per item; Claude Code topic files with `type` and `modified` frontmatter. — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work), [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory)
- Directive entries with provenance: OpenClaw `USER.md` entries carry `observed:` date and `status: active|superseded`, superseded in place. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/user-model)
- Discrete facts with metadata/expiry: mem0 memories carry `metadata` and optional `expiration_date`; mem0's newer design links facts through extracted entities instead of typed edges. — [Source](https://docs.mem0.ai/core-concepts/memory-operations/add), [Source](https://docs.mem0.ai/open-source/features/graph-memory)
- Structured template: Mastra working memory is a template injected as a system message, scoped per `resource` (user) by default. — [Source](https://mastra.ai/docs/memory/overview)
- Temporal triplets: Graphiti/Zep edges as (entity, relation, entity) with validity windows, invalidated rather than deleted; Mem0g's conflict detector likewise marks relations invalid "which preserves temporal history." — [Source](https://github.com/getzep/graphiti), [Source](https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413)

**Consolidation: reflection, decay, dedup/conflict resolution**
- Generative Agents (Park et al., arXiv 2304.03442, Apr 2023): agents "store a complete record of the agent's experiences using natural language," synthesize them "into higher-level reflections," and retrieve them to plan; ablations show observation, planning and reflection each contribute to believability. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.03442)
- MemoryBank (Zhong et al., arXiv 2305.10250, May 2023): memory updating "inspired by the Ebbinghaus Forgetting Curve theory," forgetting or reinforcing memories by elapsed time and significance; SiliconFriend companion. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.10250)
- mem0 paper: LLM chooses ADD / UPDATE / DELETE / NOOP against the 10 most similar memories; mem0's 2026 OSS default dropped this for ADD-only accumulation. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413), [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mem0ai/mem0/main/README.md)
- A-MEM: new notes trigger "memory evolution" that updates context/attributes of linked older notes (Zettelkasten). — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12110)
- OpenClaw dreaming: nightly `0 3 * * *` light/REM/deep sweep; promotion requires weighted score plus `minScore`, `minRecallCount`, `minUniqueQueries`; recency decay with 30-day half-life; importance scored at write time; untrusted/system candidates excluded; human-readable `DREAMS.md`. Pre-compaction memory flush appends to the daily note only. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/dreaming), [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-search), [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory)
- Letta dreaming: triggered by `step-count` or `compaction-event`, optionally auto-launching a background subagent that consolidates lessons; sleep-time compute paper shows offline pre-processing cuts test-time compute ~5x. — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/agent-sdk/memory/index.md), [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13171)
- Mastra Observational Memory: background agents compress older messages into a dense observation log. — [Source](https://mastra.ai/docs/memory/overview)
- Hindsight: explicit `reflect` operation reasons over the memory bank "to produce answers and update information traceably." — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.12818)
- Vendor cadence: Claude's legacy memory synthesized every 24 h; current design writes as you chat. — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work)

**User-editable memory UIs**
- Claude: Settings > Memory > Topics (edit/delete), pause, reset, per-chat off, incognito, import/export, citations to source chats. — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work)
- Gemini: "Saved Info" page for explicit facts, delete individual memories, disconnect apps, My Activity dashboard, Temporary Chat (72 h). — [Source](https://www.datacamp.com/blog/gemini-personal-intelligence), [Source](https://blog.google/products/gemini/temporary-chats-privacy-controls/)
- Copilot: dashboard to review memories, choose categories kept, opt out. — [Source](https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2025/04/04/your-ai-companion/)
- mem0 self-hosted dashboard: Memories page (browse/search), Entities page with cascade-delete, Requests audit log, API keys; no per-memory edit described. — [Source](https://docs.mem0.ai/open-source/setup)
- Letta ADE: view/edit core memory blocks, inspect the live context window, search archival memory (now inside chat.letta.com); MemFS gives git history for every memory edit. — [Source](https://docs.letta.com/guides/ade/overview), [Source](https://docs.letta.com/concepts/memfs/index.md)
- OpenClaw: `USER.md` edited in Agents -> Files or Settings -> Profile -> Personal instructions; `DREAMS.md` for human review; `openclaw memory forget` for session-derived deletion. — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/user-model), [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-architecture)
- Claude Code: `/memory` opens plain-markdown memory files in your editor; "Saved N memories / Recalled N memories" indicators. — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory)
- Microsoft AI Red Team recommends "allowing for user modification of memory elements, and for effective remediation of memory poisoning events." — [Source](https://cdn-dynmedia-1.microsoft.com/is/content/microsoftcorp/microsoft/final/en-us/microsoft-brand/documents/Taxonomy-of-Failure-Mode-in-Agentic-AI-Systems-Whitepaper.pdf)

**Benchmarks and surveys**
- LoCoMo (Maharana et al., arXiv 2402.17753, Feb 2024): LLM-agent-generated persona conversations grounded in event graphs, human-verified; ~300 turns / ~9K tokens avg, up to 35 sessions; QA, event summarization, multimodal dialogue tasks. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17753)
- LongMemEval (Wu et al., arXiv 2410.10813, ICLR 2025): five abilities (information extraction, multi-session reasoning, temporal reasoning, knowledge updates, abstention); 500 questions; 30% accuracy drop for commercial assistants; helpful designs: session decomposition, fact-augmented key expansion, time-aware query expansion. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10813)
- MemBench (Tan et al., arXiv 2506.21605, ACL 2025 Findings): factual vs reflective memory, participation vs observation scenarios, metrics for effectiveness, efficiency and capacity. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21605)
