- Survey "Memory in the Age of AI Agents" (Hu, Liu, Yue, Zhang + 43 others; arXiv 2512.13564, Dec 15 2025, v2 Jan 13 2026): taxonomy by forms (token-level, parametric, latent), functions (factual, experiential, working), dynamics (formation, evolution, retrieval); compiles benchmarks and OSS frameworks; frontiers include trustworthiness and multi-agent memory. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13564)
- Benchmark credibility: Zep documents how Mem0's LoCoMo comparison misconfigured Zep and lists LoCoMo's gold-label problems; mem0 itself says its 2026 scores reflect the managed platform; Hindsight's LongMemEval results were independently reproduced by Virginia Tech and The Washington Post. — [Source](https://www.getzep.com/blog/lies-damn-lies-statistics-is-mem0-really-sota-in-agent-memory/), [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mem0ai/mem0/main/README.md), [Source](https://github.com/vectorize-io/hindsight)

**Privacy and safety: memory poisoning, injection via stored memories**
- Microsoft AI Red Team taxonomy (Apr 2025) defines memory poisoning: "A threat actor can manipulate future actions of an agent by adding content, most notably malicious instructions, to the system's memory, which the agent will process each time it is recalled." Case study: LangGraph/GPT-4o email assistant with procedural/episodic/semantic RAG memory; a poisoned email instructing it to forward internal code/API mail externally succeeded 4/10 at baseline, and "After modifying the agent's system prompt to encourage the agent to check its memory before responding, the attack success rose to over 80%." Mitigations: "requiring external authentication or validation for all memory updates," least-privilege memory access, controlling the structure/format of stored items, live monitoring and user modification of memory. — [Source](https://cdn-dynmedia-1.microsoft.com/is/content/microsoftcorp/microsoft/final/en-us/microsoft-brand/documents/Taxonomy-of-Failure-Mode-in-Agentic-AI-Systems-Whitepaper.pdf), [Source](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/security/blog/2025/04/24/new-whitepaper-outlines-the-taxonomy-of-failure-modes-in-ai-agents/)
- MINJA (Dong et al., arXiv 2503.03704, Mar 2025, v5 Feb 2026): injects malicious memory records through ordinary queries only (bridging steps, indication prompt, progressive shortening) so later victim queries retrieve them. — [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03704)
- SpAIware (Rehberger, Sep 20 2024): prompt injection from a website made ChatGPT's memory tool store instructions that exfiltrated all future chats via image URLs; the exfil channel was fixed in ChatGPT 1.2024.247 but "websites can still store arbitrary memories through prompt injection." — [Source](https://embracethered.com/blog/posts/2024/chatgpt-macos-app-persistent-data-exfiltration/)
- Gemini (Rehberger, Feb 10 2025): a document to summarize planted a delayed tool invocation; when the user replied "yes," Gemini saved attacker-chosen facts to long-term memory that persisted across sessions; Google rated it "abuse-related risk with low likelihood and low impact." Lessons: require explicit confirmation to persist memories and review stored memories regularly. — [Source](https://embracethered.com/blog/posts/2025/gemini-memory-persistence-prompt-injection/)
- Platform-side defences in the wild: Anthropic's memory tool docs require path-traversal checks and recommend stripping sensitive data, capping file sizes and expiring stale files; OpenClaw stores provenance classes per entry, taints turns after network-sourced tool results, excludes untrusted/system candidates from dreaming, and only auto-injects trusted `MEMORY.md`/`USER.md`; Letta blocks can be `read_only`; Claude incognito and Gemini Temporary Chats keep sessions out of memory. — [Source](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/memory-tool), [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/memory-architecture), [Source](https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory-blocks), [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11817273-how-does-claude-s-memory-work), [Source](https://blog.google/products/gemini/temporary-chats-privacy-controls/)

### Inferences
- A defensible minimal design for a self-hosted personal agent: (1) `SOUL.md`-style persona + a 4k-char directive-style user profile with observed dates and supersession; (2) append-only daily notes indexed by FTS5 + vectors; (3) a nightly consolidation job that promotes only entries recalled multiple times from trusted (user-authored or user-confirmed) turns, with a 30-day recency half-life on episodic material; (4) a UI listing every memory with source link, edit, delete, pause and "forget this session."
- Write-gating is the single most important safety control: the Microsoft study shows that the very instruction that makes memory useful ("check your memory before acting") doubles poisoning success, so content derived from tool/web/email inputs should never be promoted to the auto-injected layer without user confirmation (OpenClaw's taint model is a ready template).
- Prefer "invalidate/supersede" over "delete" (Graphiti, Mem0g, OpenClaw USER.md) so the agent can reason about changed preferences and the user can audit history.
- Benchmark on LongMemEval-style tasks (knowledge updates, temporal reasoning, abstention) rather than LoCoMo QA, and expect vendor numbers not to transfer to your stack.

### Gaps
- ChatGPT's "Manage memories" UI and OpenAI's own safety mitigations after SpAIware could not be verified from primary sources (403s).
- The `mem0` OpenMemory dashboard (as distinct from the current bundled dashboard) and Letta's new in-chat ADE were not inspected directly.
- MemBench and LongMemEval leaderboards/current SOTA tables were not fetched; only the papers' abstracts were.
- No fetched source quantifies decay/forgetting parameters beyond OpenClaw's 30-day half-life and MemoryBank's qualitative Ebbinghaus design; AgentPoison (arXiv 2407.12784) and OWASP agentic-security guidance were not fetched.

Research date: 2026-10-18. Method: primary-source WebFetch of modelcontextprotocol.io, blog.modelcontextprotocol.io, GitHub repos, Anthropic/Google/Microsoft/Slack/Notion/Linear/GitHub developer docs. Web search budget was exhausted early in the session, so a few secondary claims are flagged under Gaps rather than asserted.

## MCP & connectors — 1. MCP spec status as of Oct 2026

### Takeaway
The current revision is **2026-07-28** (RC 2026-05-21, final 2026-07-28), a "stateless" rewrite that removed sessions and the `initialize` handshake, replaced server-initiated requests (sampling/elicitation/roots) with Multi Round-Trip Requests, moved Tasks into an official extension, deprecated Roots/Sampling/Logging/HTTP+SSE/Dynamic Client Registration on a 12-month clock, and formalized an extensions framework (MCP Apps, Tasks, Enterprise-Managed Auth, Skills). Governance moved to the Linux Foundation's Agentic AI Foundation (Dec 2025); the official registry is still labeled "preview"; TypeScript SDK v2 (split packages) and Python SDK v2 are the stable lines.

### Cited Findings

**Dated revisions and what each added**
- **2025-03-26** (vs 2024-11-05): comprehensive OAuth 2.1 authorization framework (PR #133); Streamable HTTP transport replacing HTTP+SSE (PR #206); JSON-RPC batching (PR #228); tool annotations such as read-only/destructive (PR #185); `message` on ProgressNotification; audio content; `completions` capability — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-03-26/changelog)
- **2025-06-18**: removed JSON-RPC batching (PR #416); structured tool output (PR #371); MCP servers classified as OAuth Resource Servers with protected resource metadata (PR #338); RFC 8707 resource indicators required (PR #734); new security best practices page; elicitation (PR #382); resource links in tool results (PR #603); `MCP-Protocol-Version` header required on HTTP (PR #548); `_meta` on more types (PR #710); `title` display field (PR #663); `context` in completions (PR #598) — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-06-18/changelog)
- **2025-11-25**: OpenID Connect Discovery for auth server discovery (PR #797); icons for tools/resources/prompts (SEP-973); incremental scope consent via `WWW-Authenticate` (SEP-835); tool-name guidance (SEP-986); enum elicitation schemas (SEP-1330); **URL-mode elicitation** (SEP-1036); **sampling with tools** via `tools`/`toolChoice` (SEP-1577); **OAuth Client ID Metadata Documents** as recommended registration (SEP-991); **experimental tasks** (SEP-1686); stderr logging clarification; `description` on `Implementation` to align with registry server.json; HTTP 403 for invalid Origin; input validation errors as tool execution errors (SEP-1303); SSE polling (SEP-1699); PRM discovery aligned to RFC 9728 with `.well-known` fallback (SEP-985); default values in elicitation (SEP-1034); JSON Schema 2020-12 default dialect (SEP-1613); governance formalized (SEP-932), Working/Interest Groups (SEP-1302), SDK tiering (SEP-1730) — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/changelog)
- **2026-07-28** major changes: removed protocol-level sessions and `Mcp-Session-Id` (SEP-2567); removed `initialize`/`initialized` handshake, protocol version and client capabilities now travel in `_meta` (`io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion`, `io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities`) (SEP-2575); new required `server/discover` RPC; `subscriptions/listen` replaces the HTTP GET stream and `resources/subscribe`; removed `ping`, `logging/setLevel`, `notifications/roots/list_changed`; tasks moved to extension `io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks` with `tasks/get` polling and `tasks/update` (SEP-2663); **Multi Round-Trip Requests (MRTR)** replace `roots/list`, `sampling/createMessage`, `elicitation/create` with `resultType: "input_required"` + `inputResponses` retry (SEP-2322); required `resultType` on all results; SSE resumability/`Last-Event-ID` removed — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/changelog)
- **2026-07-28** minor changes: `extensions` field in capabilities; OpenTelemetry `traceparent`/`tracestate`/`baggage` in `_meta` (SEP-414); deterministic `tools/list` ordering for prompt caching; required `Mcp-Method`/`Mcp-Name` headers on Streamable HTTP POSTs and `x-mcp-header` tool-parameter mirroring (SEP-2243); required `ttlMs`/`cacheScope` on list/read results (SEP-2549); RFC 9207 `iss` validation (SEP-2468); `application_type` required in DCR (SEP-837); client credentials bound to issuer (SEP-2352); any JSON Schema 2020-12 keywords in schemas (SEP-2106); removed `notifications/elicitation/complete` and `elicitationId`; error-code allocation policy (-32020..-32099 reserved) — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/changelog)
- **2026-07-28 deprecations**: Roots, Sampling, Logging deprecated (SEP-2577; migrate to tool params/resource URIs, direct LLM APIs, stderr/OTel); HTTP+SSE reclassified Deprecated (SEP-2596); `includeContext` "thisServer"/"allServers" deprecated; **Dynamic Client Registration (RFC 7591) deprecated in favor of Client ID Metadata Documents** (PR #2858); feature lifecycle policy with minimum twelve-month deprecation window and a deprecated-features registry (SEP-2596); PR-based SEP workflow (SEP-1850) — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/changelog)
- Release timeline: RC announced May 21, 2026; beta SDKs June 29, 2026; final July 28, 2026; Tier 1 SDKs (TypeScript, Python, Go, C#) supported the spec at launch, Rust in beta; legacy HTTP+SSE has a "year-long offramp" — [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2026-07-28/), [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/)
- Post-release roadmap (Aug 22, 2026): priorities are agentic messaging primitives (webhooks/channels, maturing Tasks toward core), Streamable HTTP over stdio for local servers, agent identity/DPoP/WIF, progressive tool discovery, SDK DX; Server Card WG continues `.well-known` metadata work; Enterprise-Managed Authorization is stable — [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/mcp-roadmap/)
- Stateful servers now return explicit, server-minted handles as ordinary tool arguments (e.g., `basket_id`); the spec says handles must not be treated as authentication — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools)

**Client registration / auth details**
- Registration priority order: pre-registered credentials → Client ID Metadata Documents (CIMD) when the AS advertises `client_id_metadata_document_supported` → DCR fallback via `registration_endpoint` → prompt user. CIMD `client_id` must be an HTTPS URL with a path (e.g., `https://example.com/client.json`) whose JSON includes at least `client_id`, `client_name`, `redirect_uris`; CIMD client IDs are portable across authorization servers; DCR is marked deprecated — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/client-registration.md)

**Extensions framework**
- Official extension identifiers: `io.modelcontextprotocol/ui` (MCP Apps), `io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks`, `io.modelcontextprotocol/oauth-client-credentials`, `io.modelcontextprotocol/enterprise-managed-authorization`, `io.modelcontextprotocol/skills`; official repos use `ext-` prefix, experimental ones `experimental-ext-`; extensions are negotiated via `extensions` in `_meta["io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities"]` and the `server/discover` response, are disabled by default, and follow SEP-2133 — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/extensions/overview), [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/client-matrix.md)
- **MCP Apps (SEP-1865)**: announced Jan 26, 2026 as "the first official MCP extension" and production-ready; package `@modelcontextprotocol/ext-apps`; launch hosts Claude (web and desktop), Goose, VS Code Insiders, ChatGPT; builds on MCP-UI and the OpenAI Apps SDK — [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2026-01-26-mcp-apps/). Spec version 2026-01-26 is marked Stable; license Apache-2.0; a tool declares a `ui://` resource, the host renders it in a sandboxed iframe, UI↔host traffic is JSON-RPC over postMessage; ext-apps 2.x is wire-compatible with 1.x — [Source](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/ext-apps). Tool metadata uses `_meta.ui.resourceUri`; resources can declare `_meta.ui.csp` and `permissions`; hosts can restrict which tools an app may call — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/apps/overview.md). UI resources use MIME `text/html;profile=mcp-app` — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/extensions/overview), [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp)
- MCP Apps client support per the community matrix: Claude (web), Claude Desktop, VS Code GitHub Copilot, Microsoft 365 Copilot, Goose, Postman, MCPJam, ChatGPT, Cursor, Archestra.AI, PostHog Code; Enterprise Auth: Archestra.AI and mcpc; Skills: mcpc (full), ChatGPT/fast-agent/Inspector (partial) — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/client-matrix.md)
- **Tasks extension**: server returns `CreateTaskResult` (`resultType: "task"`) with `taskId`, `ttlMs`, `pollIntervalMs`; statuses `working`, `input_required`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`; methods `tasks/get`, `tasks/update`, `tasks/cancel`; optional `notifications/tasks` via `subscriptions/listen`; spec lives in ext-tasks repo — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/tasks/overview.md)
- **Server Cards (SEP-2127)**: status Final, Extensions Track, created 2026-01-21; a static JSON document describing a remote server's identity and transport endpoints; recommended location `<streamable-http-url>/server-card`; domain-level discovery via an AI Catalog at `/.well-known/ai-catalog.json`; deliberately excludes tools/resources/prompts; normative schema lives in `experimental-ext-server-card`; clients should prefer live `server/discover` values on conflict — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2127-mcp-server-cards.md)

**Governance**
- Dec 9, 2025: Anthropic donated MCP to the Agentic AI Foundation (AAIF), a Linux Foundation directed fund co-founded by Anthropic, Block, and OpenAI with support from Google, Microsoft, AWS, Cloudflare, Bloomberg; founding projects MCP, goose, AGENTS.md; governance model unchanged; stats: 10,000+ active public servers, 75+ Claude connectors, 97M+ monthly SDK downloads — [Source](https://www.anthropic.com/news/donating-the-model-context-protocol-and-establishing-of-the-agentic-ai-foundation), [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/page/2/)
- 2026 governance changes: Contributor Ladder adopted, Working Groups triage SEPs, maintainer team expanded (Apr 8, 2026 post) — [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/mcp-roadmap/), [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/)

**Official registry**
- Preview launched 2025-09-08 at registry.modelcontextprotocol.io; API froze at v0.1 on 2025-10-24 "for at least a month" to shape v1 for GA; README still says "still a preview release and breaking changes or data resets may occur" — [Source](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/registry), [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2025-09-08-mcp-registry-preview/)
- As fetched Oct 2026, docs still state "The MCP Registry is currently in preview. Breaking changes or data resets may occur before general availability"; it is a metadata-only repository (points to npm/PyPI/Docker Hub packages or remote URLs), is "not intended to be directly consumed by host applications" (consume via aggregators/subregistries implementing its OpenAPI spec), does not support private servers, and the codebase is "not designed for self-hosting" — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/registry/about.md)
- Namespaces/publisher auth: `io.github.<user|org>/*` via GitHub OAuth device flow (`mcp-publisher login github`) or GitHub OIDC in Actions; reverse-DNS `com.example.*/*` via DNS TXT record `v=MCPv1; k=ed25519|ecdsap384; p=<pubkey>` or an HTTP file at `/.well-known/mcp-registry-auth` (keys may live in Google KMS / Azure Key Vault) — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/registry/authentication.md)
- Trust posture: security scanning is delegated to package registries and downstream aggregators; moderation is minimal ("consumers should assume minimal-to-no moderation"), removing only illegal content, malware, spam, and non-functioning servers; removed servers get `status: "deleted"` but metadata remains via API — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/registry/about.md), [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/registry/moderation-policy.md)

**SDKs**
- TypeScript: v2 is "the stable release line" implementing 2026-07-28, split into `@modelcontextprotocol/client`, `@modelcontextprotocol/server`, `@modelcontextprotocol/server-legacy`, `@modelcontextprotocol/codemod`, plus adapters `@modelcontextprotocol/express`, `/fastify`, `/hono`, `/node`; runs on Node, Bun, Deno; v1 remains `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` — [Source](https://ts.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io/v2/). GitHub releases show v2.3.1 (marked Latest) alongside v1 1.32.1, both dated 05 Oct, plus `@modelcontextprotocol/core@2.3.1` — [Source](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/typescript-sdk/releases). v2 betas shipped June 29, 2026 under `@beta`; migration via `npx @modelcontextprotocol/codemod@beta v1-to-v2 .` (e.g., `.tool()` → `registerTool`); v1.x gets bug fixes and security updates for at least six months after v2 ships — [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/sdk-betas-2026-07-28/)
- Python: `mcp` 2.3.0 released Oct 2, 2026 (2.0.0 on Jul 28, 2026; 1.x continued to 1.30.0), MIT, Python 3.11+, transports stdio/Streamable HTTP/SSE — [Source](https://pypi.org/project/mcp/), [Source](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/python-sdk). v2 breaking changes: `FastMCP` → `MCPServer` (`mcp.server.mcpserver`), types moved to `mcp-types` with snake_case fields, `Client` class defaults, `LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION` = 2026-07-28 while still handling 2025-11-25 handshake sessions, Roots/Sampling/Logging emit `MCPDeprecationWarning`, `httpx2` dependency — [Source](https://py.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io/migration/). v1.x branch continues to receive critical bug fixes and security patches — [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/sdk-betas-2026-07-28/)
- FastMCP (standalone, Prefect/jlowin): 4.1.1 released Oct 18, 2026, Apache-2.0, Python ≥3.10; "FastMCP 1.0 was incorporated into the official MCP Python SDK in 2024"; has upgrade guides from FastMCP 2 and 3 — [Source](https://pypi.org/project/fastmcp/)
- Ruby SDK reached 1.0.0 on Jul 27, 2026 — [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/)

### Inferences
- A self-hosted host app written today should target 2026-07-28 semantics (per-request `_meta`, `server/discover`, MRTR, `resultType`) but keep a 2025-11-25 `initialize` fallback, because the deprecation window keeps older servers legitimate for 12+ months and official SDK v2s already do this dual-mode negotiation.
- Do not build new features on Roots, Sampling, or Logging; pass directories via tool args/config, call the model provider directly, and log to stderr/OTel, matching the SEP-2577 migration guidance.
- Prefer CIMD (host a `client.json` on a domain you own) over DCR for your host's OAuth identity; DCR is deprecated and several vendors (e.g., Slack) never supported it.
- The registry is a discovery feed, not a trust source: namespace verification proves publisher identity only; any "verified" badge in your UI should map to namespace proof, not safety.

### Gaps
- The 2024-11-05 changelog page was not fetched; its feature set is inferred as the baseline that 2025-03-26 modified.
- No GA announcement for the registry was found; docs fetched live in Oct 2026 still say "preview". A v1 API date could not be verified.
- Exact calendar date (year) of the TypeScript SDK 2.3.1 / 1.32.1 releases is shown only as "05 Oct" on the releases page; inferred to be 2026.
- The normative Server Card schema/`docs/discovery.md` in `experimental-ext-server-card` was not fetched; field list above is from the SEP summary only.
- Current server count in the registry was not stated on fetched pages.

## MCP & connectors — 2. Claude connectors directory conventions

### Takeaway
Anthropic's surfaces converge on remote MCP over Streamable HTTP with OAuth 2.0/2.1 for hosted connectors, `.mcpb` bundles (formerly `.dxt`) with keychain-backed `user_config` for local Desktop extensions, and a `mcp__<server>__<tool>` permission vocabulary shared by Claude Code and the Agent SDK; the public "Software Directory Policy" (Apr 15, 2026) spells out what submissions must do (narrow human-readable tool descriptions, OAuth with CA certs, tool annotations, privacy policy, test account).

### Cited Findings

**Claude.ai custom connectors (remote MCP)**
- Pro/Max users add custom connectors themselves; Team requires Owners/Primary Owners; Enterprise allows custom roles with "Manage access to Libraries"; Free users get one custom connector — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11175166-getting-started-with-custom-connectors-using-remote-mcp)
- The remote server must be reachable over the public internet from Anthropic's IP ranges; auth options are "Sign in now / when needed / No sign in"; for OAuth Claude can use its published identity, register automatically (dynamic registration), or use your own OAuth client; API-key servers can receive fixed headers on every request — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11175166-getting-started-with-custom-connectors-using-remote-mcp)
- Permission UX: users can disable individual tools, toggle connectors per conversation, Claude asks approval before invoking tools, and users should only pick "Allow always" for tools trusted to run unsupervised; Research mode invokes connector tools automatically; Team/Enterprise owners can disable interactive tool calls; warnings state custom connectors "aren't verified by Anthropic", malicious servers may hide instructions, and server behavior can change without notice — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11175166-getting-started-with-custom-connectors-using-remote-mcp)

**Directory submission requirements**
- The older "Anthropic MCP Directory Policy" was consolidated (Apr 15, 2026) into the Anthropic Software Directory Policy — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11697096-anthropic-mcp-directory-policy)
- Software Directory Policy (Apr 15, 2026) requirements: narrow, unambiguous, human-readable tool descriptions that match behavior; no hidden/obfuscated/encoded instructions; no pulling behavioral instructions from external sources at runtime; no coercing Claude to call other tools; no reading Claude memory/chat history/uploads; collect only needed data; MCP servers must handle errors gracefully, keep token use proportional, tool names ≤64 chars, remote servers requiring auth must use "secure OAuth 2.0 with certificates from recognized authorities", provide tool annotations (especially `readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`, `title`), support Streamable HTTP (SSE "temporarily acceptable but will be deprecated"), local servers use current dependencies; developers must supply a privacy policy, verified contact/support, docs, a test account with sample data, ≥3 example prompts, proof of ownership of connected endpoints/domains; prohibited: money/crypto transfers, standalone AI image/video/audio generation, ad-serving/promotional software — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/13145358-anthropic-software-directory-policy)

**Claude Desktop extensions (.mcpb)**
- Desktop Extensions are ZIP archives containing the MCP server, dependencies, and `manifest.json`; the extension is `.mcpb` ("MCP Bundle"), older `.dxt` still works; Claude Desktop ships its own Node.js runtime; `user_config` fields marked `sensitive` are stored in the OS keychain; Claude Desktop shows a config UI and won't enable the extension until required values are provided; values are injected as `${user_config.key}`; a curated directory with one-click install exists, submissions go through a form and Anthropic review, enterprises can block extensions/publishers, disable the directory, or run private directories — [Source](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/desktop-extensions)
- Manifest spec (now at `modelcontextprotocol/mcpb`): required `manifest_version`, `name`, `version` (semver), `description`, `author`, `server`; optional `icon(s)`, `display_name`, `long_description`, `repository`, `homepage`, `documentation`, `support`, `screenshots`, `tools`, `tools_generated`, `prompts`, `prompts_generated`, `keywords`, `license`, `privacy_policies`, `compatibility` (`claude_desktop`, `platforms` darwin/win32/linux, `runtimes` python/node), `user_config`, `_meta`, `localization`; `server.type` ∈ node, python, binary, uv; `user_config` types string, number, boolean, directory, file; substitutions `${__dirname}`, `${user_config.KEY}`, `${HOME}`, `${DESKTOP}`, `${DOCUMENTS}`, `${DOWNLOADS}`, `${pathSeparator}`/`${/}`; current `manifest_version` "0.3" (updated 2025-12-02; a `uv` example shows "0.4") — [Source](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/mcpb/blob/main/MANIFEST.md)
- Nov 21, 2025: MCPB spec, CLI tooling, and reference implementation moved from Anthropic into the modelcontextprotocol GitHub org; named supporting clients: Claude desktop app, Claude Code, MCP for Windows — [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2025-11-20-adopting-mcpb/)

**Claude Code MCP config**
- `.mcp.json` at project root with `mcpServers` map; `type` ∈ `http` (alias `streamable-http`), `sse` (deprecated), `ws`, `stdio`; scopes: local (`~/.claude.json`, current project), project (`.mcp.json`, shared via git), user (`~/.claude.json`, all projects); precedence local → project → user → plugin → claude.ai connectors, whole entry wins; `${VAR}` and `${VAR:-default}` expansion in command/args/env/url/headers; credential variables such as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` are NOT expanded in remote `url`/`headers` to stop a project config exfiltrating keys — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp)
- `claude mcp add --transport http <name> <url>` (optionally `--header "Authorization: Bearer ..."`); OAuth 2.0 supported for remote servers (triggered on 401/403), via `/mcp` or `claude mcp login <name>`, with `--client-id/--client-secret/--callback-port` for pre-registered apps and `oauth.scopes` pinning; OAuth is not run in `claude -p` or Agent SDK runs; interactive sessions prompt to approve project `.mcp.json` servers; `disabledMcpjsonServers` blocks a server in every mode; timeouts `MCP_TIMEOUT`, `MCP_TOOL_TIMEOUT`, per-server `timeout`, idle timeout 5 min (HTTP/SSE/WS) / 30 min (stdio); output limits 25,000 tokens default (`MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS`), >50,000 chars persisted to file, 16 MiB HTTP cap; tool search on by default — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp)
- Server-author hooks Claude Code honors in `tools/list` `_meta`: `anthropic/requiresUserInteraction: true` forces a prompt on every call (even in `bypassPermissions`, no "don't ask again"); `anthropic/maxResultSizeChars` (up to 500,000); `anthropic/alwaysLoad` to bypass tool-search deferral; elicitation form and URL modes are rendered natively; `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` supports `auto:N` thresholds; managed MCP config via `managed-mcp.json`, `managedMcpServers`, `allowedMcpServers`, `deniedMcpServers`; claude.ai connectors synced into Claude Code appear as `mcp__claude_ai_<server>__<tool>` — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp), [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions)
- Permission rule syntax: `mcp__puppeteer` (whole server), `mcp__puppeteer__*`, `mcp__puppeteer__puppeteer_navigate`; dots/spaces in server names become `_`; plugin servers are `mcp__plugin_<plugin>_<server>__*`; deny/ask rules accept globs like `"mcp__*"`, but allow globs are only honored after a literal `mcp__<server>__` prefix (an unanchored `"mcp__*"` allow is skipped with a warning); `mcp__` rules with parentheses are skipped; "Yes, and don't ask again" writes rules to `.claude/settings.local.json` at the repo root; modes `default`, `acceptEdits`, `plan`, `dontAsk`, `bypassPermissions`, plus `auto` (classifier) — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions)

**Claude Agent SDK**
- `mcpServers` option accepts stdio (`command`/`args`/`env`), `http`, `sse`, and in-process SDK servers (`createSdkMcpServer`); `.mcp.json` loads when the `project` setting source is enabled; tools are `mcp__<server>__<tool>` and need `allowedTools` (wildcards like `mcp__github__*`) or a `canUseTool` callback; docs say "Prefer `allowedTools` over permission modes" because `acceptEdits` does not auto-approve MCP tools and `bypassPermissions` is broader than necessary; the SDK does not run interactive OAuth — servers report `needs-auth` and you must complete OAuth yourself and pass the token in `headers`; connection wait defaults to `MCP_TIMEOUT` 30 s; same 25,000-token output limit as Claude Code — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/mcp)

### Inferences
- If the self-hosted app wants to be listed as a Claude connector later, design for the Software Directory Policy from day one: remote Streamable HTTP, OAuth 2.0 with a real CA cert, `readOnlyHint`/`destructiveHint`/`title` on every tool, ≤64-char tool names, no runtime-fetched instructions.
- For local integrations that end users install, `.mcpb` is now the cross-client packaging format (MCP org, Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Windows), and its `sensitive` `user_config` + keychain pattern is a good model for your own secret prompts.
- Reuse the `mcp__<server>__<tool>` naming and allow/ask/deny semantics in your own permission UI; it is what Claude Code, the Agent SDK, and claude.ai connectors already expose, so users' mental models transfer.

### Gaps
- Claude Desktop's "always allow" scoping for local extensions (per-chat vs per-tool vs global) was not fetched; only the claude.ai custom-connector article's guidance was verified.
- The Desktop Extensions blog describes a directory submission form; the current form URL and review SLA were not verified.
- Whether the claude.ai directory requires CIMD or still relies on DCR for third-party connectors was not stated in fetched sources.

## MCP & connectors — 3. Google Workspace access

### Takeaway
As of Oct 2026 there is no Google-published MCP server for Gmail/Calendar/Drive in the `googleworkspace` GitHub org; Google's agent-facing offerings are the unofficial-but-Google-hosted `gws` CLI (with agent skills and encrypted keyring token storage) and the Gemini CLI `workspace` extension, while "MCP Toolbox" is databases-only. Building your own OAuth adapter means living with Google's scope tiers: `gmail.readonly`/`gmail.modify`/`drive.readonly` are **restricted** (annual CASA assessment if you publish), `gmail.send` is sensitive, `drive.file` and `gmail.labels` are non-sensitive; a personal/self-hosted app can stay unverified under the lifetime 100-user cap, but "Testing" status expires refresh tokens every 7 days.

### Cited Findings

**Official Google MCP servers?**
- The `googleworkspace` GitHub org (60 repos; first page shown) lists no MCP server repo; its flagship is `cli` ("Google Workspace CLI covering Drive, Gmail, Calendar, Sheets, Docs, Chat, Admin, and more ... including AI agent skills"), Rust, Apache-2.0, 31,657 stars — [Source](https://github.com/googleworkspace)
- `gws` is described as "One CLI for all of Google Workspace — built for humans and AI agents", is explicitly "not an officially supported Google product", installs via npm (`@googleworkspace/cli`), Homebrew, cargo, Nix; auth via `gws auth setup` (needs gcloud) or a user-created Desktop OAuth client at `~/.config/gws/client_secret.json` with your account added as a test user; unverified apps in testing mode are limited to about 25 scopes (the 85+ scope "recommended" preset fails); 100+ `SKILL.md` files installable with `npx skills add`; credentials encrypted at rest with AES-256-GCM, key in the OS keyring (or a file backend); the README does not mention an MCP server mode — [Source](https://github.com/googleworkspace/cli)
- Gemini CLI extension `gemini-cli-extensions/workspace` (TypeScript, Apache-2.0, 647 stars, updated Oct 12, 2026) gives Gemini CLI access to Workspace (documents, spreadsheets, presentations, emails, chat, calendar); it contains a `workspace-server` directory; headless auth via `npm run auth-utils -- login`; README carries a prompt-injection warning about untrusted mail/docs — [Source](https://github.com/gemini-cli-extensions), [Source](https://github.com/gemini-cli-extensions/workspace)
- "MCP Toolbox for Databases" (`googleapis/genai-toolbox`, Apache-2.0, v1.14.0 referenced) covers AlloyDB, BigQuery, Cloud SQL, Spanner, Firestore, Postgres, MySQL, MongoDB, Redis, Neo4j, Snowflake etc.; it does not cover Gmail/Calendar/Drive — [Source](https://github.com/googleapis/genai-toolbox)

**Scope classification**
- Gmail: non-sensitive `gmail.labels`, `gmail.addons.current.action.compose`, `gmail.addons.current.message.action`; sensitive `gmail.send`, `gmail.addons.current.message.metadata`, `gmail.addons.current.message.readonly`; restricted `https://mail.google.com/` (full access incl. permanent delete), `gmail.readonly`, `gmail.compose`, `gmail.insert`, `gmail.modify`, `gmail.metadata`, `gmail.settings.basic`, `gmail.settings.sharing` — [Source](https://developers.google.com/workspace/gmail/api/auth/scopes)
- Google's restricted-scope list confirms `https://mail.google.com/` "includes any use of IMAP, SMTP, and POP3" and lists Drive `drive`, `drive.readonly`, `drive.activity(.readonly)`, `drive.metadata(.readonly)`, `drive.scripts`, `drive.meet.readonly` plus Chat message scopes as restricted — [Source](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464325)
- Drive: non-sensitive `drive.file`, `drive.appdata`, `drive.appfolder`, `drive.install`; sensitive `drive.apps.readonly`; restricted `drive`, `drive.readonly`, `drive.metadata(.readonly)`, `drive.activity(.readonly)`, `drive.scripts`, `drive.meet.readonly` — [Source](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/api-specific-auth)
- Google defines the tiers as: non-sensitive (basic verification), sensitive (basic + additional verification), restricted (basic + additional verification + security assessment) — [Source](https://developers.google.com/people/v1/how-tos/authorizing)
- The Verification FAQ cites `gmail.send` as its example of a sensitive scope — [Source](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13463817)

**Verification, CASA, exemptions**
- Apps requesting sensitive or restricted scopes must complete OAuth verification; restricted-scope verification "can potentially take several weeks"; restricted-scope apps must be reverified/reassessed "at least every 12 months"; default publishing status is Testing (test users only, tester warning screen, user cap, limited refresh-token lifetime) — [Source](https://developers.google.com/identity/protocols/oauth2/production-readiness/restricted-scope-verification)
- Verification submissions require a homepage on a verified domain, a privacy policy on the same domain linked from the consent screen, a demo video showing the OAuth consent in English with the exact scopes, and a narrowest-scope justification; restricted scopes additionally require "an annual security assessment by a Google-empanelled group of security assessors" — [Source](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464321)
- The assessment uses the App Defense Alliance CASA framework (based on OWASP ASVS), assigns assurance level AL1 or AL2 based on user count/scopes/risk, yields a Letter of Validation, and is required every year — [Source](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13465431). ADA documents AL1 and AL2 as lab-tested/lab-verified by ADA-authorized labs with annual revalidation — [Source](https://appdefensealliance.dev/casa/casa-tiering)
- FAQ specifics: tier is based on data sensitivity, users per data type, and risk indicators; a "free tier 2 assessment" (self-scan) exists, and if you instead hire an authorized assessor you pay them directly; Tier 3 assessors need read-only access to the production cloud; "Google does not charge the developer any fees for security assessment"; an LOA requires remediating critical/high findings; estimated timelines: brand 2-3 business days, sensitive 10 business days, restricted 6 weeks — [Source](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13463817)
- Verification not needed when: personal use by you and a small group ("fewer than 100 users"); development/testing/staging; service-owned data only; internal use within your Google Workspace or Cloud Identity org (project owned by the org, consent screen set to internal); Workspace admin-trusted or admin-installed Marketplace apps — [Source](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464323)
- Unverified apps accessing sensitive/restricted scopes have a "100 new-user cap"; the cap "applies over the entire lifetime of the project, and it cannot be reset or changed"; exhausting it results in "Google sign-in being disabled for your users"; before that, users see the "unverified app" screen they can click through — [Source](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13463817), [Source](https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13464323)

**Refresh-token rules**
- A project with External user type and Testing publishing status issues refresh tokens that expire in 7 days (unless only `userinfo.email`/`profile`/`openid`); refresh tokens also die when the user revokes access, after six months unused, on password change if Gmail scopes are included, when the account exceeds 100 live refresh tokens per OAuth client ID (oldest silently invalidated), on time-based access expiry, or under admin policy (`admin_policy_enforced`); token size caps: refresh 512 B, access 2048 B, auth code 256 B — [Source](https://developers.google.com/identity/protocols/oauth2)

**Push notifications and fallbacks**
- Gmail push: create a Pub/Sub topic, grant publish to `gmail-api-push@system.gserviceaccount.com`, call `users.watch` (optionally `labelIds`), "You must call the watch method at least once every 7 days" (daily recommended), notification JSON carries `emailAddress` + `historyId`, then call `history.list`; max one notification per user per second (excess dropped); notifications can be delayed/dropped so keep a periodic `history.list` fallback — [Source](https://developers.google.com/workspace/gmail/api/guides/push)
- Calendar push: `watch` on events/calendarList/ACL/settings creates a channel (`id` ≤64 chars, `type: web_hook`, HTTPS `address` with a valid, non-self-signed cert); channels expire and don't auto-renew; a `sync` message arrives first; notifications carry `X-Goog-Resource-State` etc. but no payload, so you must re-query; "Notifications are not 100% reliable" — [Source](https://developers.google.com/workspace/calendar/api/guides/push)
- App passwords: 16-digit codes requiring 2-Step Verification; unavailable when 2SV uses only security keys, for work/school accounts, or Advanced Protection; Google says they "aren't recommended and are unnecessary in most cases", prefers "Sign in with Google", and revokes them on password change — [Source](https://support.google.com/accounts/answer/185833)

**Token storage patterns seen in primary sources**
- `gws`: AES-256-GCM encryption at rest with the key in the OS keyring — [Source](https://github.com/googleworkspace/cli); Claude Code: macOS Keychain, else `~/.claude/.credentials.json` mode 0600 — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/iam); MCPB: `sensitive` user_config in OS keychain — [Source](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/desktop-extensions); MCP guidance: per-server narrowly scoped tokens, OS secret store rather than plaintext config, isolate `~/.ssh`/`~/.aws` via sandboxing — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/draft/tutorials/security/local-server-security.md)

### Inferences
- For a personal, self-hosted agent: create your own GCP project, keep the consent screen External + Testing (no verification, no CASA), add yourself as a test user, and accept the 7-day refresh-token expiry by building re-auth into the UI; or move to "In production" unverified and live with the click-through warning and the lifetime 100-user cap. Both avoid the restricted-scope assessment as long as you are the only user.
- Personal gmail.com accounts cannot use the Internal user type because Google ties it to a project owned by a Workspace/Cloud Identity organization; that option is only for Workspace tenants.
- `gmail.readonly` + `gmail.send` is the minimum useful pair (read + send without `gmail.modify`'s label/archival writes); `drive.file` avoids restricted Drive scopes if you only need files the app created or the user picked.
- Wrapping `gws` (or the Gemini CLI workspace server) as a stdio MCP server is the fastest path that reuses Google-maintained API coverage and encrypted token storage, at the cost of depending on a pre-1.0, "not officially supported" tool.
- Push via Pub/Sub needs a publicly reachable endpoint or a pull subscription; for a self-hosted home server, a Pub/Sub pull subscription (no inbound port) plus daily `watch` renewal is the pragmatic design.

### Gaps
- Calendar and Contacts/People scope classifications (sensitive vs restricted) are not labeled on the fetched Google pages; commonly treated as "sensitive", but this could not be verified from a primary source in this session.
- CASA assessor cost ranges are not published by Google ("costs agreed between developer and assessor"); third-party price quotes could not be checked because the web-search budget was exhausted.
- Whether `gws` or the Gemini CLI `workspace` extension exposes an MCP endpoint (vs. skills/CLI only) was not confirmed from their READMEs.
- Google Workspace developer blog announcements about MCP/agents were not retrieved.

## MCP & connectors — 4. Other connectors

### Takeaway
Slack, Notion, Linear, and GitHub all ship first-party **remote** MCP servers over Streamable HTTP with OAuth (Linear also supports DCR; Slack explicitly does not and requires a confidential client), while Microsoft's first-party MCP servers are tenant-bound (work/school) with the Graph "MCP Server for Enterprise" in preview and read-only; personal Microsoft accounts remain a Graph/Entra app-registration question rather than an MCP offering.

### Cited Findings

**Microsoft 365 / Graph**
- Graph access is delegated (scopes on behalf of a signed-in user) or app-only (application permissions/app roles); apps register in Entra with an Application ID, redirect URI, and optional credential; least-privilege is recommended — [Source](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/auth/auth-concepts)
- Entra `signInAudience` options: `AzureADMyOrg`, `AzureADMultipleOrgs`, `AzureADandPersonalMicrosoftAccount`, `PersonalMicrosoftAccount`; apps that include personal Microsoft accounts face extra limits (max two client secrets, no national clouds, max 30 permissions per resource such as Graph, no app roles for MSA users at runtime) — [Source](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/supported-accounts-validation)
- `microsoft/mcp` (MIT) is the index of official Microsoft MCP servers: Azure MCP and Fabric MCP (Public Preview) built there; remote hosted servers include Microsoft Foundry (`mcp.ai.azure.com`), Azure Resource Manager (`mcp.management.azure.com`), Microsoft Learn (`learn.microsoft.com/api/mcp`), and the Microsoft 365 family at `https://agent365.svc.cloud.microsoft/agents/tenants/{tenant_id}/servers/mcp_MailTools` (also `mcp_CalendarTools`, `mcp_TeamsServer`, `mcp_ODSPRemoteServer`, `mcp_M365Copilot`, `mcp_MeServer`, `mcp_AdminTools`, `mcp_SharePointListsTools`), each requiring a Microsoft Entra tenant ID — [Source](https://github.com/microsoft/mcp), [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/mcp/main/README.md)
- Microsoft MCP Server for Enterprise (`https://mcp.svc.cloud.microsoft/enterprise`) is in PREVIEW, translates natural language into read-only Graph calls for Entra identity data via `microsoft_graph_suggest_queries`, `microsoft_graph_get`, `microsoft_graph_list_properties`; no extra license; 100 calls/min/user plus Graph throttling; usage is auditable in Microsoft Graph activity logs under appId `e8c77dc2-69b3-43f4-bc51-3213c9d915b4`; Global cloud only — [Source](https://learn.microsoft.com/graph/mcp-server/overview)

**Slack**
- Official remote server at `https://mcp.slack.com/mcp`; JSON-RPC over Streamable HTTP only ("SSE connections are not supported"); Dynamic Client Registration is not supported; confidential OAuth using your app's client ID and secret (PKCE available for desktop clients); discovery via `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` and `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`; user-token endpoints `slack.com/oauth/v2_user/authorize` and `api/oauth.v2.user.access`; per-tool user scopes (e.g., `search:read.public`, `chat:write`, `canvases:write`, `lists:read`/`lists:write`); tools cover search (messages/files/users/channels/emoji), messaging (send/schedule/draft/read/reactions), canvases, users, file upload, lists; available in Claude.ai, Claude Code, Perplexity, Cursor; "Only Marketplace-published or internal apps may use MCP"; admins approve MCP clients; MCP activity appears in audit logs; rate limits shared with Web API tiers (Tier 2 20+/min to Tier 4 100+/min) — [Source](https://docs.slack.dev/ai/mcp-server/)
- May 29, 2025 change: `conversations.history` and `conversations.replies` moved to Tier 1 for commercially distributed non-Marketplace apps — 1 request/minute and `limit` max 15 — applying immediately to apps created on/after May 29, 2025 and new installs; internal customer-built apps are exempt (50+/min, 1,000 objects); Marketplace apps unaffected; updated API ToS effective June 30, 2025 for older apps — [Source](https://docs.slack.dev/changelog/2025/05/29/rate-limit-changes-for-non-marketplace-apps)

**Notion**
- Hosted "Remote Notion MCP": Streamable HTTP `https://mcp.notion.com/mcp` (recommended) and SSE fallback `https://mcp.notion.com/sse`; default browser OAuth; Business/Enterprise workspace owners can mint MCP tokens used as bearer tokens; Enterprise SAML workspaces can use enterprise-managed authorization; available on every plan (AI search, SQL queries, some filters gated to Business/Enterprise with Notion AI); guests cannot connect — [Source](https://developers.notion.com/docs/get-started-with-mcp). Workspace owners manage client access under Settings → Notion MCP; restricting clients requires Enterprise; org owners can revoke connections via Admin API — [Source](https://developers.notion.com/docs/mcp)
- Open-source `makenotion/notion-mcp-server` (MIT) uses an internal integration token (`NOTION_TOKEN`), stdio or `--transport http` on 127.0.0.1:3000 `/mcp` with its own bearer token; v2.0.0 targets Notion API 2025-09-03 with 22 tools; it is "no longer actively maintained or supported" and the README recommends the hosted server — [Source](https://github.com/makenotion/notion-mcp-server)

**Linear**
- `https://mcp.linear.app/mcp` (read-write), `https://mcp.linear.app/mcp/readonly`, `https://mcp.linear.app/sse` (deprecated fallback); interactive setup uses OAuth 2.1 with dynamic client registration; alternatively pass an OAuth access token or Linear API key as `Authorization: Bearer`; request only the `read` OAuth scope or a Read-only API key for read-only access; Enterprise Okta managed authentication; supported clients include Claude (connectors, desktop, `claude mcp add`), Codex, Cursor, VS Code, Windsurf, Zed, Jules, v0, or any client via `npx -y mcp-remote` — [Source](https://linear.app/docs/mcp)

**GitHub**
- `github/github-mcp-server` (MIT): remote endpoint `https://api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/` (`"type": "http"`), insiders variant `/mcp/insiders`; OAuth (browser login, token kept in memory) or a PAT as Bearer; toolsets (`--toolsets` / `GITHUB_TOOLSETS`): context, actions, code_quality, code_security, copilot, copilot_issue_intents, dependabot, discussions, gists, git, governance, issues, labels, notifications, orgs, projects, pull_requests, repos, secret_protection, security_advisories, stargazers, users (+ remote-only copilot_spaces, github_support_docs_search); `default` = context, repos, issues, pull_requests, users; `--read-only` skips write tools; Docker image `ghcr.io/github/github-mcp-server`; some repo tools return structured output only for protocol version 2026-07-28+; the `v2.0.0` tag is unusable as a Go module pending `v2.0.1` — [Source](https://github.com/github/github-mcp-server)

### Inferences
- For a personal app, GitHub (fine-grained PAT or OAuth), Linear (DCR or API key), and Notion (OAuth, all plans) are low-friction; Slack is the outlier: without a Marketplace listing you must register your own Slack app (internal/custom app for your own workspace), supply client ID + secret, and accept that reading history via the Web API from a distributed non-Marketplace app is throttled to 1 req/min — internal apps are exempt.
- Microsoft's M365 MCP servers are built for Entra tenants (tenant ID in the URL); for an Outlook.com/personal account you will likely need your own Entra app registration with `AzureADandPersonalMicrosoftAccount` and direct Graph calls rather than an official MCP server.
- Licenses: GitHub and Notion open-source servers are MIT; Slack, Linear, Notion hosted servers are services governed by their API terms, not OSS licenses; `microsoft/mcp` is MIT but individual servers live in other repos.

### Gaps
- Slack's MCP launch date (secondary sources say Feb 17, 2026) and GA/beta status were not stated on docs.slack.dev.
- Whether the reference Slack server in `modelcontextprotocol/servers` is archived (secondary claim: May 2025) was not verified.
- Status (preview/GA), licensing (Agent 365 / Copilot), and auth details of the `agent365.svc.cloud.microsoft` M365 servers are not documented in the `microsoft/mcp` README; the linked `bap-microsoft/MCP-Platform` repo was not fetched.
- GitHub MCP "dynamic toolsets" flag and Notion's numeric rate limits were not on the fetched page sections.

## MCP & connectors — 5. Letting users add arbitrary MCP servers safely

### Takeaway
The consensus model across the spec, Claude Code, VS Code, Cursor, and Anthropic's own guidance is: treat every third-party server as untrusted code, gate each tool with allow/ask/deny rules keyed by `mcp__<server>__<tool>`, pin and re-prompt on tool-description changes (rug pulls), treat annotations as hints that only earn trust for known servers, sandbox stdio servers (Seatbelt/bubblewrap via `srt`, or containers via Docker MCP Gateway), inject per-server secrets from a keychain instead of the process environment, allowlist egress, and log every `tools/call`.

### Cited Findings

**Permission models in shipping clients**
- Spec: tools are model-controlled but "there SHOULD always be a human in the loop with the ability to deny tool invocations"; clients SHOULD prompt for confirmation on sensitive operations, show tool inputs before calling, validate results, implement timeouts, and log tool usage; servers MUST validate inputs, implement access controls, rate limit, and sanitize outputs — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools)
- Claude.ai connectors: approval before each tool call, "Allow always" only for trusted tools, per-tool disable, Research mode auto-invokes, org-level ability to disable interactive tools — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11175166-getting-started-with-custom-connectors-using-remote-mcp)
- Claude Code: allow/ask/deny rules on `mcp__<server>`, `mcp__<server>__*`, `mcp__<server>__<tool>`; glob deny `"mcp__*"` removes every MCP tool from context; persistent approvals saved to `.claude/settings.local.json`; project `.mcp.json` servers require approval; `disabledMcpjsonServers`; managed `allowedMcpServers`/`deniedMcpServers`; `anthropic/requiresUserInteraction` forces a human prompt even under `bypassPermissions` — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions), [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp)
- Agent SDK: `allowedTools` wildcards per server, `canUseTool` callback, guidance to prefer `allowedTools` over `bypassPermissions` — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/mcp)
- VS Code: workspace servers inherit Workspace Trust; servers from other sources show a trust dialog on first start or when their configuration changes; "Configure Tools" toggles individual tools; per-server `"sandboxEnabled": true` (macOS/Linux) with rules to deny reads, grant writes, and allow network domains; tool calls from sandboxed servers are auto-approved; use `${input:...}` instead of hardcoded keys; enterprise allowlists via Copilot settings — [Source](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/copilot/customization/mcp-servers)
- Cursor: approval before MCP tools by default with argument inspection; Auto-review mode runs allowlisted tools immediately and routes others through a classifier; admin MCP Allowlist by command pattern (stdio) or URL pattern (remote), per-server tool allowlists, a user MCP network denylist, per-server network modes Allow all / Allowlist / Deny all / No sandbox; OAuth via DCR or static `CLIENT_ID`/`CLIENT_SECRET`/`scopes`; `${env:NAME}` interpolation for secrets — [Source](https://cursor.com/docs/context/mcp)

**Tool annotations**
- Annotations `title`, `readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`, `idempotentHint`, `openWorldHint`; "clients MUST consider tool annotations to be untrusted unless they come from trusted servers"; tool names SHOULD be 1-128 chars from `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]`; aggregating clients SHOULD prefix names with a server identifier to avoid collisions — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools)
- Maintainer analysis (Mar 16, 2026): unannotated tools default to non-read-only, potentially destructive, non-idempotent, open-world; annotations can drive confirmation prompts and graduated trust but "don't stop prompt injection" and "aren't enforcement"; a `readOnlyHint` from an untrusted server isn't actionable; session-level risk ("lethal trifecta": private data + untrusted content + external communication) matters more than per-tool flags; open SEPs propose trust/sensitivity annotations (#1913, #1984), `unsafeOutputHint` (#1561), `secretHint` (#1560), `trustedHint` (#1487) — [Source](https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2026-03-16-tool-annotations/)

**Known attacks**
- Invariant Labs (Apr 1, 2025) Tool Poisoning Attacks: hidden instructions in tool descriptions (e.g., an `add` tool told to read `~/.cursor/mcp.json` and `~/.ssh/id_rsa` into a `sidenote` arg); **rug pulls** (description changes after approval); **shadowing** (a malicious server's description redirects a trusted server's `send_email`); tested on Cursor; mitigations: show full descriptions, pin/hash tool definitions, cross-server data-flow controls, guardrails — [Source](https://invariantlabs.ai/blog/mcp-security-notification-tool-poisoning-attacks)
- Invariant Labs (Apr 7, 2025) WhatsApp exploit: a `get_fact_of_the_day` tool swapped in a malicious description on second launch and made the agent exfiltrate chat history via the trusted whatsapp-mcp `send_message` tool; the confirmation dialog showed only "Hi" with the payload off-screen; the post notes sandboxing is not a mitigation for instruction-following attacks and that some clients don't notify on description changes — [Source](https://invariantlabs.ai/blog/whatsapp-mcp-exploited)
- MCP Security Best Practices (2026-07-28) enumerates: confused deputy in OAuth proxies (per-client consent, `__Host-` cookies, exact redirect URI match, single-use `state`); token passthrough forbidden (audience validation); SSRF via OAuth metadata URLs (HTTPS only, block private/link-local ranges incl. 169.254.169.254, egress proxies, DNS pinning); state-handle hijacking; local server compromise (one-click installs MUST show the exact command, SHOULD sandbox with minimal privileges); OAuth URL validation (reject `javascript:`/`data:`/`file:`, never open URLs via shell); stdio proxy escalation; mix-up attacks (RFC 9207 `iss`); localhost redirect-URI impersonation with CIMD; CIMD trust policies (domain allowlists); scope minimization — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/security_best_practices)
- MCP "Local Server Security" guide: a stdio server shares one trust domain with the client; threats include malicious/typosquatted packages, overcollection, compromised dependencies, poisoned tool catalogs (poisoning, shadowing, rug pulls), vulnerable local bridges; mitigations: prefer remote servers, verify publisher, pin versions (container digests freeze the whole tree; `npx`/`uvx` pins only the top-level package), read tool descriptions, prefer clients that pin definitions and re-prompt on change, run in containers with minimal mounts (`--network=none` when possible), per-server env secrets, OS secret store, read-only narrow directories, egress allowlists, audit logs; SEP-1024 requires one-click installers to show the exact command — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/draft/tutorials/security/local-server-security.md)
- OWASP MCP Top 10 (Beta): MCP01 Token Mismanagement & Secret Exposure; MCP02 Privilege Escalation via Scope Creep; MCP03 Tool Poisoning; MCP04 Software Supply Chain Attacks; MCP05 Command Injection & Execution; MCP06 Prompt Injection via Contextual Payloads; MCP07 Insufficient Authentication & Authorization; MCP08 Lack of Audit and Telemetry; MCP09 Shadow MCP Servers; MCP10 Context Injection & Over-Sharing — [Source](https://owasp.org/www-project-mcp-top-10/)

**Sandboxing stdio servers**
- Anthropic `sandbox-runtime` (`srt`, `@anthropic-ai/sandbox-runtime`, Apache-2.0, beta research preview): macOS `sandbox-exec` with generated Seatbelt profiles; Linux bubblewrap (bind mounts, no network namespace) plus seccomp BPF blocking Unix sockets and namespace creation; all network via host-side HTTP/HTTPS and SOCKS5 proxies with domain allow/deny lists; default deny for network and writes, reads allowed with `denyRead` overrides; config `~/.srt-settings.json` (`network.allowedDomains`, `filesystem.allowWrite/denyRead`, `allowUnixSockets`, `allowLocalBinding`, `enableWeakerNetworkIsolation`, `allowAppleEvents`); wraps MCP servers by prefixing the command in `.mcp.json` (`"command": "srt", "args": ["npx", "-y", "@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem"]`); Windows alpha uses a dedicated `srt-sandbox` user and WFP egress fence; caveats: `allowLocalBinding` bypasses the proxy for loopback, `enableWeakerNetworkIsolation` opens a `trustd` exfil path, Linux glob support is narrower, seccomp unavailable on some architectures — [Source](https://github.com/anthropic-experimental/sandbox-runtime)
- Anthropic's Claude Code sandboxing post (Oct 20, 2025): filesystem + network isolation via bubblewrap/Seatbelt covering subprocesses, proxy over a Unix domain socket that prompts for new domains, 84% fewer permission prompts internally, `srt` open-sourced — [Source](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/claude-code-sandboxing)
- Docker MCP Gateway (MIT): runs catalog servers as per-server containers with restricted privileges/network/resources, profiles select which servers a client sees, secrets via Docker Desktop (`docker mcp secret set 'brave.api_key=...'`), built-in OAuth flows, flags `--verify-signatures`, `--block-secrets`, `--log-calls`, pluggable interceptors; transports stdio, sse, streaming — [Source](https://github.com/docker/mcp-gateway), [Source](https://www.docker.com/blog/docker-mcp-gateway-secure-infrastructure-for-agentic-ai/), [Source](https://docs.docker.com/ai/mcp-catalog-and-toolkit/mcp-gateway/). The Docker MCP Catalog lists "300+ verified servers" as container images; the Gateway under Docker AI Governance is invite-only — [Source](https://docs.docker.com/ai/mcp-catalog-and-toolkit/)
- VS Code per-server `sandboxEnabled` and Cursor per-server network modes (above) show client-integrated sandboxing is now mainstream — [Source](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/copilot/customization/mcp-servers), [Source](https://cursor.com/docs/context/mcp)

**Secret handling**
- Claude Code refuses to expand credential variables (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`) into remote server `url`/`headers` — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp); MCPB keychain-backed `sensitive` fields — [Source](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/desktop-extensions); Docker secrets store — [Source](https://www.docker.com/blog/docker-mcp-gateway-secure-infrastructure-for-agentic-ai/); MCP guidance for per-server scoped tokens, OS secret store, short-lived broker-issued credentials — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/draft/tutorials/security/local-server-security.md); spec warns not to mark sensitive params with `x-mcp-header` since headers are visible to intermediaries — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools)

**Auditing, rate limits, trust signals**
- Spec: clients SHOULD "Log tool usage for audit purposes"; servers MUST rate limit; OTel `traceparent` propagation in `_meta` is now documented — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools), [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/changelog)
- Vendor examples: Slack MCP activity in audit logs with per-tool rate tiers — [Source](https://docs.slack.dev/ai/mcp-server/); Microsoft Graph activity logs by appId and 100 calls/min/user — [Source](https://learn.microsoft.com/graph/mcp-server/overview); Docker `--log-calls` — [Source](https://www.docker.com/blog/docker-mcp-gateway-secure-infrastructure-for-agentic-ai/)
- Client-side containment knobs in Claude Code: idle timeouts (5 min HTTP, 30 min stdio), auto-backgrounding after 2 min, 25,000-token output cap, 16 MiB message cap — [Source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp)
- Registry trust signals: namespace ownership proven via GitHub login/OIDC or DNS/HTTP challenges; no security scanning by the registry; minimal moderation; `status: "deleted"` for removed entries — [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/registry/about.md), [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/registry/moderation-policy.md), [Source](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/registry/authentication.md)
- Anthropic: custom connectors "aren't verified by Anthropic"; directory-listed software must meet the Software Directory Policy — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11175166-getting-started-with-custom-connectors-using-remote-mcp), [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/13145358-anthropic-software-directory-policy)

### Inferences
- A workable policy for a personal self-hosted host: three trust tiers (first-party/bundled, registry-namespace-verified, unknown). Only tier 1 may auto-approve based on `readOnlyHint`; tier 2/3 default to "ask" for every tool, with per-tool "always allow" scoped to the server's pinned tool-definition hash so a rug pull resets approvals.
- Hash and store `tools/list` (name, description, schema, annotations) per server; on any diff, revoke standing approvals and show a visual diff — this directly addresses rug pulls and shadowing, which sandboxing cannot.
- Render full tool arguments (no truncation) in confirmation dialogs; the WhatsApp exploit worked because the UI hid the payload.
- Run every stdio server under `srt` (macOS/Linux) or in a container with `--network=none` by default and a per-server domain allowlist when network is needed; keep OAuth tokens in the OS keychain and inject them per server via env at spawn time, never via a shared shell environment.
- Prefer remote OAuth servers for SaaS (Slack/Notion/Linear/GitHub) since they run no local code; reserve local servers for filesystem/hardware access.
- Record an append-only audit log of `{server, tool, args hash, annotations, approval source, duration, result size}` and apply per-server token-bucket limits in the host; the spec puts rate limiting on servers, but a host cannot rely on unknown servers to do it.

### Gaps
- Default CPU/memory limits and the `--block-network` flag of Docker MCP Gateway were not confirmed on fetched pages.
- VS Code's exact tool-confirmation options ("Allow in this session / workspace / always") and `chat.tools.global.autoApprove` were not present on the fetched page version.
- gVisor, Landlock, and Apple App Sandbox specifics for MCP servers were not researched (no search budget); `srt` does not mention Landlock.
- OWASP MCP Top 10 publication date and version are not stated on the project page (IDs use ":2025", status Beta).
- Claude Desktop's handling of changed tool descriptions after approval was not verified.

Research date: 2026-10-18. Versions/licenses were read from the npm registry JSON (`registry.npmjs.org/<pkg>/latest`) and GitHub's REST API; npmjs.com HTML pages returned 403 and the WebSearch budget was exhausted after the first round, so everything below comes from direct fetches of primary sources. "Unpacked size" is npm's on-disk figure, not shipped bundle weight.

## Editors & viewers — Markdown editors with WYSIWYG + source modes

### Takeaway
Only MDXEditor ships a first-party rich-text / source / diff toggle; every other candidate needs a hand-built split view and a lossy AST round trip. For a React app that must edit arbitrary user Markdown faithfully, the safest pattern is CodeMirror 6 as the source of truth (with Obsidian-style decorations if you want "live preview") plus a remark-based WYSIWYG (MDXEditor or Milkdown Crepe) as an optional mode; BlockNote's XL packages (GPL-3.0) and TipTap's paid platform features need licence attention.

### Cited Findings
- **Milkdown / Crepe**: `@milkdown/crepe` 7.22.3, MIT, 3.46 MB unpacked; dependencies include `codemirror`, `@codemirror/view`, `katex`, `dompurify`, `remark-math` and, notably, `vue` — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@milkdown/crepe/latest). Latest release v7.22.3 published 2026-10-10 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/Milkdown/milkdown/releases/latest). Milkdown is "built on ProseMirror and remark", Typora-inspired, MIT — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Milkdown/milkdown/main/README.md). The Crepe package README only says it is "The crêpe editor of milkdown" (MIT) and defers to milkdown.dev — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Milkdown/milkdown/main/packages/crepe/README.md).
- **TipTap v3**: `@tiptap/core` 3.32.0, MIT, 3.3 MB unpacked — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@tiptap/core/latest); v3.32.0 published 2026-10-13 (adds `schemaSpec`, fixes Markdown link escaping) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/ueberdosis/tiptap/releases/latest). Official `@tiptap/markdown` 3.32.0 is MIT, 486 KB, single dependency `marked` — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@tiptap/markdown/latest). Docs call it an "early release" that "may have edge cases"; it uses the MarkedJS lexer, supports CommonMark, tables (one child node per cell), HTML embedded in Markdown via Tiptap's HTML parser, custom tokenizers; comments are not supported and may be lost when replacing content with Markdown — [Source](https://tiptap.dev/docs/editor/markdown). It first appeared in the 3.7.0 release notes as "a new official extension ... using MarkedJS" — [Source](https://newreleases.io/project/npm/@tiptap/pm/release/3.7.0). The older community `tiptap-markdown` README says Tiptap now has its own solution and the maintainer does not plan further work — [Source](https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/tiptap-markdown@0.9.0/README.md). Pricing page: the editor itself is "MIT open source"; paid plans Start $59/mo, Team $179/mo, Business $1,199/mo cover platform features: collaboration, inline AI, DOCX and Markdown import, multi-format export, document history, comments, cloud docs; extra dev licences $49/dev/mo — [Source](https://tiptap.dev/pricing).
- **Lexical (Meta)**: `lexical` 0.52.0, MIT, 3.2 MB unpacked — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/lexical/latest); v0.52.0 published 2026-09-28, described as bundling "a week of updates toward Lexical 1.0" with a unified `@lexical/mdast` configuration, headless editor support and several breaking changes — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/facebook/lexical/releases/latest). `@lexical/markdown` 0.52.0, MIT, 296 KB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@lexical/markdown/latest); it exposes `$convertToMarkdownString`/`$convertFromMarkdownString` with element, text-format and text-match transformers; built-ins cover lists, headings, quotes, code blocks, bold/italic/strike/inline code and links; no built-in table transformer is listed — [Source](https://lexical.dev/docs/packages/lexical-markdown). `@lexical/mdast` is an experimental micromark/mdast-based alternative: CommonMark + GFM tables/strikethrough/task lists, raw HTML imported as literal text by default (opt-in `MdastHtmlExtension`), preserves original bullet/fence style so re-serialised output "differs minimally from the source"; docs recommend `@lexical/markdown` for production — [Source](https://lexical.dev/docs/packages/lexical-mdast).
- **BlockNote**: `@blocknote/core` 0.56.0, MPL-2.0, 11.0 MB unpacked, built on ProseMirror + Tiptap packages — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@blocknote/core/latest). `@blocknote/xl-multi-column` 0.56.0 has license field `GPL-3.0 OR PROPRIETARY` — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@blocknote/xl-multi-column/latest). README: "The majority of BlockNote is licensed under the MPL-2.0 license"; XL packages (`packages/xl-*`, npm `@blocknote/xl-*`) "are licensed under the GPL-3.0"; otherwise "you'll need a commercial license" — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/TypeCellOS/BlockNote/main/README.md). v0.56.0 published 2026-10-14 with breaking changes (side menu, Mod+A, import paths, versioning API) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/TypeCellOS/BlockNote/releases/latest). Markdown export is literally named `blocksToMarkdownLossy`; docs: export "is considered 'lossy'", "children of blocks which aren't list items are un-nested" and "certain styles are removed"; JSON is recommended for storage — [Source](https://www.blocknotejs.org/docs/features/export/markdown).
- **CodeMirror 6**: `@codemirror/view` 6.43.14, MIT, 1.26 MB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@codemirror/view/latest); `@codemirror/lang-markdown` 6.5.2, MIT, 72.6 KB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@codemirror/lang-markdown/latest). lang-markdown provides `markdown()` with per-fence code languages, GFM (+ sub/superscript, emoji) via `markdownLanguage`, strict `commonmarkLanguage`, `insertNewlineContinueMarkup`/`deleteMarkupBackward` keymap and `pasteURLAsLink`; it does no rendering/preview — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/codemirror/lang-markdown/main/README.md). Obsidian "uses CodeMirror 6 (CM6) to power the Markdown editor" and Live Preview changes are done through CM6 editor extensions (view plugins, state fields) — [Source](https://docs.obsidian.md/Plugins/Editor/Editor+extensions). `ink-mde` is an MIT CM6-based Markdown editor with live-preview styling (304 stars, pushed 2026-07-31) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/davidmyersdev/ink-mde).
- **Novel**: `novel` 1.0.2, Apache-2.0, pinned to `@tiptap/*` `^2.11.2` (TipTap v2) — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/novel/latest); repository last pushed 2025-01-18, 118 open issues, 16,449 stars — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/steven-tey/novel).
- **MDXEditor**: `@mdxeditor/editor` 4.3.3, MIT, 636 KB; dependencies include `lexical`, `@lexical/*`, `codemirror`, `@codemirror/view`, `mdast-util-mdx`, `unidiff`, `js-yaml` — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdxeditor/editor/latest); v4.3.3 published 2026-10-13 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/mdx-editor/editor/releases/latest). `diffSourcePlugin` provides rich-text, source and diff modes via `viewMode`, `diffMarkdown`, `readOnlyDiff`, toggled by the `DiffSourceToggleWrapper` toolbar component — [Source](https://mdxeditor.dev/editor/docs/diff-source).
- **Plate**: GitHub latest release v53.3.18 published 2026-10-18 (Slate dependency bumps; markdown now preserves unknown HTML and reference links) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/udecode/plate/releases/latest); the legacy `@udecode/plate` npm package is at 49.0.0, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@udecode/plate/latest). `@platejs/markdown` runs a unified/remark pipeline with `remark-gfm` (tables, strikethrough, task lists, footnotes with matching plugins), `remark-math`, and `remark-mdx` for custom elements; raw HTML is not processed by default and is kept as `rawHtml` text written back unchanged; there is no rehype stage, so sanitize HTML before deserialising — [Source](https://platejs.org/docs/markdown).
- **Yoopta**: `@yoopta/editor` 6.0.5, MIT, 348 KB, peer `slate ^0.120` — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@yoopta/editor/latest).

### Inferences
- Round-trip losses cluster by parser family: remark/mdast editors (Milkdown, Plate, Lexical-mdast, MDXEditor) keep raw HTML blocks as opaque text and normalise list markers/whitespace; TipTap's marked-based path parses HTML into schema nodes and drops anything the schema lacks (its docs already admit comments are lost); BlockNote is explicitly lossy. Frontmatter survives only where a plugin models it (MDXEditor bundles `js-yaml`, implying a frontmatter node); footnotes are supported by Plate via `remark-gfm` + footnote plugins and by nothing else above out of the box; tables are the weakest in stock `@lexical/markdown` (no table transformer).
- Cleanest built-in source toggle: MDXEditor (rich/source/diff). Close second for DIY: Milkdown Crepe, because it already embeds CodeMirror for code blocks and is remark-native, so a split view can reuse the same remark pipeline. TipTap/BlockNote give "WYSIWYG-first" ergonomics but need a separate CodeMirror pane and will reformat the source on every toggle.
- For arbitrary user files (not app-authored documents), keep the raw Markdown text as the canonical store and treat WYSIWYG as a view: that avoids silently rewriting users' files.
- Crepe pulling in `vue` is extra weight in a React bundle; measure before adopting.
- Novel is effectively unmaintained (no push since Jan 2025, pinned to TipTap v2) and should not be the base for new work.

### Gaps
- No measured shipped bundle sizes (gzip) for any editor; only npm unpacked sizes were available.
- Could not open the Milkdown "Using Crepe" docs (SPA shell only) to confirm Crepe's feature-flag list or whether it has any built-in source mode; treated as absent.
- TipTap's pricing page does not enumerate "Pro extensions"; whether any extensions beyond the listed platform features are paywalled was not verified.
- Lexical's roadmap/date for 1.0 not verified beyond the release note wording.

## Editors & viewers — Spreadsheet viewing/editing

### Takeaway
View-only: parse with SheetJS CE 0.20.3 (install from cdn.sheetjs.com, never npm's 0.18.5) or ExcelJS, render with TanStack Table (React 19-safe) or AG Grid Community. Edit path: Univer (Apache-2.0 core, formulas included, React 18/19) is the only actively maintained permissive engine; Handsontable (proprietary, key required even for non-commercial) and HyperFormula (GPL-3.0) are licence traps for a self-hosted product.

### Cited Findings
- **SheetJS**: npm `xlsx` latest is 0.18.5, Apache-2.0, with no deprecation notice — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/xlsx/latest). CVE-2023-30533 (prototype pollution, <0.19.3, High 7.8): "A non-vulnerable version cannot be found via npm" — [Source](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-4r6h-8v6p-xvw6). CVE-2024-22363 (ReDoS, <0.20.2, High 7.5), fix only on the SheetJS CDN — [Source](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-5pgg-2g8v-p4x9). Current CE is 0.20.3, installed via `npm i https://cdn.sheetjs.com/xlsx-0.20.3/xlsx-0.20.3.tgz`; docs state the public registry "is out of date" at 0.18.5 — [Source](https://docs.sheetjs.com/docs/getting-started/installation/nodejs/). GitHub mirror last pushed 2024-04-18 and points to git.sheetjs.com as the new home — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/SheetJS/sheetjs).
- **Univer**: `@univerjs/presets` 1.1.0, Apache-2.0 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@univerjs/presets/latest); v1.1.0 published 2026-10-17 (iterative calculation, 3D charts, breaking changes incl. removal of Node.js core presets) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/dream-num/univer/releases/latest). README: Apache-2.0 (DreamNum); open source includes core SDK, rendering and formula engines, Facade API, sheets features (formulas, number formatting, filtering, sorting, data validation, conditional formatting, comments, find/replace, tables); Pro/commercial covers real-time collaboration, edit history, import/export, printing, charts, pivot tables, sparklines, shapes, data connectors, server-side calculation; React 18 and 19 supported; targets include Electron; `Intl.Segmenter` may need a polyfill — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/dream-num/univer/dev/README.md). Install docs: preset mode vs plugin mode, React and RxJS are peer dependencies, formula plugins `UniverFormulaEnginePlugin`/`UniverSheetsFormulaPlugin`, all `@univerjs/*` versions must match, mobile support experimental — [Source](https://docs.univer.ai/en-US/guides/sheets/getting-started/installation).
- **Handsontable**: `handsontable` 18.1.2, license field "SEE LICENSE IN LICENSE.txt", 34.6 MB unpacked — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/handsontable/latest); 18.1.2 published 2026-10-07 (licence-key validation changes) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/handsontable/handsontable/releases/latest).
