Licensing docs: proprietary Non-Commercial licence (research, personal study, trial; in a commercial setting only evaluation/dev/testing, "you can't enter the production stage if your project is in any way connected with your commercial activity"), commercial licence via sales; a `licenseKey` is required even for non-commercial/open-source use; last MIT release was 6.2.2 (2018-12-19) — [Source](https://handsontable.com/docs/javascript-data-grid/software-license/).
- **HyperFormula**: 3.5.0, `GPL-3.0-only`, 14.1 MB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/hyperformula/latest); key `'gpl-v3'` or a purchased proprietary key, validated locally, partial keys yield `#LIC!` — [Source](https://handsontable.com/docs/hyperformula/guide/license-key.html).
- **x-spreadsheet**: `x-data-spreadsheet` 1.1.9, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/x-data-spreadsheet/latest); repo description says "migrated to @wolf-table/table", last push 2024-08-07, 412 open issues — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/myliang/x-spreadsheet); successor `@wolf-table/table` is at 0.0.3, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@wolf-table/table/latest).
- **Fortune-sheet**: `@fortune-sheet/react` 1.0.4, MIT, 8.2 MB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@fortune-sheet/react/latest); repo pushed 2025-12-15, 96 open issues — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/ruilisi/fortune-sheet).
- **Jspreadsheet CE**: `jspreadsheet-ce` 5.0.4 (no license field in package.json) — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/jspreadsheet-ce/latest); GitHub licence MIT, pushed 2026-10-14 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/jspreadsheet/ce).
- **Glide Data Grid**: 6.0.3, MIT; peer deps `react ^16.12 || 17.x || 18.x` (no React 19), plus `lodash`, `marked ^4`, `react-responsive-carousel` — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@glideapps/glide-data-grid/latest); v6.0.3 released 2024-02-03 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/glideapps/glide-data-grid/releases/latest); repo pushed 2026-01-21 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/glideapps/glide-data-grid).
- **AG Grid**: `ag-grid-community` 36.2.0, MIT, 21.6 MB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/ag-grid-community/latest); Enterprise $999/developer; Enterprise-only features include Excel export, clipboard operations, cell range selection, fill handle, set/multi/advanced filters, row grouping, pivoting, master-detail, formulas — [Source](https://www.ag-grid.com/license-pricing/).
- **TanStack Table**: `@tanstack/react-table` 9.3.1, MIT, 122 KB, headless — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@tanstack/react-table/latest).
- **ExcelJS**: 4.4.0, MIT, 21.8 MB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/exceljs/latest); repo pushed 2025-01-21 with 817 open issues — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/exceljs/exceljs).

### Inferences
- View-only path: SheetJS CE 0.20.3 (CDN tarball pinned in package.json) → `sheet_to_json` → TanStack Table + a virtualizer for large sheets; AG Grid Community if you want built-in column menus and sorting. Glide Data Grid is canvas-fast but its React ≤18 peer range makes it a risk on React 19.
- Edit path: Univer presets for a real spreadsheet (formulas, conditional formatting) under Apache-2.0; because xlsx import/export is a Pro feature, bridge files yourself with SheetJS/ExcelJS into Univer's workbook snapshot. Fortune-sheet is the fallback if Univer is too heavy, but its release cadence is slow.
- Avoid Handsontable/HyperFormula in a product that could ever be monetised; the key requirement also phones nothing home but blocks the grid when absent.
- Pin SheetJS via the CDN tarball URL; npm `xlsx` 0.18.5 will keep tripping `npm audit`.

### Gaps
- Univer bundle size: README and install docs give no figures.
- SheetJS Pro pricing/feature list not fetched (sheetjs.com commercial pages not read).
- Handsontable commercial price not listed on the docs page (sales-only).
- Formula coverage counts for Univer and Fortune-sheet not verified.

## Editors & viewers — PDF

### Takeaway
PDF.js is now on the 6.x line (pdfjs-dist 6.4.299, Apache-2.0) with react-pdf 11 requiring React 19 and modern browsers; it remains the default for a web viewer. EmbedPDF (Apache-2.0 core, PDFium fork; v2 stable, v3 unstable) is the credible permissive PDFium-in-WASM option with annotations/redaction; in a native macOS shell, Apple PDFKit is free and already installed.

### Cited Findings
- **pdfjs-dist**: 6.4.299, Apache-2.0, 34.9 MB unpacked, `engines.node >=22.13.0 || >=24` — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/pdfjs-dist/latest); v6.4.299 published 2026-10-03 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/mozilla/pdf.js/releases/latest).
- **react-pdf**: 11.0.0, MIT, pins `pdfjs-dist` 6.3.289 exactly — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/react-pdf/latest); v11 (2026-09-10) adds Suspense/Error Boundary support, upgrades to PDF.js 6, requires React 19 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/wojtekmaj/react-pdf/releases/latest). README: set `pdfjs.GlobalWorkerOptions.workerSrc = new URL('pdfjs-dist/build/pdf.worker.min.mjs', import.meta.url).toString()` in the same module as `<Document>`; import `react-pdf/dist/Page/AnnotationLayer.css` and `TextLayer.css`; minimum Chrome 125 / Safari 18, Node 22.13 — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/wojtekmaj/react-pdf/main/packages/react-pdf/README.md).
- **EmbedPDF**: `@embedpdf/core` 2.15.1, npm license MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@embedpdf/core/latest); repo created 2025-01-18, 4,561 stars, pushed 2026-10-18, GitHub licence shows NOASSERTION — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/embedpdf/embed-pdf-viewer). LICENSE file is Apache-2.0 with a repository licensing notice excluding `cloudpdf/server` — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/embedpdf/embed-pdf-viewer/main/LICENSE). README: engine is "EmbedPDF Runtime, a fork of Google's PDFium"; React/Vue/Svelte/Preact/vanilla; annotations, true redaction, search, virtualised scrolling; `cloudpdf/server` is FCL-1.0-ALv2 (needs a CloudPDF licence, converts to Apache-2.0 two years after each release); v3 "under active development and not recommended for production", stable is the `v2` branch — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/embedpdf/embed-pdf-viewer/main/README.md).
- **@hyzyla/pdfium**: 3.0.0, MIT, 14.1 MB, "Universal wrapper for PDFium" — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@hyzyla/pdfium/latest).
- **pdfium-render (Rust)**: 0.9.4, MIT OR Apache-2.0, updated 2026-09-06, 3.3 M downloads — [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/pdfium-render).
- **Nutrient Web SDK (ex-PSPDFKit)**: client-side WebAssembly rendering on a PDFium engine; 17+ annotation types, forms, signatures, text editing, redaction, Office files rendered in-browser; sold as annual subscription via Sales or self-serve API key with a free tier (250 viewer sessions/month); Starter/Growth prices not disclosed — [Source](https://www.nutrient.io/sdk/web/).
- **pdf-lib**: 1.17.1, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/pdf-lib/latest); repo last pushed 2024-07-17 with 318 open issues — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/Hopding/pdf-lib); maintained fork `@cantoo/pdf-lib` 2.11.1, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@cantoo/pdf-lib/latest).
- **Apple PDFKit**: "Display and manipulate PDF documents in your apps"; iOS/iPadOS 11.0+, Mac Catalyst 11.0+, macOS 10.4+, visionOS 1.0+ — [Source](https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/pdfkit.json).

### Inferences
- The brief's "pdfjs-dist 5.x" is out of date; budget for the 6.x API changes (react-pdf's upgrade guide lists them) and for the Chrome 125/Safari 18 floor, which matters if the desktop shell embeds an older WebView.
- Self-host the worker file (import.meta.url pattern) rather than unpkg; a self-hosted desktop app should not depend on a CDN at runtime.
- EmbedPDF v2 is the pragmatic choice when you need annotations/redaction without a commercial SDK; keep the CloudPDF server out of scope (fair-source licence).
- For editing (merge/split/fill forms) use `@cantoo/pdf-lib` rather than the stale upstream.
- In a Swift/AppKit shell, PDFKit's `PDFView` gives search, annotations and text selection for free; a Tauri/Electron shell still needs PDF.js in the web layer.

### Gaps
- Apryse WebViewer licensing page returned 404; terms (trial watermark, pricing) unverified.
- pdf.js text-layer/annotation-layer API specifics were only read via react-pdf's README, not pdf.js's own docs.
- No measured WASM payload sizes for EmbedPDF or @hyzyla/pdfium.

## Editors & viewers — DOCX/PPTX preview

### Takeaway
In-browser: mammoth (BSD-2) for semantic DOCX→HTML and docx-preview (Apache-2.0, active) for layout-faithful DOCX; there is no maintained permissive PPTX renderer in JS, so route PPTX (and fidelity-critical DOCX) through LibreOffice headless, ideally via Gotenberg (MIT, Docker) or, in a macOS shell, QuickLook (`qlmanage -t/-p -o`). Full in-browser editing means Collabora CODE (MPL-2.0) or ONLYOFFICE (AGPL-3.0, flag).

### Cited Findings
- **mammoth.js**: 1.13.0, BSD-2-Clause, "Convert Word documents from docx to simple HTML and Markdown" — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/mammoth/latest); repo pushed 2026-10-16, 6,325 stars — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/mwilliamson/mammoth.js).
- **docx-preview**: 0.4.1, Apache-2.0, 978 KB, depends on `jszip` — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/docx-preview/latest); repo `VolodymyrBaydalka/docxjs` pushed 2026-09-21, 2,122 stars, 84 open issues — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/VolodymyrBaydalka/docxjs).
- **LibreOffice headless**: `--headless` runs without UI; `--convert-to OutputFileExtension[:OutputFilterName[:OutputFilterParams]] [--outdir dir]`, e.g. `--convert-to pdf *.doc`, `--convert-to "html:XHTML Writer File:UTF8"` — [Source](https://help.libreoffice.org/latest/en-US/text/shared/guide/start_parameters.html). Homebrew cask `libreoffice` is at 26.8.1 (`brew install --cask libreoffice`) — [Source](https://formulae.brew.sh/cask/libreoffice).
- **Gotenberg**: MIT, 13,340 stars, pushed 2026-10-16 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/gotenberg/gotenberg); v8.38.1 published 2026-10-15 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/gotenberg/gotenberg/releases/latest); Docker image `gotenberg/gotenberg:8` (`docker run --rm -p 3000:3000 gotenberg/gotenberg:8`), Chromium module for HTML/URL/Markdown→PDF, LibreOffice module for "100+ formats" → PDF, plus merge/split/flatten/PDF-A tools — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/gotenberg/gotenberg/main/README.md).
- **Collabora Online (CODE)**: `COPYING` is MPL-2.0 ("This Source Code Form is subject to the terms of the Mozilla Public...") — [Source](https://github.com/CollaboraOnline/online/blob/master/COPYING); GitHub repo is an issue tracker with development on Gerrit, pushed 2026-10-09 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/CollaboraOnline/online).
- **ONLYOFFICE Document Server**: AGPL-3.0, 7,032 stars, pushed 2026-10-16 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/ONLYOFFICE/DocumentServer).
- **PPTX renderers**: `pptx-preview` 1.0.7, ISC, created 2024-10-15, last published 2025-10-17, no repository or homepage field, author listed as a WeChat handle — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/pptx-preview). PPTXjs: MIT jQuery plugin, 278 stars, last push 2022-03-26 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/meshesha/PPTXjs). PPTX2HTML: MIT, 637 stars, last push 2017-02-03 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/g21589/PPTX2HTML).
- **Nutrient (commercial)** renders Word/Excel/PowerPoint in the browser and converts them to PDF client-side — [Source](https://www.nutrient.io/sdk/web/).
- **qlmanage**: `-p` renders Quick Look previews, `-t` thumbnails with `-s size` and `-f factor`, `-c contentTypeUTI`, `-g generator`, `-x` uses quicklookd — [Source](https://keith.github.io/xcode-man-pages/qlmanage.1.html); local `qlmanage -h` on this macOS 27 machine additionally lists `-o dir` ("Output result in dir (don't display thumbnails or previews)") and `-r cache` (verified locally; no URL).
- **Pandoc**: GitHub licence detected as GPL-2.0, 46,627 stars, pushed 2026-10-18 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/jgm/pandoc); 3.12.1 released 2026-10-08 (adds flat ODT I/O) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/jgm/pandoc/releases/latest).

### Inferences
- DOCX: show docx-preview for "looks like Word" and offer mammoth output when the agent needs clean HTML/Markdown to reason over; both run client-side with no server.
- PPTX: none of the JS renderers is trustworthy (two are dead, one is anonymous with no repo); convert PPTX→PDF with LibreOffice/Gotenberg and reuse the PDF viewer, or on macOS call `qlmanage -t -s 1600 -o <dir>` for per-file thumbnails and `-p` for a full preview.
- Shipping LibreOffice inside a desktop app is heavy (the cask is a full office suite); prefer detecting an existing install (`/Applications/LibreOffice.app/Contents/MacOS/soffice`) or an optional Docker/Gotenberg sidecar. Neither soffice nor pandoc was present on this dev machine.
- Collabora CODE (MPL-2.0) is the licence-safe editor server; ONLYOFFICE's AGPL-3.0 would obligate source disclosure of a network-served combined work and should be flagged.
- Pandoc being GPL is fine when invoked as a separate process (not linked), which is the normal use.

### Gaps
- Microsoft Office Online viewer constraints (`view.officeapps.live.com/op/embed.aspx?src=` needing a publicly reachable URL) were not verified from a Microsoft page this session; treat as unverified but it is inapplicable to local files regardless.
- Gotenberg's routes page returned 404; the exact LibreOffice route path and non-PDF output support were not confirmed.
- CODE (the free Collabora Online developer edition) connection limits were not verified.

## Editors & viewers — Image / CSV / JSON / code viewers

### Takeaway
CodeMirror 6 is the right default code/CSV/JSON editor for a desktop-shipped React app (MIT, ~1.3 MB unpacked core, no worker setup); reserve Monaco (0.57.0, MIT, 101 MB on disk, workers that fail on `file://`) for a VS Code-like experience. Shiki is now v4 with a JS RegExp engine that avoids the WASM download; Streamdown (Apache-2.0) is the purpose-built streaming Markdown renderer with sanitisation baked in.

### Cited Findings
- **Monaco**: `monaco-editor` 0.57.0, MIT, 101.7 MB unpacked — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/monaco-editor/latest); v0.57.0 published 2026-09-24 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/microsoft/monaco-editor/releases/latest). README: ESM build for bundlers, AMD "deprecated and will be removed", language services run in web workers, workers fail on `file://` pages, no mobile browser support — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/monaco-editor/main/README.md).
- **CodeMirror 6**: `@codemirror/view` 6.43.14, MIT, 1.26 MB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@codemirror/view/latest).
- **Shiki**: 4.5.0, MIT, 602 KB (core package) — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/shiki/latest); v4.5.0 published 2026-10-01, adds `transformerRenderLineNumber`, `codeToANSI` colour levels, streaming fixes — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/shikijs/shiki/releases/latest). Bundles: full 6.4 MB min / 1.2 MB gzip; web 3.8 MB / 695 KB gzip; fine-grained `createHighlighterCore` from `shiki/core` with explicit themes/langs — [Source](https://shiki.style/guide/bundles). Engines: Oniguruma WASM (max compatibility) vs `createJavaScriptRegexEngine` (no WASM download, native regex; all built-in languages supported as of 3.9.1; `forgiving` option) — recommended for browsers — [Source](https://shiki.style/guide/regex-engines).
- **react-markdown**: 10.1.0, MIT, 52.6 KB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/react-markdown/latest); README security: raw HTML is escaped/ignored by default, `rehype-raw` only for trusted content (+~60 KB), `defaultUrlTransform` allows http/https/irc/ircs/mailto/xmpp/relative, `rehype-sanitize` recommended; ESM-only — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/remarkjs/react-markdown/main/readme.md).
- **Streamdown (Vercel)**: 2.7.0, Apache-2.0, 124 KB; dependencies include `rehype-harden`, `rehype-sanitize`, `rehype-raw`, `remark-gfm`, `marked`, `remend`; peers React 18/19 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/streamdown/latest); v2.7.0 (2026-09-30) adds incremental code highlighting and a bounded highlight cache; `@streamdown/code` 2.0.0 requires Node 20+ — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/vercel/streamdown/releases/latest); README: "drop-in replacement for react-markdown" for AI streaming, "Security-first" via `rehype-harden`, plugins `@streamdown/code` (Shiki), `@streamdown/mermaid`, `@streamdown/math` (KaTeX), `@streamdown/cjk`, `remend` for unterminated blocks — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/vercel/streamdown/main/packages/streamdown/README.md).
- **Mermaid**: 12.2.0, MIT, 122 MB unpacked — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/mermaid/latest); release tagged 2026-10-13 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/mermaid-js/mermaid/releases/latest). `securityLevel`: `strict` (default, HTML encoded, clicks off), `antiscript`, `loose`, `sandbox` (renders in a sandboxed iframe, "prevents any JavaScript from running", breaks interactivity, noted as beta) — [Source](https://mermaid.js.org/config/usage.html).
- **KaTeX**: 0.19.0, MIT, 4.0 MB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/katex/latest); security page: output "should be safe" but sanitise anyway (SVG/MathML need a generous allowlist); `trust` gates `\includegraphics`/`\htmlClass`; `maxSize`, `maxExpand` limit abuse; error messages may contain unescaped source — [Source](https://katex.org/docs/security).
- **JSON viewers**: `@textea/json-viewer` 4.0.1, MIT, but peer-depends on `@mui/material ^6` and Emotion — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@textea/json-viewer/latest); `@microlink/react-json-view` 1.31.40, MIT, 147 KB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@microlink/react-json-view/latest); `@uiw/react-json-view` 2.0.0-alpha.44, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@uiw/react-json-view/latest); `react-json-tree` 0.20.0, MIT, 63 KB, React 16–19 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/react-json-tree/latest).
- **CSV**: PapaParse 5.7.0, MIT, 271 KB, web workers and streaming for large files — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/papaparse/latest); TanStack Table 9.3.1 MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@tanstack/react-table/latest); Glide Data Grid 6.0.3 MIT (React ≤18 peers) — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@glideapps/glide-data-grid/latest).
- **Images**: `react-zoom-pan-pinch` 4.2.0, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/react-zoom-pan-pinch/latest); `yet-another-react-lightbox` 3.32.2, MIT, 241 KB — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/yet-another-react-lightbox/latest).
- **Audio/video**: `media-chrome` 4.19.3, MIT, web-component player controls — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/media-chrome/latest).

### Inferences
- Monaco in a desktop shell: serve the renderer over `http(s)://` or a custom scheme, not `file://`, or its language workers will not start; CodeMirror has no such constraint.
- Use Shiki with the JavaScript engine and a fine-grained bundle (a dozen languages, two themes) to stay well under the 695 KB gzip "web" bundle; both Streamdown's code plugin and MDXEditor/Crepe already embed CodeMirror/Shiki pieces, so dedupe.
- Render Mermaid with `securityLevel: 'sandbox'` or inside your own sandboxed iframe; even `strict` still executes Mermaid's parser on untrusted text in the main document.
- Prefer `react-json-tree` or `@microlink/react-json-view` over `@textea/json-viewer` unless you already ship MUI.
- CSV: PapaParse worker mode + TanStack Table with row virtualisation handles multi-100k-row files; Glide Data Grid is faster but check React 19 compatibility first.
- Native `<img>`/`<audio>`/`<video>` elements cover most needs; add react-zoom-pan-pinch only for zoomable images.

### Gaps
- No measured runtime bundle for Monaco (unpacked 101 MB is not the shipped size).
- Streamdown's exact `rehype-harden` allowlist (image/link prefixes) was not visible in the README fetched.
- Mermaid sandbox mode's current beta status was stated in docs; not re-verified against the 12.x changelog.

## Editors & viewers — Rendering "artifacts" (HTML, React, email previews) safely

### Takeaway
Render untrusted HTML/React output in an `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts" srcdoc=...>` (never add `allow-same-origin`), served from a second origin with a strict CSP, resized through your own ResizeObserver+postMessage handshake rather than iframe-resizer (GPL-3.0/commercial). For React artifacts, Sandpack (Apache-2.0) with a self-hosted bundler or an import-map/esm.sh page is licence-clean; WebContainers require a commercial licence in production, and E2B's self-host path needs Linux/KVM, so for a personal desktop app treat server-side sandboxes as optional.

### Cited Findings
- **iframe sandbox**: empty `sandbox` applies all restrictions; tokens such as `allow-scripts`, `allow-forms`, `allow-popups`, `allow-same-origin`, `allow-downloads`; MDN warns that for same-origin content using both `allow-scripts` and `allow-same-origin` "lets the embedded document remove the sandbox attribute"; `srcdoc` documents have location `about:srcdoc`; the `csp` attribute enforces a policy on the embedded resource — [Source](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/iframe).
- **CSP delivery**: a policy can be set with `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy" content="...">`; the `sandbox` directive "Enables a sandbox for the requested resource similar to the `<iframe>` sandbox attribute"; scheme sources like `https:` are written with a trailing colon — [Source](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Content-Security-Policy). `img-src` accepts host-source, scheme-source and `'self'`, or `'none'`; falls back to `default-src` — [Source](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Content-Security-Policy/img-src).
- **Claude Artifacts (public observation)**: artifacts load from `www.claudeusercontent.com`; CSP allows scripts from that host, `cdnjs.cloudflare.com` and the Pyodide path on `cdn.jsdelivr.net` (inline/eval permitted), styles from cdnjs and `fonts.googleapis.com`, `connect-src` only the Pyodide jsdelivr path, `object-src` disabled, `frame-ancestors` limited to claude.ai domains; consequence: artifacts "can't make API requests to external hosts directly" — [Source](https://simonwillison.net/2024/Oct/21/claude-artifacts/). Anthropic's help article adds that artifacts may use connected apps with first-use approval, store up to 20 MB of text, and warns to treat code in others' published artifacts "like a file from an unknown sender" — [Source](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/9487310-what-are-artifacts-and-how-do-i-use-them).
- **iframe-resizer**: 5.5.9, license `GPL-3.0`, funding link to iframe-resizer.com/pricing — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/iframe-resizer/latest); v5.5.9 published 2026-02-06 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/davidjbradshaw/iframe-resizer/releases/latest). Pricing: free only under GPL v3 (project source must be public and GPL); one-time commercial tiers Solo $16 (same-origin only), Professional $76 (cross-origin, ≤8 devs/sites), Business $486 (multi-origin, mobile apps, widgets), OEM/SaaS custom for distributed products — [Source](https://iframe-resizer.com/pricing/).
- **DOMPurify**: 3.4.17, `(MPL-2.0 OR Apache-2.0)`, HTML/MathML/SVG sanitiser — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/dompurify/latest); 3.4.17 published 2026-10-12 (IN_PLACE, SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES, shadow-root fixes) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/cure53/DOMPurify/releases/latest).
- **CSS inlining**: `juice` 12.2.0, MIT, "Inlines css into html source" — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/juice/latest).
- **Sandpack**: `@codesandbox/sandpack-react` 2.20.0, Apache-2.0, 1.2 MB, React 16.8–19 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@codesandbox/sandpack-react/latest); v2.20.0 published 2025-02-14 (React 19 support) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/codesandbox/sandpack/releases/latest). Self-hosting: the bundler "runs code in an iframe on a separate subdomain" to protect host cookies; build via `codesandbox-client` (`yarn build:deps`, `yarn build:sandpack` → static `www`), then pass `bundlerURL`; transpilers load on demand in Web Workers and a Service Worker enables offline reuse — [Source](https://sandpack.codesandbox.io/docs/guides/hosting-the-bundler).
- **esm.sh**: "A no-build JavaScript CDN", MIT, 4,182 stars, pushed 2026-10-19 (self-hostable server) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/esm-dev/esm.sh).
- **WebContainers**: `@webcontainer/api` 1.6.4 npm wrapper is MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@webcontainer/api/latest); but "Licensing is required for production usage of the API in a commercial, for-profit setting"; "Prototypes or POCs do not require a commercial license"; pricing by contact — [Source](https://webcontainers.io/enterprise); the README notes deployed pages must be served over HTTPS — [Source](https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@webcontainer/api@1.6.4/README.md).
- **E2B**: `e2b` SDK 2.56.0, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/e2b/latest); runtime repo (`e2b-dev/runtime`, formerly `infra`) is Apache-2.0, 1,742 stars, pushed 2026-10-18 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/e2b-dev/infra); README: Go orchestration of Firecracker microVMs; self-host as "E2B Embed" on one Linux host with KVM via Docker Compose, or Terraform for GCP/AWS/Azure and a Kubernetes manifest; multi-node is enterprise — [Source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/e2b-dev/runtime/main/README.md).
- **Vercel Sandbox**: Firecracker microVMs, `@vercel/sandbox` JS SDK plus Python SDK and CLI, OIDC or access-token auth, default image with Node LTS and Python 3.14, linked blog "now generally available" — [Source](https://vercel.com/docs/vercel-sandbox). Pricing: Hobby 5 Active-CPU hours/month, 45-minute max session, 10 concurrent; Pro $0.128/Active-CPU-hour + $0.0212/GB-hour memory, 24-hour sessions, up to 8 vCPU/16 GB — [Source](https://vercel.com/docs/sandbox/pricing).

### Inferences
- Minimum viable HTML artifact sandbox: `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts allow-forms allow-popups allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox" srcdoc>` with a `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy">` injected at the top of the srcdoc (`default-src 'none'; script-src 'unsafe-inline' https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com; style-src 'unsafe-inline' https://fonts.googleapis.com; img-src data: blob: https:; connect-src 'none'`). Because `frame-ancestors`, `report-uri` and `sandbox` are ignored in `<meta>` policies (CSP spec; not re-verified this session), rely on the iframe `sandbox` attribute for those.
- Mirror Claude's two-origin design: serve artifact documents from a second origin (a different localhost port, a `*.localhost` host, or a dedicated custom scheme in Electron/Tauri) so even a sandbox escape lands on an origin with no app cookies/IndexedDB.
- Resize protocol: inside the frame, a tiny inline script posts `{type:'resize', height: document.documentElement.scrollHeight}` from a `ResizeObserver`; the parent validates `event.source === iframe.contentWindow` and sets `style.height`. Omitting `allow-same-origin` makes the frame opaque, so `contentDocument` reads are impossible and postMessage is the only channel; this replaces iframe-resizer and avoids its GPL/commercial terms.
- Email preview: sanitise with DOMPurify (forbid `script`, `iframe`, `object`, `form`, `meta`, `link`; restrict URI schemes), then render in the same sandboxed iframe with `img-src data: cid:` only by default and a "load remote images" affordance that widens `img-src` to `https:` — this blocks tracking pixels until the user opts in. `juice` is for inlining CSS when sending; for viewing, scope styles inside the iframe instead.
- React artifacts: Sandpack with a self-hosted bundler on the second origin is the only option that is fully offline-capable and Apache-2.0; an import-map page loading React from a self-hosted esm.sh instance is lighter but means transpiling JSX yourself (e.g. Sucrase/esbuild-wasm in a worker). WebContainers are out for a product unless licensed; E2B/Vercel Sandbox are server-side and cloud-or-Linux-only, so they fit an optional "run this code" feature rather than the artifact viewer.

### Gaps
- OpenAI's canvas announcement page returned 403; no public description of ChatGPT canvas/HTML preview isolation was verified.
- The `<meta>` CSP directive limitations (`frame-ancestors`, `report-uri`, `sandbox` ignored) come from the CSP spec in memory; the MDN pages fetched did not state them.
- WebContainers' cross-origin-isolation (COOP/COEP) requirement was not read from the README this session.
- E2B self-host hardware requirement (Linux + KVM) implies no native macOS self-host; a Linux VM path was not verified.

Research date: 2026-10-18. Versions/dates below come from the npm registry API, GitHub API, crates.io API and official docs fetched today; web search budget ran out mid-task, so a few items are flagged under Gaps.

## UI kits, theming & shells — 1. Chat UI kits: assistant-ui, CopilotKit, Vercel AI Elements, alternatives, reference chat apps, shadcn/Radix/Base UI/Tailwind status

### Takeaway
assistant-ui (MIT, 0.15.x, published ~weekly) is the most complete headless-plus-shadcn chat kit, with first-class AI SDK v7, LangGraph, AG-UI, A2A and external-store runtimes and an optional cloud; Vercel AI Elements is being folded into shadcn/ui (standalone registry deprecated end of November 2026), and CopilotKit stays MIT but its roadmap centres on the paid "Intelligence" layer. Build on shadcn CLI v4 (Base UI is now the default base, Radix still supported through the unified `radix-ui` package) plus Tailwind v4.3; for whole-app references prefer LibreChat (MIT) or Hugging Face chat-ui (Apache-2.0) over Open WebUI (branding clause) or LobeHub (source-available).

### Cited Findings
**assistant-ui**
- `@assistant-ui/react` latest 0.15.29, MIT, registry modified 2026-10-15 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@assistant-ui/react/latest); companions `@assistant-ui/react-ai-sdk` 1.4.18 and `@assistant-ui/react-langgraph` 0.14.36 published the same day — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@assistant-ui/react-ai-sdk/latest), [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@assistant-ui/react-langgraph/latest)
- Repo: MIT, 12.6k stars, 6,865 commits; runtimes: Vercel AI SDK (`useChatRuntime`), LangGraph/LangChain, AG-UI and A2A, Google ADK, OpenCode, custom data-stream (`useDataStreamRuntime`); generative/tool UI renders tool calls and JSON as React components with inline human approvals and frontend actions; primitives Thread, ThreadList, Message, Composer; optional paid Assistant Cloud (thread history, telemetry, file storage); CLI `npx assistant-ui@latest create` / `init` installs shadcn/ui-styled components — [Source](https://github.com/assistant-ui/assistant-ui); last push 2026-10-19 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/assistant-ui/assistant-ui)
- Runtime picker: framework adapters for AI SDK ("v7 is current; v6, v5, and v4 are legacy"), Eve, LangGraph (`@langchain/langgraph-sdk`), LangChain useStream (`@langchain/react`), Google ADK, A2A v1.0, AG-UI, OpenCode (experimental); core runtimes LocalRuntime, ExternalStoreRuntime (redux/zustand/tanstack-query), DataStream, AssistantTransport (full agent-state snapshots); Mastra/OpenAI Agents SDK/Cloudflare Agents go through the AI SDK runtime, Pydantic AI through AG-UI, Claude Managed Agents through the external store — [Source](https://www.assistant-ui.com/docs/runtimes/pick-a-runtime)
- Assistant Cloud pricing: Free $0 up to 200 MAU (thread persistence/history, thread list with auto titles, user/workspace auth); Pro $50/mo for 500 MAU + $0.10 per extra MAU; Enterprise custom incl. on-prem; library "MIT licensed", works with your own backend and you can "never open a Cloud account" — [Source](https://www.assistant-ui.com/pricing)

**CopilotKit and AG-UI**
- `@copilotkit/react-core` 1.79.1, MIT, modified 2026-10-16 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@copilotkit/react-core/latest); `@ag-ui/core` 1.0.3, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@ag-ui/core/latest)
- Repo MIT, 38.1k stars; CopilotKit is the maker of AG-UI ("Agent–User Interaction Protocol"), "adopted by Google, LangChain, AWS, Microsoft, Mastra, PydanticAI"; React/Next.js GA, Angular/Vue/React Native/Slack/Teams "supported"; commercial "CopilotKit Intelligence" adds rich threads, user memories, automatic learning, product analytics, self-hosting (Kubernetes/VPC) — [Source](https://github.com/CopilotKit/CopilotKit); pushed 2026-10-18 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/CopilotKit/CopilotKit)

The other runtime implementations (Python, Go, Ruby, C#/.NET) require it." — [Source](https://docs.copilotkit.ai/angular/google-adk/intelligence/overview)
- AG-UI: open, event-based protocol on HTTP and WebSockets covering streaming chat, generative UI, shared state, thinking steps, frontend/backend tool calls, human-in-the-loop interrupts, sub-agents; integrations: LangGraph and CrewAI (partners), Microsoft Agent Framework, Google ADK, AWS Strands, Bedrock AgentCore, Mastra, Pydantic AI, Agno, LlamaIndex, AG2 (first-party), Claude Agent SDK and Claude Managed Agents SDK (community); CopilotKit is the first-party client — [Source](https://docs.ag-ui.com/introduction); `ag-ui-protocol/ag-ui` MIT, 16.7k stars — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/ag-ui-protocol/ag-ui)

### Inferences
- assistant-ui is still 0.x (0.15.29) despite near-weekly publishing; expect API churn and pin minor versions. Its AG-UI/DataStream/ExternalStore runtimes make it backend-agnostic, which suits a custom local agent server.
- AI Elements' move into shadcn/ui means Conversation/Message/PromptInput/Reasoning/Tool will live in the shadcn registry; adopt via `npx shadcn add <registry-url>` now and plan to re-point before December 2026. Third-party notes say AI Elements relies on Radix APIs, so `--base radix` is the safer shadcn base if you use it (unverified).
- CopilotKit gating non-TypeScript runtimes behind Intelligence signals a commercial pivot; for a self-hosted personal app the MIT TS runtime plus AG-UI is usable without paying, but assistant-ui is the more neutral choice (and can speak AG-UI).
- Radix Primitives is maintained (push Oct 2026, 1.8.1) but WorkOS-owned with no GitHub releases: "maintained, low visibility". shadcn now defaults to Base UI (MUI team, 1.9.0), which is the lower-risk base for a new project; Radix Colors/Themes remain useful independently of the primitives choice.
- Open WebUI's branding clause only matters if you copy its UI into a rebranded product; LibreChat (MIT) and HF chat-ui (Apache-2.0) are safer code references. The `@lobehub/chat` package.json says "MIT" while its LICENSE file is the Community License: treat the LICENSE file as authoritative.

- Current "Copilot Cloud" naming/pricing: docs now say "CopilotKit Intelligence"; no price list found on fetched pages.
- Web-search budget was exhausted, so the body of shadcn's "Base UI as the Default" entry and the AI Elements/Base UI incompatibility claim were not independently verified.
- Radix Primitives changelog content was not retrieved (Releases tab empty; tags page not fetched).
- Jan v0.6.0 release year is not shown on the release page and the GitHub API returned no record for the redirected repo.

## UI kits, theming & shells — 2. Theming with user-chosen accent colours: OKLCH tokens, Radix Colors, scale generation from a seed, CSS features, contrast, font and density tokens

### Takeaway
Use shadcn's OKLCH CSS-variable theme exposed to Tailwind v4 with `@theme inline` as the token layer, and generate accent and gray 12-step scales at runtime from a user seed with the open Radix generator (colorjs.io + bezier easing, APCA-checked text, P3 variants) or a culori/Leonardo contrast-driven curve; `light-dark()`, `color-mix()` and `@property` are Baseline (2023-2024) and safe for Chrome 111+/Safari 16.4+. Gate accessibility with WCAG 2 contrast (WCAG 3's method is still "to be determined") and use APCA only as a design heuristic given apca-w3's restrictive licence.

### Cited Findings
**Token formats and tooling**
- shadcn theming: semantic background/foreground pairs; tokens background, foreground, card, popover, primary, secondary, muted, accent, destructive, border, input, ring, radius, chart-1..5, sidebar-*; OKLCH values such as `--primary: oklch(0.205 0 0)` and `--destructive: oklch(0.577 0.245 27.325)`; `@theme inline` maps `--color-primary: var(--primary)`; radius scale `--radius-sm: calc(var(--radius) * 0.6)` from `--radius: 0.625rem`; dark mode via `.dark` and `@custom-variant dark (&:is(.dark *))`; base colours Neutral, Stone, Zinc, Mauve, Olive, Mist, Taupe; shadcn/create previews colours, radius, fonts, icons and emits a preset — [Source](https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/theming)
- shadcn dark mode (Vite): ThemeProvider stores "light" | "dark" | "system" in localStorage, sets the class on `document.documentElement`, resolves "system" with `window.matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)")` — [Source](https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/dark-mode/vite)
- Tailwind v4 theme variables: namespaces `--color-*`, `--font-*`, `--text-*`, `--spacing`, `--radius-*`, `--breakpoint-*`, `--shadow-*`, `--font-weight-*`, `--tracking-*`, `--leading-*`, `--ease-*`, `--animate-*`; `@theme inline` is required when a token references another CSS variable; `--color-*: initial` disables defaults; tokens compile to `:root` variables usable at runtime (`var(--color-mint-500)`); `--spacing: 4px` example — [Source](https://tailwindcss.com/docs/theme); Tailwind recommends `color-mix()` for runtime colour adjustment of CSS variables/currentColor — [Source](https://tailwindcss.com/docs/compatibility)
- tweakcn: "visual no-code theme editor for shadcn/ui components" with presets, Tailwind v4, hosted at tweakcn.com, Apache-2.0, 10.5k stars — [Source](https://github.com/jnsahaj/tweakcn); pushed 2026-09-03 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/jnsahaj/tweakcn)

**Radix Colors**
- 12-step semantics: 1-2 app/subtle backgrounds; 3-5 component backgrounds (normal/hover/pressed); 6-8 borders (6 subtle, 7 interactive, 8 strong + focus rings); 9-10 solid (9 normal, 10 hover); 11-12 text (low/high contrast); step 9 has the highest chroma; "Steps 11 and 12 are guaranteed to meet APCA contrast of Lc 60 and Lc 90, respectively, on a step 2 background"; Sky, Mint, Lime, Yellow, Amber step 9 are meant for dark text — [Source](https://www.radix-ui.com/colors/docs/palette-composition/understanding-the-scale)
- 31 scales (Gray, Mauve, Slate, Sage, Olive, Sand, Gold, Bronze, Brown, Yellow, Amber, Orange, Tomato, Red, Ruby, Crimson, Pink, Plum, Purple, Violet, Iris, Indigo, Blue, Cyan, Teal, Jade, Green, Grass, Lime, Mint, Sky), each with base, Alpha, Dark and Dark Alpha versions, plus Black/White alpha overlays — [Source](https://www.radix-ui.com/colors/docs/palette-composition/scales); `@radix-ui/colors` 3.0.0 — [Source](https://www.radix-ui.com/colors/docs/overview/installation); shipped CSS adds P3 overrides inside `@supports (color: color(display-p3 1 1 1)) { @media (color-gamut: p3) { ... --blue-1: color(display-p3 ...) } }` and alpha variables like `--blue-a1` — [Source](https://unpkg.com/@radix-ui/colors@3.0.0/blue.css), [Source](https://unpkg.com/@radix-ui/colors@3.0.0/blue-dark-alpha.css); repo MIT, 1.7k stars, pushed 2025-12-17 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/radix-ui/colors)
- Custom palette tool: inputs Accent, Gray, Background with Light/Dark toggle; outputs 12-step accent and gray scales — [Source](https://www.radix-ui.com/colors/custom)
- The generator algorithm is open (website repo): `generateRadixColors({ appearance, accent, gray, background })` imports `@radix-ui/colors` P3 reference scales, `colorjs.io` (OKLCH, `deltaEOK`, `contrastAPCA`, mixing) and `bezier-easing`; `getScaleFromColor` finds the two nearest reference scales by delta-E OK and blends them; chroma scaled toward the seed (cap 1.5x), hue set to the seed; lightness remapped from the background with `lightModeEasing = [0, 2, 0, 2]` / `darkModeEasing = [1, 0, 1, 0]`; `getStep9Colors` puts the seed at step 9 (falls back if distance to background < 25); `getTextColor` chooses white or dark text with an APCA threshold (< 40); `getButtonHoverColor` derives step 10; chroma of steps 11/12 capped against 8/9; `getAlphaColor`/`getAlphaColorP3` derive alpha scales; returns accent/gray scales plus alpha, wide-gamut, surface and contrast colours — [Source](https://github.com/radix-ui/website/blob/main/components/generate-radix-colors.tsx); website repo MIT, pushed 2026-10-07 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/radix-ui/website)
- Radix Themes: `<Theme accentColor="mint" grayColor="gray" panelBackground="solid" scaling="100%" radius="full">` — [Source](https://www.radix-ui.com/themes/docs/theme/overview); 26 accent options, grays Gray/Mauve/Slate/Sage/Olive/Sand; tokens `--accent-1..12`, `--accent-surface`, `--accent-indicator`, `--accent-track`, `--accent-contrast`, same shape for `--gray-*`; custom palette = paste generated CSS, override variables on `.radix-themes`, brand colour = override step 9 and `-a9` — [Source](https://www.radix-ui.com/themes/docs/theme/color); typography tokens `--default-font-family`, `--heading-font-family`, `--code-font-family`, `--strong/em/quote-font-family`, 9-step type scale (12px/16px line-height up to 60px), `--font-weight-light/regular/medium/bold` — [Source](https://www.radix-ui.com/themes/docs/theme/typography)

**Scale-generation libraries**
- culori v4.0.2 (MIT): modes oklch, oklab, lch, lab, hsl, p3 (`display-p3`), rec2020, prophoto and more; `inGamut`, `displayable`, `clampGamut`, `clampChroma`, `toGamut`; `interpolate` + `samples`; `wcagContrast`/`wcagLuminance` (WCAG 2.0, no APCA); `formatCss`/`formatHex` — [Source](https://culorijs.org/api/); npm 4.0.2 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/culori/latest); pushed 2026-10-15 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/Evercoder/culori)
- Material Color Utilities (Apache-2.0): HCT = "hue, chrome, tone based on CAM16 x L*"; schemes "from a single color or a core palette"; tonal palette = "range of colors that varies only in tone"; TS API `Hct.fromInt`, `argbFromHex`, `themeFromSourceColor(sourceArgb, customColors)`, `applyTheme(theme, { target, dark })` — [Source](https://github.com/material-foundation/material-color-utilities), [Source](https://github.com/material-foundation/material-color-utilities/tree/main/typescript); `dynamiccolor/` contains `color_spec_2021.ts`, `color_spec_2025.ts`, `color_spec_2026.ts`, `dynamic_scheme.ts`, `contrast_curve.ts`, `tone_delta_pair.ts` — [Source](https://github.com/material-foundation/material-color-utilities/tree/main/typescript/dynamiccolor); npm `@material/material-color-utilities` 0.4.0 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@material/material-color-utilities/latest); pushed 2026-10-15 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/material-foundation/material-color-utilities)
- Adobe Leonardo (Apache-2.0): "generating color based on a desired contrast ratio"; `@adobe/leonardo-contrast-colors` exposes `generateContrastColors()`; built on d3-color for CIECAM02 — [Source](https://github.com/adobe/leonardo); npm 1.1.0 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@adobe/leonardo-contrast-colors/latest); pushed 2026-07-08 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/adobe/leonardo)
- Huetone ("A tool to create accessible color systems"): MIT, 456 stars, last push 2023-11-19 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/ardov/huetone)

**CSS platform features**
- `light-dark(a, b)` returns the first value for light/no preference and the second for dark; requires `color-scheme: light dark` (sections may override with `color-scheme: light|dark`); Baseline 2024 "Newly available" since May 2024 — [Source](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/color_value/light-dark)
- `color-mix()`: Baseline "Widely available" since May 2023; default interpolation space oklab; e.g. `color-mix(in srgb, var(--base) 50%, transparent)` — [Source](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/color_value/color-mix)
- `@property` registers typed custom properties (`syntax`, `inherits`, `initial-value`), making them animatable; Baseline 2024 since July 2024 — [Source](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/@property)
- Tailwind v4 itself relies on `@property` (for `--tw-*`), `color-mix(in oklab, ...)` for opacity, and cascade layers — [Source](https://tailwindcss.com/blog/tailwindcss-v4)

**Contrast**
- WCAG 3.0 Working Draft of 10 September 2026: "The contrast algorithm used in WCAG 3 is yet to be determined"; "it still has several years of work" — [Source](https://www.w3.org/TR/wcag-3.0/)
- APCA repo badge reads "Beta Non-Com"; `apca-w3` is "the approved code intended for public guidelines" — [Source](https://github.com/Myndex/SAPC-APCA); LICENSE.md is a custom "APCA — LICENSE and RESTRICTIONS": royalty-free only for non-commercial prediction of contrast in web content, commercial use requires a signed licence (apca-w3 web use covered by a W3 cooperative agreement), constants must not be changed, APCA/SAPC are trademarks — [Source](https://github.com/Myndex/SAPC-APCA/blob/master/LICENSE.md); npm `apca-w3` 0.1.9, "Limited W3 License", last modified 2022-07-04 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/apca-w3); repo pushed 2026-07-25 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/Myndex/SAPC-APCA)

### Inferences
- Recipe A (recommended), Radix port: vendor `generate-radix-colors.tsx` (MIT) with `colorjs.io`, `bezier-easing` and `@radix-ui/colors`; run it twice (appearance light/dark) with the user's accent, a chosen gray and the surface colour; write `--accent-1..12`, `--accent-a1..12`, P3 variants under `@supports`, and `--accent-contrast`. Map onto shadcn: `--primary: var(--accent-9)`, `--primary-foreground: var(--accent-contrast)`, `--ring: var(--accent-8)`, `--accent: var(--accent-3)`, `--accent-foreground: var(--accent-11)`, `--muted: var(--gray-3)`, `--border: var(--gray-6)`, `--input: var(--gray-7)`, `--foreground: var(--gray-12)`, `--muted-foreground: var(--gray-11)`. You inherit Radix's semantics (11/12 readable on 2) without hand-tuning.
- Recipe B, culori curve: convert the seed to OKLCH, fix hue, use lightness arrays per mode (light roughly .995, .98, .955, .925, .89, .85, .80, .73, seedL, seedL-.04, ~.50, ~.25; dark roughly .17, .20, .25, .29, .33, .38, .45, .55, seedL, seedL+.05, ~.80, ~.95), a chroma envelope peaking at the seed's chroma on step 9 and tapering to ~0.02 at both ends, `toGamut('p3')` / `clampChroma(c, 'oklch', 'rgb')` per step, alpha steps solved as `a = (step - bg) / (fg - bg)` per channel, then assert `wcagContrast(step11, step2) >= 4.5` and `wcagContrast(step12, step2) >= 7`. The numbers are starting points, not verified constants.
- Recipe C, Leonardo contrast-first: `generateContrastColors({ colorKeys: [seed], base: background, ratios: [1.05, 1.1, 1.2, 1.35, 1.55, 1.9, 2.4, 3.2, r9, r9 + 0.6, 4.6, 9] })` per mode (base = light or dark surface) so every step is defined by its contrast to the background; derive `r9` from the seed's own ratio against the surface. Most robust for arbitrary seeds, less "Radix-like" in saturation.
- Recipe D, HCT: `themeFromSourceColor(argbFromHex(seed))` then `TonalPalette.tone(t)` with tones per step (light roughly 99, 97, 94, 91, 87, 82, 76, 68, seedTone, seedTone-6, 40, 15; dark roughly 10, 13, 17, 21, 25, 30, 36, 45, seedTone, seedTone+6, 80, 95); perceptually uniform but needs its own gamut handling.
- Wiring: emit tokens on `:root` (light) and `.dark`/`[data-theme="dark"]`, plus `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` scoped to `[data-theme="system"]`; or emit each token as `light-dark(l, d)` and drive `color-scheme` from the `data-theme` attribute. Register `--accent-9` and friends with `@property { syntax: "<color>" }` so accent switches can animate. Expose to Tailwind with `@theme inline { --color-accent-1: var(--accent-1); ... }` so `bg-accent-3` / `text-accent-11` utilities exist. Use `color-mix(in oklch, var(--accent-9) 12%, transparent)` for ad-hoc tints without precomputed alpha steps.
- Density and font tokens: copy Radix Themes' structure: `--scaling` multiplier (90-110%), `--radius` base with derived steps (shadcn does this), Tailwind's `--spacing` base, and font-family tokens; a compact/comfortable switch is `--spacing: 3.5px | 4px | 4.5px` plus a one-step font-size shift.
- Contrast policy: gate with WCAG 2 (`wcagContrast`) and display APCA Lc as advisory via colorjs.io's `contrastAPCA` (as Radix does), which avoids depending on the stale, restrictively licensed `apca-w3` package.

### Gaps
- Radix docs do not describe the P3/alpha naming or OKLCH use; verified only from the shipped CSS. JS export names (e.g. `blueP3A`) were not confirmed (the unpkg `index.d.ts` fetch came back empty).
- Material 3 "how the system works" page is JS-rendered; the canonical tone-to-role mapping (40/80, 90/30, 100/20) was not verified.
- Exact first-supporting browser versions for `light-dark()` and `@property` were not captured (MDN compat tables truncated).
- Leonardo's interpolation colour spaces and any APCA option were not confirmed from the README.
- tweakcn feature specifics (OKLCH editing, AI theme generation, CSS import) were not verified beyond the README summary.

## UI kits, theming & shells — 3. Desktop shells compared: Electron, Tauri 2 and 3-alpha, native Swift + WKWebView, Electrobun, Neutralinojs, Wails v3, Flutter; precedents; trade-off matrix

### Takeaway
Only Chromium-based shells give an embedded browser the agent can drive over CDP today: Electron 44 (WebContentsView + `webContents.debugger` / `--remote-debugging-port` + Playwright `connectOverCDP`), Electrobun with `bundleCEF`, or the brand-new Tauri 3 alpha CEF runtime. Tauri 2 and a native Swift shell use WKWebView, which exposes only Safari Web Inspector, so they would delegate browsing to a separate Chrome/Playwright process. Deep macOS integration (App Intents, Keychain, Foundation Models, Accessibility) is Swift-only, so plan a Swift layer either way: a native app hosting the React UI, or Electron plus node-swift/napi-rs addons and a Swift helper target.

### Cited Findings
**Electron**
- Latest stable 44.9.0 (October 16, 2026): Chromium 152.0.7977.167, Node.js 24.22.0; 43.8.0 = Chromium 150.0.7871.250; 42.11.15 = Chromium 148.0.7778.280 — [Source](https://releases.electronjs.org/); npm `electron` 44.9.0, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/electron/latest); repo MIT, 123.4k stars, pushed 2026-10-18; v44.9.0 published 2026-10-17 UTC — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/electron/electron), [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/electron/electron/releases/latest)
- Electron 44.0.0 (August 25, 2026): Chromium 152.0.7977.54, Node 24.18.1, V8 15.2; macOS 13+ required (macOS 12 dropped); W3C Clipboard API; `windowStatePersistence`; Electron 43 EOL January 2027 — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/blog/electron-44-0)
- `WebContentsView` is a main-process `View` added via `win.contentView.addChildView(view)` and positioned with `setBounds`; each has its own `view.webContents` (`loadURL`), a WebContents can be shown in one view at a time, existing WebContents can be adopted; `BrowserView` is listed as Deprecated — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/web-contents-view)
- `<webview>`: "We currently recommend to not use the webview tag and to consider alternatives, like iframe, a WebContentsView, or an architecture that avoids embedded content altogether"; disabled by default since Electron 5 (`webviewTag`) — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/webview-tag)
- `webContents.debugger` is "An alternate transport for Chrome's remote debugging protocol": `attach('1.1')`, `sendCommand(method, params, sessionId)` (sessionId from `Target.attachToTarget`), `'message'` events; the session ends if DevTools is opened on that WebContents — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/debugger)
- `--remote-debugging-port`: "Enables remote debugging over HTTP on the specified port", set with `app.commandLine.appendSwitch('remote-debugging-port', '8315')` before `ready` — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/command-line-switches)
- Playwright `browserType.connectOverCDP(endpointURL)`: "only supported for Chromium-based browsers", "significantly lower fidelity than the Playwright protocol connection", default context via `browser.contexts()` — [Source](https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-browsertype); "Playwright has experimental support for Electron automation" via `_electron.launch`, Electron v21+, requires the `EnableNodeCliInspectArguments` fuse not set to false, native dialogs not intercepted — [Source](https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-electron); `playwright` 1.64.1, Apache-2.0 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/playwright/latest)
- Sandbox: renderer sandbox on by default since Electron 20; `nodeIntegration: true` disables it; sandboxed preloads get a limited `require`; untrusted remote content is "somewhat uncharted territory" (no Safe Browsing, vendor must ship updates) — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/sandbox)
- Updates: built-in `autoUpdater` (Squirrel.Mac / Squirrel.Windows) from static storage (`releases.json`) or a server; free update.electronjs.org for public GitHub repos with code-signed macOS builds; `update-electron-app` checks at startup and every ten minutes; Hazel/Nuts/Nucleus for private hosting — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/updates); signing/notarization needs an Apple Developer Program membership and Xcode; Forge uses `@electron/osx-sign` + `@electron/notarize`; `safeStorage`, `app.setLoginItemSettings()`, the `cookieEncryption` fuse and `autoUpdater` need a properly signed app — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/code-signing); `@electron/notarize` 3.1.2 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@electron/notarize/latest); `electron-updater` 6.8.9 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/electron-updater/latest); `electron-builder` 26.15.3, `@electron-forge/cli` 8.0.1, `@electron/rebuild` 4.2.1, `@electron/fuses` 2.1.3 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/electron-builder), [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@electron-forge/cli), [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@electron/rebuild), [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@electron/fuses)
- macOS integration APIs: `safeStorage` keys live in Keychain on macOS, Keychain access may block for a user prompt, apps should be code signed or macOS may re-prompt after updates — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/safe-storage); `Notification` uses UNNotification and macOS "requires an application to be code-signed in order for notifications to appear"; `hasReply`, `actions`, `sound`, `subtitle` — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/notification); `globalShortcut.register` fires without keyboard focus, fails silently on conflicts, media keys need accessibility trust — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/global-shortcut); `Tray` with `setTitle` text, Template images, `guid` position memory, rich macOS mouse/drag events — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/tray); `setAlwaysOnTop(flag, 'floating' | 'pop-up-menu' | 'screen-saver')`, `setVisibleOnAllWorkspaces(true, { visibleOnFullScreen })`, `setVibrancy('hud' | 'popover' | 'sidebar' ...)`, `setHiddenInMissionControl`, `setWindowButtonVisibility` — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/browser-window)
- Native addons: NAPI-RS builds "pre-compiled Node.js addons in Rust", MIT, CLI requires Node 22.13+ or 24+, ecosystem lists Cursor, Tauri, Bitwarden, Prisma, Next.js — [Source](https://napi.rs/); `@napi-rs/cli` 3.10.8 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@napi-rs/cli/latest); node-swift ("Create Node modules in Swift") bridges via Node-API with a `#NodeModule` macro and SwiftPM, MIT, 583 stars, lists "Using native macOS APIs and SwiftPM in an Electron app" as a use case — [Source](https://github.com/kabiroberai/node-swift); pushed 2026-09-20 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/kabiroberai/node-swift)
- Size: Electrobun's comparison says "a typical Electron app is about 85 MiB" (compressed macOS ARM64 download) — [Source](https://blackboard.sh/electrobun/)

**Tauri 2 and 3-alpha**
- crates.io: `tauri` max stable 2.12.4, newest 3.0.0-alpha.5 (2026-10-14) — [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/tauri); `wry` 0.57.0 — [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/wry); `tao` 0.37.1 — [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/tao); `objc2` 0.6.5 — [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/objc2); npm `@tauri-apps/cli` 2.12.3 and `@tauri-apps/api` 2.12.4, "Apache-2.0 OR MIT" — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@tauri-apps/cli/latest), [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@tauri-apps/api/latest); repo 112k stars — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/tauri-apps/tauri)
- GitHub releases on 14 Oct: `tauri` 3.0.0-alpha.5 (marked Latest) and 2.12.4, `tauri-runtime-cef` 3.0.0-alpha.6 with a macOS overlay-titlebar fix — [Source](https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/releases); `tauri-runtime-cef` = "Tauri runtime interface for Chromium Embedded Framework", 3.0.0-alpha.6, depends on `cef = 154.5.0` and `tauri ^3.0.0-alpha.5`; docs.rs shows builds for x86_64 macOS/Windows/Linux doc targets — [Source](https://docs.rs/crate/tauri-runtime-cef/latest); `cef` crate 154.5.0+154.0.34 — [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/cef)
- Blog: "Announcing Tauri 2.12" (Sep 26, 2026); "Experimental Tauri Verso Integration" (Mar 17, 2025, Servo-based engine) — [Source](https://v2.tauri.app/blog/)
- Multi-webview: `Window::builder` and `add_child` are "Available on crate feature unstable only"; "This is the multiwebview API: a single window can host any number of webviews, each of them positioned and sized by you"; Windows deadlock caveat for sync commands — [Source](https://docs.rs/tauri/latest/tauri/window/struct.Window.html); features `unstable`, `macos-private-api`, `tray-icon`, `devtools`, default `wry` — [Source](https://docs.rs/crate/tauri/latest/features)
- Sidecars: `bundle.externalBin`, binaries named `<name>-<target-triple>`, run via `Command.sidecar()` (`@tauri-apps/plugin-shell`) with `shell:allow-execute` capability — [Source](https://v2.tauri.app/develop/sidecar/); the Node.js sidecar guide compiles with `@yao-pkg/pkg` and notes "any other tool that can compile JavaScript or Typescript into a binary application will work"; alternatives listed: localhost servers, stdin/stdout, local sockets — [Source](https://v2.tauri.app/learn/sidecar-nodejs/)
- Updater: signatures mandatory ("cannot be disabled"), `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY`; artifacts macOS `.app.tar.gz` + `.sig`, Windows NSIS/MSI, Linux AppImage; static JSON or dynamic endpoint with `{{target}}`, `{{arch}}`, `{{current_version}}`; Rust 1.90+ — [Source](https://v2.tauri.app/plugin/updater/); macOS signing with a Developer ID Application certificate, `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY`, notarization via App Store Connect API key or Apple ID, free accounts cannot notarize — [Source](https://v2.tauri.app/distribute/sign/macos/)
- 30 official plugins incl. Global Shortcut, Notifications, Updater, Shell, Store, Stronghold ("Encrypted, secure database"), Localhost, Single Instance, Positioner, Window State, Autostart, Deep Linking; no keychain plugin — [Source](https://v2.tauri.app/plugin/); system tray via `TrayIconBuilder` / `TrayIcon.new`, `tray-icon` feature, tray events unsupported on Linux — [Source](https://v2.tauri.app/learn/system-tray/); `@tauri-apps/plugin-global-shortcut` 2.4.1 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@tauri-apps/plugin-global-shortcut/latest); `@tauri-apps/plugin-notification` 2.7.0 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@tauri-apps/plugin-notification); Rust `keyring` 4.2.0 ("All-in-One Rust Keyring") — [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/keyring); `tauri-plugin-keyring` 0.1.0 (2024-12) — [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/tauri-plugin-keyring); tauri-nspanel creates macOS `NSPanel` floating panels for Tauri v2 (PanelBuilder, `PanelLevel::Floating`), Apache-2.0/MIT, 427 stars, used by Handy, Cap, screenpipe — [Source](https://github.com/ahkohd/tauri-nspanel); crate 2.1.1 (2026-10-08) — [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/tauri-nspanel)

**Native Swift/AppKit + WKWebView**
- `WKWebView.isInspectable` (macOS 13.3 / iOS 16.4) defaults to false; inspection happens through Safari's Develop menu (Web Inspector); no other remote protocol is described — [Source](https://webkit.org/blog/13936/enabling-the-inspection-of-web-content-in-apps/)
- Foundation Models framework gives developers direct access to the ~3B-parameter on-device model; guided generation via the `@Generable` macro with OS-level constrained decoding; tool calling via the `Tool` Swift protocol; "not designed to be a chatbot for general world knowledge" (June 9, 2025) — [Source](https://machinelearning.apple.com/research/apple-foundation-models-2025-updates)
- macOS Tahoe 26 (June 9, 2025): Spotlight can run actions from Apple and third-party apps through the "App Intents API", with "quick keys"; Shortcuts can call Apple Intelligence models on-device or via Private Cloud Compute — [Source](https://www.apple.com/newsroom/2025/06/macos-tahoe-26-makes-the-mac-more-capable-productive-and-intelligent-than-ever/)

**Electrobun**
- Site: "Electrobun 2.0"; system webview by default, optional bundled Chromium (CEF) or WGPU surface; hello-world native main process ~1.28 MiB, Cottontail app ~15 MiB vs typical Electron ~85 MiB; differential updates with zig-bsdiff ("patches as small as 4 KB") self-hosted on S3/R2/static hosts; Warren UI templates experimental — [Source](https://blackboard.sh/electrobun/); repo MIT, 12.9k stars; Bun is the default runtime, Cottontail is "an experimental JSC-based alternative"; `bundleCEF` flag "bundles Chromium for version consistency at the cost of file size"; macOS 14+ Official, Windows 11+ x64 Official (ARM64 Beta), Ubuntu 24.04+ Official; built by Blackboard — [Source](https://github.com/blackboardsh/electrobun); npm `electrobun` 2.0.4, MIT (2026-10-18) — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/electrobun/latest); release v2.0.4 2026-10-18 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/blackboardsh/electrobun/releases/latest)

**Neutralinojs**
- Releases: v6.10.0 (06 Oct), v6.9.0 (24 Jul), v6.8.0 (03 Jun), v6.7.0 (02 Apr) — [Source](https://github.com/neutralinojs/neutralinojs/releases); v6.10.0 published 2026-10-06 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/neutralinojs/neutralinojs/releases/latest); MIT core with bundled third-party notices (webview, WebSocket++, Mbed TLS, cpp-httplib) — [Source](https://github.com/neutralinojs/neutralinojs/blob/main/LICENSE); no bundled Chromium or Node, uses the OS webview (gtk-webkit2 on Linux), WebSocket for native ops, embedded static server, backend logic through "extensions" IPC in any language — [Source](https://neutralino.js.org/docs/); `@neutralinojs/neu` 11.8.0, `@neutralinojs/lib` 6.10.0 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@neutralinojs/neu/latest), [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@neutralinojs/lib/latest)

**Wails**
- v3.0.0-beta.32 (18 Oct) "pre-release software. The API is stable, but you may still encounter issues before the final 3.0 release"; v2.17.0 is the latest stable — [Source](https://github.com/wailsapp/wails/releases); MIT, 36.6k stars; v2.17.0 published 2026-10-18 — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/wailsapp/wails), [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/wailsapp/wails/releases/latest)

**Flutter desktop**
- Windows, macOS and Linux targets; docs reflect Flutter 3.47; `flutter build macos`; desktop plugins federated — [Source](https://docs.flutter.dev/platform-integration/desktop)

**Precedents**
- VS Code: "The Electron shell used by Visual Studio Code" — [Source](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/supporting/faq)
- Goose: Apache-2.0, "Built in Rust"; desktop package `goose-app` 1.55.0 depends on `electron` 43.4.0, React 19.2, Electron Forge 7.11 with electron-builder for the Windows installer — [Source](https://github.com/block/goose), [Source](https://github.com/block/goose/blob/main/ui/desktop/package.json); repo now aaif-goose/goose, 55.4k stars — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/block/goose)
- OpenClaw: MIT, ~392k stars; Gateway is the local control plane; native companion apps for macOS, iOS, Android, Windows, Linux; stewarded by the OpenClaw Foundation — [Source](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw); macOS app is a "menu bar companion" requiring macOS 26.2+, Quick Chat on Option-Space, Sparkle updates, WebKit-rendered Mac tabs, permission prompts for screen/mic/speech/automation/accessibility, Node 24 or 26 Gateway — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/platforms/macos.md); the app no longer bundles Node or the Gateway, attaches to a running local Gateway or installs a per-user launchd service (`openclaw gateway install`), or connects remotely over SSH/Tailscale; quitting the app does not stop the Gateway — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/platforms/mac/bundled-gateway.md); `apps/macos` is a SwiftPM package (`Package.swift`, Sources, Tests) with per-profile Keychain services and notarized ZIP/DMG packaging — [Source](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/tree/main/apps/macos); v2026.10.5 (2026-10-18) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/openclaw/openclaw/releases/latest)
- Cherry Studio ships Electron build config — [Source](https://github.com/CherryHQ/cherry-studio); Jan moved to Tauri in v0.6.0 — [Source](https://github.com/menloresearch/jan/releases/tag/v0.6.0)
- Zed: "licensed primarily under GPL-3.0-or-later, with Apache-2.0 components"; Rust/`gpui` topics; macOS, Linux, Windows — [Source](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed); v1.24.2 (2026-10-14) — [Source](https://api.github.com/repos/zed-industries/zed/releases/latest)
- Raycast: "Extensions are built with React and TypeScript"; Windows app "built to feel like it belongs here, not like something ported over" (Nov 20, 2025) — [Source](https://www.raycast.com/blog/raycast-for-windows)

### Inferences
- Trade-off matrix (good / partial / poor):

| Criterion | Electron 44 | Tauri 2 (WKWebView) | Tauri 3 alpha + CEF | Native Swift + WKWebView | Electrobun (+CEF) | Neutralinojs | Wails v3 beta |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Embedded controllable browser (CDP) | good: WebContentsView + debugger / remote-debugging-port | poor: no CDP; multiwebview behind `unstable` | partial: alpha; CEF 154; Apple Silicon status unverified | poor: Web Inspector only; drive external Chrome via Playwright | partial: `bundleCEF`; CDP exposure undocumented | poor | poor |
| Deep macOS integration (App Intents, Keychain, FM, AX) | partial: node-swift/napi-rs addons + Swift extension target | partial: Rust objc2 + keyring; App Intents need a Swift target | partial (same) | good: first-class | partial: Zig/Rust native layer | poor | partial: Go/cgo |
| Shared React UI with the web server | good | good | good | good (WKWebView loads the same SPA) | good | good | good |
| Binary size / memory | poor: ~85 MiB compressed, Chromium RAM | good | poor: CEF is Electron-class | good: smallest | good: ~15 MiB (CEF adds Electron-class size) | good | good |
| Security sandboxing | good: Chromium sandbox default, mature docs | partial: capabilities/ACL + WKWebView process model | partial | good: App Sandbox + hardened runtime | partial: young | partial | partial |
| Update story | good: Squirrel / update.electronjs.org / electron-updater | good: signed updater plugin | partial: alpha | good: Sparkle or App Store | good: bsdiff patches | partial | partial |
| Linux server reuse | good: same Node core | partial: Node sidecar (pkg) or Rust core | partial | poor: no UI reuse, Swift core only | good: Bun core | partial | good: Go core |
| Maturity, Oct 2026 | good | good | poor: alpha | good | partial: 2.0, small team | partial | partial: beta.32 |

- For this app the two coherent designs are (a) Electron shell + Swift helper (node-swift or napi-rs addons, a signed Swift app-extension for App Intents) or (b) a native Swift menu-bar app hosting the React SPA in WKWebView with browsing delegated to a Playwright-driven Chromium process over CDP. OpenClaw is the precedent for (b); Goose, Cherry Studio and Claude Desktop for (a).
- Tauri 3's CEF runtime is the most interesting future option (Rust core + Chromium only when needed) but it is alpha and Apple Silicon coverage is unverified; not production-ready for 2026.
- Claude Desktop is Electron: local inspection on this Mac (2026-10-18) found `/Applications/Claude.app/Contents/Frameworks/Electron Framework.framework` (no public URL); Visual Studio Code's bundle carries the same framework.
- Electron 44's macOS 13+ floor and bundled Chromium 152 make Tailwind v4's Safari 16.4 floor moot for the Electron path; for WKWebView shells the floor is macOS 13.3+.
- App Intents, Shortcuts and Foundation Models are Swift frameworks; an Electron or Tauri app needs an embedded Swift app-extension or helper target signed with the same Team ID to participate (implementation detail unverified this session).

### Gaps
- Could not verify from a URL that ChatGPT macOS, Cursor or LM Studio are native/Electron (Cursor docs URLs 404; search budget exhausted); Raycast docs do not state Swift.
- Tauri `unstable` multiwebview stability on macOS, and whether `tauri-runtime-cef` or Electrobun's CEF expose a remote-debugging port, are not documented on fetched pages.
- No primary benchmark for Electron process memory; only Electrobun's size comparison.
- Apple developer documentation pages (Foundation Models, App Intents) were JS-rendered or 404 via fetch; OS minimums (macOS 26) are inferred from the WWDC25 timeline rather than quoted.
- Flutter desktop "stable since" version and `webview_flutter` macOS/Windows status not verified.

## UI kits, theming & shells — 4. Architecture for one React UI, two hosts: local server core + SPA, thin shells, IPC, auth, hotkey windows and tray

### Takeaway
Run the agent as a long-lived local server (Node/Bun) that serves the SPA and a typed SSE/WebSocket API; the macOS shell (Electron or Swift) is a thin client that supervises or attaches to that server via launchd, exactly like OpenClaw's Gateway and VS Code Server. Keep the server loopback-only by default, use Tailscale Serve identity headers or passkeys (WebAuthn needs a secure context: localhost or HTTPS) for tailnet/remote access, and implement hotkey, tray and menu-bar features in the shell layer against the same API.

### Cited Findings
- VS Code Server runs on the remote machine and a local client connects through a tunnel (`code tunnel`); its licence disallows hosting the server as a service and targets a single user — [Source](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/remote/vscode-server)
- OpenClaw Gateway: one long-lived daemon per host bound to `127.0.0.1:18789`; typed WebSocket API (first frame `connect`, then `req`/`res` frames and pushed `event` frames, validated against JSON Schema generated from TypeBox, with Swift models generated too); idempotency keys for `send`/`agent`; CLI, macOS app and web admin each hold one WebSocket; mobile/headless nodes join with `role: node` and declare capabilities; auth via shared token/password or identity-based modes (Tailscale Serve / trusted-proxy headers); device identity with challenge signing and pairing, loopback auto-approved; HTTP on the same port serves `/__openclaw__/canvas/` and A2UI assets; remote access via Tailscale/VPN or an SSH tunnel to 18789 — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/architecture.md); macOS app attaches to a running Gateway or enables a launchd LaunchAgent; app quit does not stop the Gateway — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/platforms/mac/bundled-gateway.md)
- Open WebUI runs as a single container/process serving UI and API on port 8080 (`open-webui serve`), Uvicorn Python backend and a Node-built frontend — [Source](https://docs.openwebui.com/getting-started/quick-start/)
- Tailscale Serve: `tailscale serve 3000` proxies a local port at `https://device-name.tailnet-name.ts.net` with automatic certificates; injects `Tailscale-User-Login`, `Tailscale-User-Name`, `Tailscale-User-Profile-Pic` and strips client-supplied copies; backend should listen only on localhost; Funnel is public and carries no identity headers — [Source](https://tailscale.com/kb/1312/serve)
- Secure contexts: `https`, `wss`, `file`, `127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`, `localhost`, `*.localhost` are potentially trustworthy; `http://example.com` is not — [Source](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Security/Secure_Contexts); `@simplewebauthn/server` 14.0.3, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@simplewebauthn/server/latest)
- tRPC v11 subscriptions over WebSockets (`wsLink`) or SSE (`httpSubscriptionLink`), SSE recommended as "easier to setup and doesn't require setting up a WebSocket server"; `tracked(id, data)` enables resume via `lastEventId`; 5xx errors auto-reconnect — [Source](https://trpc.io/docs/server/subscriptions); `@trpc/server` 11.19.0 — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/@trpc/server/latest); `hono` 4.13.13, MIT — [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/hono/latest)
- Protocol alternatives: AG-UI is event-based over HTTP/WebSockets with streaming, shared state and HITL — [Source](https://docs.ag-ui.com/introduction); assistant-ui speaks AG-UI, DataStream, ExternalStore and "AssistantTransport" (full agent-state snapshots) — [Source](https://www.assistant-ui.com/docs/runtimes/pick-a-runtime)
- Shell primitives: Electron `globalShortcut` works without focus — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/global-shortcut); `Tray` with macOS title text and Template images — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/tray); floating quick-entry via `setAlwaysOnTop('floating' | 'pop-up-menu')`, `setVisibleOnAllWorkspaces`, `setVibrancy`, `setHiddenInMissionControl` — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/browser-window); Tauri Global Shortcut, Positioner, Single Instance, Localhost, Autostart, Window State plugins — [Source](https://v2.tauri.app/plugin/); `TrayIconBuilder` — [Source](https://v2.tauri.app/learn/system-tray/); tauri-nspanel for NSPanel palettes — [Source](https://github.com/ahkohd/tauri-nspanel); OpenClaw Quick Chat opens on Option-Space — [Source](https://docs.openclaw.ai/platforms/macos.md)
- Signing prerequisites for shell features: Electron notifications and `safeStorage` require a code-signed app — [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/notification), [Source](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/code-signing)
- Tauri Node sidecar: compile with pkg, bundle as `externalBin`; talk via shell plugin stdout or "localhost servers, stdin/stdout, and local sockets" — [Source](https://v2.tauri.app/learn/sidecar-nodejs/)

### Inferences
- Reference layout: `core/` (Node or Bun; Hono/Fastify serving `dist/` + `/api`; SSE for agent streams, one WebSocket for bidirectional control; SQLite state) <-> `ui/` (React + assistant-ui + shadcn) <-> `shell-mac/` (Electron or Swift). The shell should attach to or install the core as a launchd agent (OpenClaw's pattern) rather than spawn it as a child, so the server survives shell restarts and the identical binary runs on the Linux host.
- IPC: prefer HTTP + SSE (tRPC `httpSubscriptionLink`, AI SDK data stream, or AG-UI events) for chat streams because it traverses Tailscale Serve and reverse proxies without WebSocket special-casing; keep one WebSocket (or OpenClaw-style schema-validated frames) for low-latency shell <-> core control such as "hotkey pressed", tray status and approvals. In Electron the renderer still talks HTTP to localhost; Electron IPC is only for native features.
- Auth tiers: (1) loopback-only with a token in a 0600 file the shell reads; (2) tailnet via Tailscale Serve identity headers with the core bound to 127.0.0.1; (3) passkeys (SimpleWebAuthn) when exposing HTTPS beyond the tailnet. WebAuthn requires a secure context, so plain-HTTP LAN IPs cannot register passkeys; terminate TLS with Tailscale certs or Caddy.
- Serving the SPA from the same binary: Hono `serveStatic`/Bun static routes over the Vite build (Bun `--compile` can embed assets; Tauri's Localhost plugin or Electron `protocol.handle` could instead load the SPA locally). Choose "always served by core" so both hosts exercise one code path and one version.
- Quick-entry window: Electron frameless window with `setAlwaysOnTop('pop-up-menu')`, `setVisibleOnAllWorkspaces(true, { visibleOnFullScreen: true })`, `vibrancy: 'hud'`, toggled by `globalShortcut`; in Tauri a non-activating NSPanel from tauri-nspanel plus the global-shortcut plugin; in Swift an `NSPanel` with `.nonactivatingPanel` hosting a second WKWebView pointed at a `/quick` route of the same SPA.
- Menu bar: Electron `Tray` (template icon plus `setTitle` status), Tauri `TrayIconBuilder`, or Swift `NSStatusItem`; keep tray state in the shell and feed it from core WebSocket events so the Linux web deployment needs no changes.

### Gaps
- VS Code `code serve-web` / openvscode-server and the full VS Code Server licence text were not fetched.
- Tailscale `tsnet` (embedding Tailscale in the server binary) was not verified this session.
- Bun single-file compile with embedded static assets and Node SEA details were not verified; Tauri's sidecar guide documents only pkg.
- How OpenClaw serves its web Control UI (beyond canvas/A2UI assets) is not covered by the fetched architecture page.
- SimpleWebAuthn RP-ID behaviour on localhost and passkey UX inside WKWebView/Electron were not verified.

Research date: 2026-10-18. Method: 24 web searches (budget then exhausted) + ~75 direct fetches of primary pages (official blogs/docs, GitHub, arXiv). OpenAI's domains (openai.com, help.openai.com) returned 403/JS-challenge to every fetch, so Pulse/Tasks facts come from dated secondary coverage and are flagged where unverified.

## Feed, ideas & goals — How existing products generate and personalize feeds

### Takeaway
Consumer "AI feeds" have converged on two recipes: (a) overnight LLM research over chats + memory + connected Gmail/Calendar rendered as 5–10 visual cards with explicit feedback (ChatGPT Pulse), and (b) RSS/publisher ingestion → story clustering → cited multi-source summaries with light topic preferences (Particle, Kagi News, Google Discover's AI summaries). The strongest signal for a self-hosted design is that OpenAI retired Pulse in June 2026 in favor of user-authored Scheduled tasks ("set up a daily briefing"), while Google moved the same idea into a Gemini "Daily Brief" (May 2026) — i.e., user-declared recurring briefs are the pragmatic default and fully autonomous feeds are costly to sustain.

### Cited Findings
- **ChatGPT Pulse (launch)** — Launched Sept 25, 2025 for $200/mo Pro subscribers as a new tab; generates personalized reports "while users sleep"; with Gmail connected it scans email overnight for important messages, with Google Calendar it builds an agenda; with memory on it draws on previous chats; users can give feedback on existing reports and ask for new automated reports; each report is a card with AI-generated image + text that opens into a full report you can query; 5–10 briefs per day, ending with "Great, that's it for today"; Plus access promised later, eventually all users (Fidji Simo: "the first step in that direction") — [TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2025/09/25/openai-launches-chatgpt-pulse-to-proactively-write-you-morning-briefs/). Official post (not fetchable, 403): [OpenAI](https://openai.com/index/introducing-chatgpt-pulse/).
