- **ChatGPT Pulse (status, Oct 2026: discontinued)** — On June 18, 2026 OpenAI announced a revamped "Scheduled tasks" feature (Go, Plus, Pro, Business, Enterprise; web + mobile; new "Scheduled" page in the sidebar; tasks can link to apps, e.g. "summarize emails as they arrive" or "notify when a package arrives"; desktop notifications) and said Pulse would be shut down within 14 days; users were told they can still get daily updates by creating a daily briefing task "based on interests, past chats, and connected apps" — [Gigazine](https://gigazine.net/gsc_news/en/20260619-chatgpt-scheduled-tasks/), citing [@ChatGPTapp on Pulse](https://x.com/ChatGPTapp/status/2067316962225684888) and [@ChatGPTapp on Scheduled tasks](https://x.com/ChatGPTapp/status/2067316960875168181).
- **Meta AI app Discover feed** — Meta AI app launched April 29, 2025 with a Discover feed described as "a place to share and explore how others are using AI": it shows the best prompts people choose to share, you can remix a prompt, and "nothing is shared to your feed unless you choose to post it". Meta AI's personalization (not the feed specifically) draws on memory (things you tell it or it picks up from context), your profile and content you like/engage with on Meta products, and both Facebook and Instagram if linked in Accounts Center; personalized responses were US/Canada at launch — [Meta Newsroom](https://about.fb.com/news/2025/04/introducing-meta-ai-app-new-way-access-ai-assistant/).
- **Meta Vibes** — Sept 25, 2025: a feed of short-form AI-generated videos in the Meta AI app and meta.ai; "Over time, Meta's algorithm will begin to show you personalized content"; users generate from scratch or remix feed videos, add visuals/music, post to Vibes, DM, or cross-post to Instagram/Facebook Stories and Reels; early version "partnered with AI image generators Midjourney and Black Forest Labs" (Alexandr Wang) — [TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2025/09/25/meta-launches-vibes-a-short-form-video-feed-of-ai-slop/).
- **Perplexity Discover** — A personalized Discover feed "tailored to individual user interests and languages" shipped Sept 16, 2024 — [AlternativeTo](https://alternativeto.net/news/2024/9/perplexity-ai-launches-personalized-discover-feed). Third-party write-ups describe topic + content-language selection and a "For You" tab — [Clubic](https://www.clubic.com/actualite-537599-perplexity-s-attaque-a-google-discover-avec-un-flux-personnel-dope-a-l-ia.html) (search-result summary, not fetched) — and say the AI Profile is used to surface relevant Discover content — [DEV Community](https://dev.to/dr_hernani_costa/perplexity-ai-profile-setup-personalize-your-home-experience-a47) (search-result summary, not fetched). Perplexity's help center lists no Discover article (only a 9-article Comet collection) — [Perplexity Help Center](https://www.perplexity.ai/help-center/en/).
- **Perplexity Comet** — Marketed as an AI browser that is "a personal assistant" (Mac, Windows, iOS, Android) to which you delegate tasks (inbox, groceries, finances, vacation planning), with a "Pause Comet Assistant" control; the page does not mention Discover or feeds — [Perplexity](https://www.perplexity.ai/comet).
- **Google Discover (ranking guidance)** — Discover shows content related to a person's interests "based on their Web and App Activity"; personalization "can be based on their search activity"; it "uses many of the same signals and systems as Search"; eligibility = indexed + content policies, no special tags; images ≥1200 px wide with `max-image-preview:large`; avoid clickbait/withholding; timely content and unique insights help; SafeSearch filtering; Discover traffic is "less predictable" and should be treated as supplemental — [Google Search Central](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/appearance/google-discover).
- **Google Discover (user feedback controls)** — Follow a topic from Search; card menu offers "Not interested in [Topic]" and "Don't show content from [Source]"; unhide under Settings > Interests > Not interested; like (heart) to save; personalization governed by "Search Services History" and "Personalized recommendations" toggles; location from home or IP estimate — [Google Help](https://support.google.com/websearch/answer/2819496).
- **Google Discover (history + AI headlines)** — Google Feed (July 2017, replacing Google Now cards, with a Follow button) was renamed Discover on Sept 24, 2018 with topic headers and more/less controls; content is matched to a user's familiarity with a topic via the Knowledge Graph; Sept 2025 added following individual sites and creators; from late 2025 Google replaced publisher headlines with AI-generated ones (first "a small UI experiment", later "performs well for user satisfaction"); Jan 2026 Nieman Lab found generated headlines sometimes inverted article meaning and were not clearly labeled — [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google_Discover).
- **Google Discover (AI summaries test, July 2025)** — Cards show several overlapping publisher icons (tap for a "More" sheet of source articles), ~3 lines of summary + "See more", and the disclaimer "Generated with AI, which can make mistakes"; seen on Android and iOS, "not widely available"; a separate test added a Save icon — [9to5Google](https://9to5google.com/2025/07/15/google-discover-ai-summaries/).
- **Particle (design)** — Founded by Sara Beykpour (ex-Twitter senior director of PM) and Marcel Molina (ex-Twitter/Tesla engineer); public iOS launch Nov 12, 2024; pipeline takes related coverage and generates bullet points, headline, sub-headline, extracted quotes and links, with "proprietary ranking algorithms" placing the most relevant link/quote first; uses GPT-4o/4o-mini, Anthropic, Cohere and non-LLM Google tech; claims accuracy errors cut from 1-in-100 to 1-in-10,000; "Opposite Sides" shows differing viewpoints; a red/blue "story spectrum" bubble chart shows lean and coverage volume per side; ELI5 and 5Ws modes, other languages, audio; Reuters/AFP/Fortune content via APIs with gold-highlighted links and better positioning; $4.4M seed + $10.9M Series A (Lightspeed); human editors curate the homepage — [TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2024/11/12/particle-launches-an-ai-news-app-to-help-publishers-instead-of-just-stealing-their-work).
- **Particle (status Oct 2026)** — particle.news is live ("Personalized news, summarized", © 2026 Mina Labs); story cards show how many articles each cluster aggregates; category tabs plus topic pages and 2026 midterm hubs; a new "Radar" product searches what 150,000+ podcasts say about a topic/company/person; iOS and Android apps — [particle.news](https://particle.news/).
- **Artifact** — Launched Jan 31, 2023 (private beta; open beta Feb 22, 2023) by Kevin Systrom and Mike Krieger (Nokto, Inc.); ML recommended articles "based on topics, news sources, and authors a reader was interested in"; reader reputation scores; AI voice narration; AI rewriting of user-flagged clickbait headlines; later a social layer (posts, links, likes, comments); ~160k waitlist, 7 employees; shutdown announced Jan 12, 2024 because the market opportunity "isn't big enough to warrant continued investment in this way"; Yahoo acquisition reported April 2, 2024 for an upgraded Yahoo News app, which relaunched with the tech in June 2024 — [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artifact_(app)).
- **Artifact → Yahoo** — Deal closed March 29, 2024; Artifact "will be integrated across its network to enhance personalization," with Yahoo News the main beneficiary; founders stayed on only as advisors — [recommender-systems.com](https://recommender-systems.com/news/2024/04/03/yahoo-acquires-artifact/); press release (403 on fetch): [Business Wire](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20240402939591/en/Yahoo-announces-the-acquisition-of-Artifact-the-news-discovery-platform-created-by-Instagram-cofounders-Kevin-Systrom-and-Mike-Krieger).
- **Readwise Reader (API)** — Reader API v3: `POST /api/v3/save/` (50/min; optional `html`, `summary`, `tags`, `location`, `category`), `GET /api/v3/list/` (20/min; filters `updatedAfter`, `location`, `category`, up to 5 tags; cursor paging; `withHtmlContent`), `PATCH /update/<id>/`, `PATCH /bulk_update/` (≤50), `DELETE`, `GET /tags/`; documents expose `reading_progress`, `location`, `tags`, `summary`, `notes`, `word_count`, `reading_time`, `first_opened_at`, `last_opened_at`, `saved_at`; webhooks for document/highlight changes — [Readwise Reader API](https://readwise.io/reader_api).
- **Readwise Reader (Ghostreader)** — Default prompts: document-level "Generate thought-provoking questions", "Extract key takeaways and to dos", "Draft newsletter blurb"; passage-level "Explain", "Expand", "Pick up where I left off", "Translate"; "Summarize the document" runs automatically on manually saved documents and writes to the metadata summary field; feed items are not auto-summarized unless you add your own OpenAI key; auto-tagging is an experimental off-by-default prompt; default model GPT-5 Mini included in the subscription — [Readwise Docs](https://docs.readwise.io/reader/guides/ghostreader/default-prompts).
- **Readwise (Daily Review / spaced repetition)** — A document's chance of appearing is proportional to its share of your highlights (100 of 500 ≈ 20%), tunable per source type and per document; "Mastery" uses decaying recall probability with half-lives of 7/14/28 days ("soon/later/someday"), resurfacing items once recall ≤50%, lowest first; actions: Discard (soft), Master, Feedback (rate recall), Favorite, Keep; Themed Reviews with custom schedules; audio playback; quality filter hides fragments — [Readwise Docs](https://docs.readwise.io/readwise/docs/faqs/reviewing-highlights).
- **Matter (status 2026)** — Still marketed as a read-later app for iPhone/iPad/web with a browser extension; saves articles, threads, PDFs; transcribes YouTube videos and podcasts to time-synced text; newsletters via Gmail sync or a Matter email address; "follow writers and feeds"; synthesized-speech audio playlists; the page lists no AI summaries, no pricing, and no shutdown notice — [getmatter.com](https://getmatter.com/). TechCrunch listed Matter first among Pocket alternatives in May 2025 — [TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2025/05/27/read-it-later-app-pocket-is-shutting-down-here-are-the-best-alternatives/).
- **Kagi News (design)** — Launched Sept 30, 2025; reads "thousands of RSS feeds" and does not scrape; sources live in a public GitHub repo anyone can PR; one briefing per day around noon UTC, readable in ~5 minutes; AI condenses feeds into the briefing with per-story citations; no tracking/profiling; users select and reorder categories, adjust story count and sections; Kagi "deliberately avoids personalizing feeds to match existing preferences"; content translated via Kagi Translate — [Kagi Blog](https://blog.kagi.com/kagi-news).
- **Kagi News (open source)** — `kagisearch/kite-public`: MIT-licensed Vite front end plus community-curated feeds; app data at kite.kagi.com/kite.json is CC BY-NC 4.0 (commercial licensing via Kagi); `kite_feeds.json` maps categories → RSS URLs, a category needs ≥25 feeds to appear, core feeds in `core_feeds.py`; stories are "summarized by language models" and auto-translated; third-party front ends exist (Raycast, Pebble); clustering method not documented — [GitHub kagisearch/kite-public](https://github.com/kagisearch/kite-public). Free, no account, web/iOS/Android — [Nieman Lab](https://www.niemanlab.org/2025/10/kagi-news-is-an-ai-powered-app-for-keeping-up-with-the-world/) (search-result summary; direct fetch returned 403).
- **Folo (ex-Follow)** — "Open-source AI RSS reader" (AGPL-3.0 with an icon-set exception; 39.1k stars; browser/iOS/Android/macOS/Windows/Linux); Folo AI answers questions over an article, feed, folder, or everything (bring your own model/API key; MCP services); "Timeline summary" condenses hundreds of entries; AI translation (EN/ZH/JA/FR) and on-demand or automatic summaries; "AI tasks" schedule daily/weekly briefings delivered to chat or email; sources: RSS/Atom/JSON, thousands of RSSHub routes, a newsletter inbox address; rule-based "Actions" (filter, star, rewrite, notify, summarize); "Spotlight" keyword highlighting; integrations with Obsidian, Readwise, Zotero — [GitHub RSSNext/Folo](https://github.com/RSSNext/Folo).
- **Microsoft Copilot Daily** — Oct 1, 2024: a morning news + weather briefing read aloud in a Copilot voice, drawn only from authorized publishers (Reuters, Axel Springer, Hearst, USA TODAY Network, FT), US/UK first, "personalization and controls" promised later; "Copilot Discover" offers conversation starters customized from your use of other Microsoft services — [Microsoft](https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2024/10/01/an-ai-companion-for-everyone/).
- **Gemini Daily Brief** — 2026.05.19 release note: "Daily Brief is your personalized overview of what matters today," powered by Personal Intelligence over connected apps like Gmail and Calendar, rolling out to all Google AI subscribers 18+ in the US — [Gemini release notes](https://gemini.google/release-notes/).

### Inferences
- The feedback vocabulary is stable across products and worth copying verbatim: topic follow/unfollow, "Not interested in [topic]", "Don't show from [source]", like/save, plus Pulse-style "ask for a new recurring report". None of the surveyed products exposes implicit dwell-time tuning to the user; Readwise's per-source "frequency" slider is the only transparent weighting control.
- Pulse's hard cap (5–10 cards, explicit "that's it for today") and Kagi's once-a-day digest are both anti-doomscroll designs; a self-hosted agent should default to a bounded daily batch rather than an infinite feed.
- The Pulse → Scheduled-tasks pivot and Gemini's Ultra-only "Spark" suggest open-ended autonomous research is expensive per user; the cheap, durable primitive is "user-declared recurring brief + optional agent-suggested new briefs".
- Multi-source story clustering with citations (Particle's per-cluster article counts, Kagi's citations, Google's publisher-icon stack) is now the expected presentation for news-type items; single-source cards look dated.
- Kagi's explicit anti-personalization stance is a useful design dial: offer a "serendipity / no-profile" section alongside the personalized one.

### Gaps
- OpenAI pages (Pulse launch post, Tasks/Scheduled tasks help article) could not be fetched (403 + JS challenge via WebFetch and curl). Unverified from memory: Pulse launched as a *mobile* preview, the "curate" and thumbs-up/down controls by name, Plus-tier rollout timing, and the exact shutdown date (Gigazine implies by ~July 2, 2026).
- Perplexity publishes no fetchable official documentation of Discover personalization; details rest on third-party coverage.
- Artifact specifics asked for ("TikTok for text", 10-article onboarding, reading-time signals, AI summaries) could not be verified: The Verge is blocked for fetching and Wikipedia does not mention them.
- Whether Vibes *replaced* the Discover feed (claimed in search-result summaries) is unverified; the Meta newsroom Vibes post URL could not be located.
- Matter pricing and any AI features in 2026 are not stated on its site; Apple News+ was not covered (search budget exhausted).
- Which signals the Meta AI Discover feed itself uses for ranking is not documented by Meta.

## Feed, ideas & goals — Architectures for interest profile → sourcing → ranking → LLM summarization with feedback loops

### Takeaway
A self-hosted pipeline can be built almost entirely on free/open sources (RSS via Miniflux/FreshRSS/Folo, HN Firebase, arXiv, GDELT, Bluesky Jetstream, Readwise/Karakeep for saved items) with paid search APIs (Brave $5/1k, Exa $4–15/1k, Tavily ~$0.005–0.008/credit, Perplexity Search $1–5/1k) only for gap-filling, since Bing's News API is gone (Aug 11, 2025) and NewsAPI's free tier is dev-only. Ranking should be a cheap retrieval stage (embedding similarity + recency decay + dedup/clustering) followed by an LLM reranker over a small candidate set, with a contextual-bandit-style exploration slot and explicit negative feedback written back to a text interest ledger.

### Cited Findings
- **Bing News/Web Search APIs retired** — "Public Bing Search and Bing Custom Search APIs were retired on 11th August 2025" (docs archived; News Search listed among them) — [Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/bing/search-apis/). Retirement notice AZ492574 covered F1/S1–S9 Bing Search and F0/S1–S4 Custom Search tiers and pointed to "Grounding with Bing Search" in Azure AI Agents — [Message Center AZ492574](https://mc.merill.net/message/AZ492574); Simon Willison flagged the retirement May 14, 2025 — [simonwillison.net](https://simonwillison.net/2025/May/14/bing-search-api-is-being-retired).
- **Brave Search API** — Search $5 per 1,000 requests with $5 free credits/month, 50 QPS; "Answers" $4/1k + $5 per M tokens, 2 QPS; endpoints include web, news, images, videos, local, "LLM Context" and Answers; storing results or training on them requires a plan with storage rights — [Brave](https://brave.com/search/api/).
- **Exa** — Per 1k requests (≤10 results): instant $4, fast/auto $7, deep/deep-lite $12, deep-reasoning $15; extra results $1/1k; contents $1 per 1k pages per content type; free tier $10 credits resetting monthly, no card — [Exa pricing](https://exa.ai/pricing).
- **Tavily** — 1,000 free credits/month; search basic 1 credit, advanced 2; extract 1 credit per 5 URLs; map 1 per 10 pages; research 4–250 credits; paid $0.008/credit pay-as-you-go down to $0.005 (Growth: 100k credits/$500) — [Tavily docs](https://docs.tavily.com/documentation/api-credits); endpoints Search, Extract, Research, Crawl, Map — [tavily.com](https://www.tavily.com/).
- **Perplexity Sonar / Search API** — sonar $1/$1 per M input/output tokens plus $5/$8/$12 per 1k requests for low/medium/high search context; sonar-pro $3/$15 plus $6/$10/$14 per 1k; sonar-reasoning-pro $2/$8; sonar-deep-research $2/$8 plus $2/M citation tokens, $3/M reasoning tokens and $5 per 1k search queries; standalone Search API $5/1k (standard) or $1/1k (fast) — [Perplexity docs](https://docs.perplexity.ai/getting-started/pricing).
- **SerpAPI** — Free 250 searches/mo; Developer $75/5,000; Production $150/15,000; Big Data $275/30,000 — [SerpAPI](https://serpapi.com/pricing).
- **NewsAPI** — Developer tier free but 100 requests/day, 24-hour article delay, 1-month history, localhost-only CORS, "cannot be used in a staging or production environment"; Business $449/mo for 250k requests (real-time, 5-year history); Advanced $1,749/mo for 2M — [NewsAPI](https://newsapi.org/pricing).
- **GDELT DOC 2.0** — Full-text search over global news with modes ArtList, ArtGallery, TimelineVol, TimelineTone, ToneChart, word clouds; default window last 3 months, searchable back to Jan 1, 2017; English queries across 65 machine-translated languages; JSON/RSS/CSV/JSONFeed output; 15-minute timeline resolution for spans <72h — [GDELT blog](https://blog.gdeltproject.org/gdelt-doc-2-0-api-debuts/).
- **Hacker News Firebase API** — `https://hacker-news.firebaseio.com/v0/`; `topstories`/`newstories` up to 500 IDs, `beststories`, ask/show/job up to 200, `item`, `user`, `maxitem`, `updates` (changed items/profiles); "There is currently no rate limit"; MIT — [GitHub HackerNews/API](https://github.com/HackerNews/API).
- **arXiv API** — "No more than one request every three seconds" with a single connection, across all your machines; metadata is CC0; RSS feeds, OAI-PMH and bulk S3 access available — [arXiv API ToU](https://info.arxiv.org/help/api/tou.html).
- **Bluesky Jetstream** — Delivers decoded JSON over WebSocket (vs the relay's DAG-CBOR+CAR firehose), supports live and historical replay, filtering by `collections` (NSIDs or wildcards like `app.bsky.feed.*`, ≤100), `dids` (≤10,000) and `kinds`; public v2 hosts `wss://jetstream.us-west.bsky.network` and `wss://jetstream.us-east.bsky.network`; `?cursor=` resume (inclusive, at-least-once) within "a bounded lookback window" — [bsky.network docs](https://bsky.network/docs/jetstream); dual MIT/Apache-2.0 — [GitHub bluesky-social/jetstream](https://github.com/bluesky-social/jetstream).
- **YouTube Data API quota (as documented Oct 2026)** — 10,000 units/day combined for most endpoints, with separate default limits of 100 calls/day each for `search.list` and `videos.insert`; `videos.list`, `playlistItems.list`, `channels.list`, `activities.list` cost 1 unit; quota extension via form — [Google](https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/determine_quota_cost).
- **Pocket shutdown** — Mozilla announced the shutdown in May 2025; export deadline Oct 8, 2025; stated reason "the way people are browsing the web is changing"; alternatives named include Matter, Instapaper, Raindrop.io, Readwise Reader, Wallabag, Readeck, Karakeep, Obsidian Web Clipper — [TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2025/05/27/read-it-later-app-pocket-is-shutting-down-here-are-the-best-alternatives/); Pocket's farewell page cites changed habits, Firefox Tab Groups/bookmarks, and focusing resources — [getpocket.com/farewell](https://getpocket.com/farewell).
- **Omnivore** — "The Cloud application was deprecated in November of 2024"; now self-hosted only (AGPL-3.0; 16.3k stars; docker compose with Postgres, API, web, content-fetch and a Chromium `puppeteer-parse` service); community keeps fixes going — [GitHub omnivore-app/omnivore](https://github.com/omnivore-app/omnivore).
- **Karakeep (formerly Hoarder)** — Self-hostable bookmarks/notes/images app, AGPL-3.0, ~29.8k stars; LLM auto-tagging and summarization with Ollama support; REST API; automatic saving from RSS feeds; Chrome/Firefox/Safari extensions; imports from Pocket, Omnivore, Linkwarden, Chrome — [GitHub karakeep-app/karakeep](https://github.com/karakeep-app/karakeep).
- **Wallabag** — Self-hosted read-later (MIT, 13.0k stars) or hosted at wallabag.it — [GitHub wallabag/wallabag](https://github.com/wallabag/wallabag).
- **Miniflux** — Apache-2.0, 9.8k stars, Go; REST API with Go/Python clients; Fever and Google Reader API compatibility; 25+ integrations incl. Readwise Reader and Wallabag; CSS-selector scraper rules + Readability; no AI features — [GitHub miniflux/v2](https://github.com/miniflux/v2).
- **FreshRSS** — AGPL-3.0, 16.3k stars; multi-user; Google Reader API (recommended) and Fever API; XPath/JSON scraping for sites without feeds; WebSub push; extension ecosystem; no AI features — [GitHub FreshRSS/FreshRSS](https://github.com/FreshRSS/FreshRSS).
- **NewsBlur** — MIT, 7.7k stars; "training": like/dislike authors, tags, titles and feeds to hide or highlight stories; full-text expansion and search; MCP server (premium), IFTTT, third-party apps — [GitHub samuelclay/NewsBlur](https://github.com/samuelclay/NewsBlur).
- **Open-source "Pulse-like" projects** — The `chatgpt-pulse` GitHub topic has no repositories — [GitHub topic](https://github.com/topics/chatgpt-pulse). The `personalized-news` topic lists only small projects: `rizumita/feed-curator` (23 stars, "developer RSS briefings powered by Claude Code"), `deepak-terse/brief-engine` (ingest→dedup→cluster→summarize→deliver, local-LLM tags), `CainPatel/tech-digest` (self-hosted Telegram digest that "learns from thumbs up/down"), `TakalaWang/codex-chronicle` (self-updating personal newspaper), `Saibharath19/ai-news-curator` — [GitHub topic](https://github.com/topics/personalized-news).
- **LLMs as zero-shot rerankers** — Hou et al., "Large Language Models are Zero-Shot Rankers for Recommender Systems" (arXiv:2305.08845, ECIR 2024): recommendation framed as conditional ranking over a candidate list given interaction history; LLMs rank well zero-shot but "struggle to perceive the order of historical interactions" and show popularity and position bias, mitigated by prompting/bootstrapping; competitive when candidates come from multiple generators — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.08845).
- **MIND news-rec benchmark** — ~160k English Microsoft News articles, 15M+ impression logs, 1M users; MIND-small = 50k users; Microsoft Research License (research only); leaderboard top AUC 0.7326 — [msnews.github.io](https://msnews.github.io/).
- **Contextual bandits for news (LinUCB)** — Li, Chu, Langford, Schapire, "A Contextual-Bandit Approach to Personalized News Article Recommendation" (WWW 2010, arXiv:1003.0146): article choice from user/article context updated by click feedback; offline evaluation on logged random traffic; 12.5% click lift over context-free bandit on a 33M-event Yahoo! Front Page dataset — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1003.0146).
- **TikTok/ByteDance Monolith** — "Monolith: Real Time Recommendation System With Collisionless Embedding Table" (arXiv:2209.07663, ORSUM@RecSys 2022): collisionless embedding table with expirable embeddings and frequency filtering; online training so the model learns from "time-sensitive customer feedback" as it arrives — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.07663).
- **Twitter/X open-sourced recsys** — `twitter/the-algorithm` (AGPL-3.0, 74k stars): candidate sources (search-index ≈50% in-network, tweet-mixer, UTEG graph, FRS), light ranker (Earlybird) and neural heavy ranker (the-algorithm-ml), SimClusters and TwHIN embeddings, home-mixer, trust-and-safety filters — [GitHub](https://github.com/twitter/the-algorithm).
- **LLM-recsys survey** — Zhao et al., "Recommender Systems in the Era of Large Language Models" (arXiv:2307.02046; IEEE TKDE vol. 36 no. 11, Nov 2024): pre-training, fine-tuning and prompting paradigms; LLMs as feature encoders — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.02046).
- **ONCE** — Liu et al., "ONCE: Boosting Content-based Recommendation with Both Open- and Closed-source LLMs" (arXiv:2305.06566, v4 Aug 31, 2023): open-source LLM layers as content encoders + closed-source LLM prompting to enrich training data; up to 19.32% relative improvement — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.06566).
- **Generative News Recommendation (GNR)** — Gao et al. (arXiv:2403.03424, WWW 2024): LLM-generated theme-level + semantic-level dual representations; retrieves related news filtered by user preference; UIFT training fuses multiple articles into one coherent, factually consistent narrative — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03424).
- **PNR-LLM** — LLM-enriched titles/abstracts aggregated into user/news embeddings, outperforming baselines on MIND (arXiv:2504.20452, Apr 2025) — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20452) (search-result summary).
- **Sleep-time compute (overnight pre-research)** — Lin et al. (arXiv:2504.13171): pre-computing on context before queries arrive cuts test-time compute ~5x at equal accuracy and lifts accuracy up to 13–18%; sharing across related queries cuts cost 2.5x; gains scale with query predictability — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13171).

### Inferences
- Reference architecture: (1) **Interest ledger** = a versioned Markdown/JSON file of topics, entities, goals, explicit blocks and recent accept/reject events (human-readable, LLM-editable, like Devin's `MEMORY.md`), plus a derived embedding centroid per topic for recall. (2) **Sourcing** = RSS/OPML through Miniflux or FreshRSS (both expose Google Reader/Fever APIs) + HN + arXiv RSS + Jetstream filtered to followed DIDs + saved items via Readwise `list?updatedAfter` or Karakeep; paid search (Brave news endpoint, Exa, Tavily) only for ledger topics with no feed coverage. (3) **Normalize/dedup** = canonical URL + MinHash over text for near-duplicates, then embedding clustering into stories (Particle/Kagi presentation). (4) **Retrieve** = cosine similarity to topic centroids × exponential recency decay, keep top ~50. (5) **Rerank** = LLM reranker prompted with the ledger text (2305.08845 shows candidate order/popularity bias, so shuffle candidates and cap list length). (6) **Explore** = reserve 1–2 of the 5–10 slots for a bandit arm (LinUCB-style on topic features) so new interests can emerge. (7) **Summarize** = per-cluster map-reduce with mandatory citations and a Particle-style "other perspectives" section. (8) **Feedback** = thumbs/"not interested"/"less from source" written back to the ledger immediately and to the bandit reward log; nightly LLM pass consolidates the ledger (Devin "Dreaming" pattern).
- Cost envelope: at 2026 list prices, 30 Exa "fast" searches/day ≈ $0.21/day, 30 Brave searches/day fits inside the $5 monthly credit, and Tavily's 1,000 free credits ≈ 33 basic searches/day; RSS + HN + arXiv + Jetstream are free, so a single-user daily brief can run under ~$10/month before LLM tokens.
- Text ledger vs vectors: keep both — the ledger is what the LLM reranker and summarizer actually consume and what the user can audit/edit; vectors only gate recall. Google's and NewsBlur's exposed controls (topic/source blocks, like/dislike by author/tag) map naturally onto ledger entries.
- Evaluation: offline replay on your own logged impressions (the LinUCB offline-evaluation trick) plus MIND for sanity-checking ranking code; online metrics worth tracking are thumbs-up rate, open/expand rate per card, "cards reached before quitting", and the ratio of exploration-slot accepts.
- Diversity: apply MMR-style penalization on embedding similarity between already-chosen cards; cap one card per story cluster and one per source domain.

### Gaps
- Reddit Data API terms/rate limits: reddit's policy pages returned 403 to every fetch; the 100 queries/min per OAuth client and commercial-approval requirements could not be verified here.
- Instapaper API docs are JavaScript-rendered and returned only a title; Full API scope/approval requirements unverified.
- Google News RSS has no official documentation page found; treat `news.google.com/rss` search feeds as undocumented/unstable.
- No open-source "Pulse clone" with meaningful adoption was found; the repos above are hobby-scale and were not inspected beyond their topic listing.
- Jetstream's replay-window length, Kagi's clustering algorithm, and the date Twitter open-sourced the-algorithm (blog path says 2023) are not stated on the fetched pages.
- 2025–2026 papers specifically on LLM-*generated* personalized feeds beyond GNR/PNR-LLM were not surveyed (search budget exhausted before a dedicated pass); MMR and MinHash were not re-verified against primary sources.

## Feed, ideas & goals — Proactive suggestion systems and goal extraction

### Takeaway
As of Oct 2026 every major assistant offers user-authored recurring/scheduled work (ChatGPT Scheduled tasks, Gemini scheduled actions, Claude Code routines + Desktop scheduled tasks + `/loop`, Notion custom agents with schedule/event/Slack triggers), while agent-initiated proactivity is either retired (Pulse), Ultra-tier beta (Gemini Spark), or narrow (Apple Suggestions, Motion at-risk alerts). Research now treats proactivity as a cost-sensitive gating decision (ProactiveBench F1 66%, PRISM −23% false alarms, ProAgentBench's 28k real events) and finds human agreement on *when* to interrupt is near chance — so a self-hosted agent should batch suggestions into scheduled briefs, learn an accept/reject model, and induce skills/goals from observed repetition (AWM/ASI/SkillWeaver; Devin "Dreaming"; OpenClaw Skill Workshop) rather than interrupt in real time.

### Cited Findings
- **ChatGPT Tasks → Scheduled tasks** — Official help article exists at [OpenAI Help](https://help.openai.com/en/articles/10291617-scheduled-tasks-in-chatgpt) (403 on fetch). The June 2026 relaunch: tasks run at a chosen date/time or recurrence, available to Go/Plus/Pro/Business/Enterprise on web and mobile, managed from a "Scheduled" sidebar page, can be linked to apps ("summarize emails as they arrive"), with desktop pop-up notifications; it replaced Pulse for daily briefs — [Gigazine](https://gigazine.net/gsc_news/en/20260619-chatgpt-scheduled-tasks/).
- **Gemini scheduled actions** — Rolled out June 6, 2025 to Google AI Pro/Ultra and qualifying Workspace plans; ask Gemini in conversation to do something at a time or turn an existing prompt into a recurring action; examples: morning calendar + unread-email summary, "five blog ideas every Monday", one-time post-event summaries; managed on a scheduled-actions settings page — [Google blog](https://blog.google/products/gemini/scheduled-actions-gemini-app/).
- **Gemini Agent / Personal Intelligence / Spark** — Release notes: 2025.03.13 opt-in "Personalization" using Search history; 2025.06.19 scheduled actions; 2025.11.18 "Gemini Agent" ("experimental tool that can complete multi-step tasks from start to finish", Ultra, US web, uses Gmail/Calendar, confirms before critical actions); 2026.01.20 "Personal Intelligence" beta connecting Gmail, Photos, YouTube, Search (off by default); 2026.05.19 "Daily Brief" and "Gemini Spark — your 24/7 personal AI agent" that "works proactively on your behalf" (Ultra beta, US 18+); 2026.06.30 Spark on macOS — [Gemini release notes](https://gemini.google/release-notes/).
- **Claude Code routines (cloud)** — Research preview; a routine = prompt + repos + connectors run on Anthropic-managed (or self-hosted) infra; triggers: schedule (hourly/daily/weekdays/weekly presets, custom cron via `/schedule update`, minimum interval 1 hour, one-off runs), API (`POST .../routines/<id>/fire` with per-routine bearer token under beta header `experimental-cc-routine-2026-04-01`; optional `text` arrives wrapped as untrusted `<routine-fire-payload>`), GitHub events (pull_request, release, with field filters); Pro/Max/Team/Enterprise; create at claude.ai/code/routines, Desktop, or `/schedule`; limits 100 scheduled runs/hour/account and 30 run-now/API fires/hour/routine; runs autonomously without permission prompts — [Claude Code docs](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/routines).
- **Claude Code Desktop scheduled tasks (local)** — Run on your machine only while the app is open and awake; 1-minute minimum interval; presets Manual/Hourly/Daily/Weekdays/Weekly, or natural language ("remind me at 3pm tomorrow…" creates a self-disabling one-off); one catch-up run for misses within 7 days; per-task permission mode with saved "always allow"; prompt stored as `~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/<name>/SKILL.md`; a running task can reschedule itself via the `update_scheduled_task` MCP tool — [Claude Code docs](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/desktop-scheduled-tasks).
- **Claude Code `/loop` (session-scoped)** — `CronCreate`/`CronList`/`CronDelete` tools with 5-field cron; up to 50 tasks per session; recurring tasks expire after 7 days; `/loop <prompt>` without interval lets Claude self-pace between 1 minute and 1 hour; `.claude/loop.md` customizes the default maintenance prompt; fires only while the session is idle — [Claude Code docs](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/scheduled-tasks).
- **Anthropic Agent Skills** — Launched Oct 16, 2025: folders of instructions, scripts and resources that Claude loads only when relevant; available in Claude apps (Pro/Max/Team/Enterprise), Claude Code (plugins from `anthropics/skills` or `~/.claude/skills`), and the API (`/v1/skills`, requires Code Execution beta); an interactive "skill-creator" skill interviews you and writes SKILL.md; Dec 18, 2025 update added org-wide management, a partner skills directory and Agent Skills as an open standard; the post does **not** describe automatic skill creation from repeated tasks, only "simplified skill creation workflows" as a goal — [Claude blog](https://claude.com/blog/skills).
- **OpenClaw skills / ClawHub** — Skills are `SKILL.md` directories (YAML frontmatter `name`/`description`), discovered with precedence workspace > project > personal > managed > workshop > bundled; load-time gating via `metadata.openclaw.requires` (binaries, env vars, config, OS); **"Skill Workshop" lets agents create and update their own skills** — changes apply immediately, are announced, keep prior versions for undo, and learned skills are visible only to the creating agent; ClawHub install `openclaw skills install @owner/<slug>`, `openclaw skills verify` checks a trust envelope; publishing via separate `clawhub` CLI — [OpenClaw docs](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/skills). Registry size claims vary: 13,000+ skills in early 2026 — [Fast.io](https://www.fast.io/resources/openclaw-clawhub-setup/) — vs 67,000+ by Aug 2026 — [parallel.ai](https://parallel.ai/articles/best-clawhub-skills-for-openclaw) (both search-result summaries); no verified-publisher system is noted — [travis.media](https://travis.media/blog/openclaw-skills-guide/) (search-result summary).
- **Notion 3.0 Agents** — Launched Sept 18, 2025; agents take multi-step actions for up to 20 minutes across hundreds of pages; instructions live in a Notion page the post calls a "memory bank"; Custom Agents that run "on schedules or triggers you set" were "coming soon" at launch; the post does not claim agents infer goals on their own — [Notion blog](https://www.notion.com/blog/introducing-notion-3-0).
- **Notion Custom Agents (now shipped)** — Triggers: recurring (daily/weekly/monthly/yearly, weekend options, time + timezone), Notion events (comment added, page added/removed, property updated, AI Meeting Note finished) with property/view filters, Slack (message posted, emoji reaction, @-mention, keyword filters); multiple triggers per agent; Business/Enterprise plans; "Create with AI chat" drafts instructions, triggers and access; agents access only explicitly granted pages/databases; sub-agent hand-offs — [Notion help](https://www.notion.com/help/custom-agents).
- **Reclaim.ai** — Acquired by Dropbox in **August 2024** (not 2025): 22-person team joined, 320k+ users across 43k+ companies, Google Calendar-only at the time with Outlook planned, pricing unchanged — [TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2024/08/22/dropbox-acquires-index-ventures-backed-ai-scheduling-tool-reclaim-ai). Habits = flexible recurring calendar blocks with per-habit windows, ideal days, flexibility and frequency, auto-rescheduled around conflicts (PTO, overrunning meetings), priority levels, 130+ templates, Google and Outlook calendars; branded "Reclaim.ai from Dropbox" — [Reclaim](https://reclaim.ai/features/habits).
- **Motion** — AI time-blocks tasks by priority, dependencies, deadline and duration and re-optimizes as things change; separates "plan to work on" date from due date; proactively warns of at-risk tasks "days or weeks in advance", predicts project completion and alerts when off track; gathers tasks from email, meetings and messages; AI notetaker produces action items — [usemotion.com](https://www.usemotion.com/).
- **Microsoft Copilot** — Oct 1, 2024 "AI companion" launch: Copilot Daily briefing, Copilot Vision (opt-in, ephemeral), Think Deeper; Copilot is said to adapt to "mannerisms and preferences over time" and, with permission, is expected to eventually act on your behalf (e.g., follow-ups after an appointment) — [Microsoft](https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2024/10/01/an-ai-companion-for-everyone/).
- **Apple Intelligence (proactive features)** — "Suggestions" offer one-tap actions from conversation context in Mail (Messages "coming in a future update"); Siri personal context and onscreen awareness; Call Context surfaces relevant app info during calls; Safari "Notify Me" monitors pages for price drops/restocks; Siri AI is in beta on iOS/iPadOS/macOS/watchOS/visionOS 27, English first, not initially in the EU on iOS — [Apple](https://www.apple.com/apple-intelligence/).
- **Cursor rules** — Project rules (`.cursor/rules/*.mdc`, auto-attached by `globs`), user rules, `AGENTS.md`, team rules; `/create-rule` in chat creates a rule from a conversation — [Cursor docs](https://cursor.com/docs/context/rules).
- **Devin Memory and "Dreaming"** — Devin saves memories autonomously (no approval) as one-line bullets with source-session links in a Git "memory drive" (`MEMORY.md` read at session start); **Dreaming is a background session about once a day** that reviews recent sessions and notes to merge overlaps, drop stale/transient details, "add useful lessons that weren't captured during the work" and reorganize the index; memory is per-user, while Skills are deliberately written procedures scoped repo/personal/org/enterprise; automation-started sessions don't read/write memory — [Devin docs](https://docs.devin.ai/product-guides/memory.md). Devin Coach gives inline prompt-drafting suggestions (five waste patterns) with admin acceptance analytics — [Devin docs](https://docs.devin.ai/enterprise/features/devin-coach.md).
- **Proactive Agent / ProactiveBench** — Lu et al., "Proactive Agent: Shifting LLM Agents from Reactive Responses to Active Assistance" (arXiv:2410.12361, Oct 2024): collect human activities → generate proactive task predictions → humans label accept/reject → train a reward model as judge; ProactiveBench has 6,790 events; fine-tuned model reaches F1 66.47% — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12361).
- **ContextAgent** — Yang et al. (arXiv:2505.14668, NeurIPS 2025): proactive agent over wearable video/audio context plus personas from history; ContextAgentBench with 1,000 samples, 9 daily scenarios, 20 tools; +8.5% proactive-prediction and +6.0% tool-calling accuracy — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14668).
- **Proactive dialogue survey** — Deng, Lei, Lam, Chua, "A Survey on Proactive Dialogue Systems" (arXiv:2305.02750, IJCAI 2023 survey track): systems that steer conversation toward system-side goals; strategic/motivational interaction — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.02750).
- **Sleep-time compute** — Lin et al. (arXiv:2504.13171, Apr 2025): offline "thinking" on context before queries; ~5x less test-time compute for equal accuracy, up to +13%/+18% accuracy on Stateful GSM-Symbolic/AIME, 2.5x lower cost across related queries; benefit tracks query predictability — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13171).
- **ProAgentBench (2026)** — Tang et al., "ProAgentBench: Evaluating LLM Agents for Proactive Assistance with Real-World Data" (arXiv:2602.04482, Feb 2026): 28,000+ events from 500+ hours of real user sessions (burstiness B=0.787); splits proactive assistance into timing prediction and content generation; long-term memory/history significantly improves prediction; real data beats synthetic — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04482).
- **PRISM (2026)** — Fu et al., "PRISM: Festina Lente Proactivity — Risk-Sensitive, Uncertainty-Aware Deliberation for Proactive Agents" (arXiv:2602.01532, Feb 2026): cost-sensitive selective intervention — intervene only when "a calibrated probability of user acceptance exceeds a threshold" derived from asymmetric miss/false-alarm costs; fast path by default, slow counterfactual mode near the boundary; −22.78% false alarms and +20.14% F1 on ProactiveBench — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01532).
- **Intervention timing is unreliable (2026)** — Modgil, "The Saturation Trap and the Subjectivity of Intervention Timing" (arXiv:2606.04296, June 2026; v2 Oct 9, 2026): on SWE-bench-Verified traces three trained annotators agreed on *where* to intervene only slightly above chance (Krippendorff's α = +0.047; best pairwise κ = +0.349); affect-threshold triggers saturate and fire on 39–83% of actions; concludes single-annotator F1 is an unsuitable optimization target — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04296).
- **Unified proactive decision framework (2026)** — Tang et al., "Proactive Service Agents: A Unified Decision Framework, Methods, and Evaluation" (arXiv:2609.03727, Sept 2026): agent chooses silent / ask / assist / act as a POMDP constrained by authorization and risk, with costs for interruption, misunderstanding, overreach and privacy and an explicit "option value of waiting"; four-stage pipeline (state/need estimation → intervention gating → action construction → feedback adaptation); metrics for triggering, timing, calibration, user burden, safety and policy value; "offline classification performance does not predict real deployment benefit" — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03727).
- **Proactive programming support** — Pu et al., "Assistance or Disruption?…" (arXiv:2502.18658; DOI 10.1145/3706598.3713357): Codellaborator initiates help from editor activity and task context; N=18 within-subjects; proactive agent raised efficiency but caused workflow disruption; presence indicators and interaction context reduced disruption — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18658).
- **Skill induction research** — Voyager (arXiv:2305.16291): automatic curriculum + ever-growing library of executable-code skills; 3.3x unique items, 2.3x distance, 15.3x faster milestones in Minecraft — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16291). Agent Workflow Memory (arXiv:2409.07429): induce reusable workflows from past trajectories offline or online; +24.6% Mind2Web, +51.1% WebArena relative success, advantage grows with train/test distribution gap — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07429). SkillWeaver (arXiv:2504.07079): agents discover, practice and package skills as APIs; +31.8% WebArena, +39.8% real sites; strong-agent APIs lift weak agents up to 54.3% — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07079). Agent Skill Induction (arXiv:2504.06821): program-based skills induced and verified during tasks; +23.5% vs static agent, +11.3% vs text skills, 10.7–15.3% fewer steps — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06821). Learn-by-interact (arXiv:2501.10893): synthesize trajectories from documentation with "backward construction" of instructions; up to +12.2% ICL (Claude-3.5) and +19.5% fine-tuning (Codestral-22B) on SWE-bench/WebArena/OSWorld/Spider2-V — [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.10893).

### Inferences
- Goal extraction is nowhere productized as autonomous inference: Pulse conditioned on chats + memory + connected apps, Notion and Gemini rely on user-authored instructions/schedules, Devin writes memories from corrections. A defensible design is an LLM pass over conversation/tool logs that emits *candidate* goals into the interest ledger with source links, surfaced via an "ask" action for confirmation (the 2609.03727 framework's cheapest intervention) rather than acted on silently.
- Idea/skill ideation from repeated tasks should follow AWM/ASI: mine recurring tool-call sequences, draft a SKILL.md (Anthropic/OpenClaw format is now a de-facto standard), verify it by replay, and *propose* it; OpenClaw's agent-private auto-skills and Devin's unapproved memories are the only shipped precedents for silent writes, and both scope them to the agent/user and keep undo history.
- Timing: given near-chance human agreement on interruption points (2606.04296) and Codellaborator's disruption findings, prefer scheduled delivery windows (Pulse/Daily Brief/Desktop scheduled tasks) and in-context "presence" hints over real-time interrupts; reserve immediate interrupts for user-declared triggers (Notion-style events, Safari-style "Notify Me" watches).
- Gate every proactive item with an acceptance-probability threshold learned from thumbs/accept-reject logs (ProactiveBench reward-model pattern, PRISM asymmetric costs); start with a high threshold and few slots, and track calibration and user burden, not just F1 (2609.03727).
- Overnight pre-research is justified by sleep-time compute's results and is cheapest when queries are predictable — exactly the case for ledger topics and recurring briefs; Devin's daily "Dreaming" is the operational template for a nightly consolidation job that also updates the ledger and drafts new scheduled-task suggestions.
- Implementation on this stack: Claude Code Desktop scheduled tasks (local files, 1-minute granularity, self-rescheduling via `update_scheduled_task`) or cloud routines (no machine required, API trigger for webhooks) already provide the scheduler; the agent app only needs to own the ledger, the feed store, and the feedback endpoints.

### Gaps
- ChatGPT Tasks' original Jan 2025 details (10 active-task limit, beta plans, whether ChatGPT proactively suggested tasks) could not be verified: OpenAI pages are blocked and guessed TechCrunch URLs returned 404.
- Cursor "Memories" (auto-generated from chats) documentation was not found at the expected URLs; Devin's `knowledge` page 404'd (Memory/Dreaming page used instead).
- Microsoft Copilot proactive features after Oct 2024 and Apple "Priority Notifications" were not verified (search budget exhausted; Apple's page does not mention them).
- Google's "Agent Mode" naming from I/O 2025 was not verified; release notes call the shipped feature "Gemini Agent" (Nov 18, 2025).
- Whether OpenClaw's Skill Workshop proposes skills *after detecting repeated tasks* (vs on request) is not stated in its docs; ClawHub size figures conflict.
- No dedicated 2023–2026 literature pass on user-goal/intent mining from dialogue logs was possible; ProactiveBench/ProAgentBench cover task prediction from activity streams but not conversational goal extraction specifically.
- Reclaim.ai post-acquisition product changes (2025–2026) and Motion pricing were not found on the fetched pages.

## Precedents — What architecture do OpenClaw, Hermes Agent and Khoj converge on, and what does that imply for a new self-hosted agent app?

### Takeaway
The three most-cited self-hosted personal agents of 2026 converge on the same shape: one long-running "gateway" daemon (WebSocket/HTTP) that owns sessions, channel adapters, a scheduler (cron + periodic "heartbeat"), file-based memory and a skills directory, with thin clients (CLI, web UI, native macOS app, mobile "nodes") attached. OpenClaw's decision to remove its "inferred commitments" experiment in v2026.8.1 is a concrete data point that implicit goal extraction from chat history under-delivered compared with explicit, user-created automations.

### Cited Findings
- OpenClaw is an open-source, self-hosted AI assistant that runs on your own hardware and is reached from chat apps; it is developed by the OpenClaw Foundation (an independent 501(c)(3)), has no paid tier, and is MIT licensed; it requires Node 26 (recommended) or Node 24.16+/26.1+ plus a model-provider API key — [OpenClaw docs](https://docs.openclaw.ai/)
- OpenClaw's components: a single Gateway process that is "the single source of truth for sessions, routing, and channel connections"; the agent (tool use, sessions, memory, multi-agent routing); clients (CLI, web Control UI, macOS app, iOS/Android nodes); channels (Discord, Signal, Telegram, WhatsApp, iMessage, Slack, Matrix, Microsoft Teams, plus plugins); multi-provider models with failover and local model services; on-demand "Skills"; automation via cron jobs, hooks and webhooks; configuration in `~/.openclaw/openclaw.json`; DMs share the main session by default while each group chat gets its own session, with allowlists and mention rules — [OpenClaw docs](https://docs.openclaw.ai/)
- Gateway internals: one long-lived daemon per host; WhatsApp via Baileys and Telegram via grammY; clients connect over WebSocket on the configured bind (default `127.0.0.1:18789`); the same port serves `/__openclaw__/canvas/` (hosted widget documents) and `/__openclaw__/a2ui/` (A2UI renderer assets); a typed WS API validates frames against JSON Schema and emits `agent`, `chat`, `presence`, `health`, `heartbeat` and `cron` events; protocol types are defined with TypeBox, which generates JSON Schema and then Swift models — [OpenClaw architecture](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/architecture)
- Wire protocol: first frame must be `connect`; requests are `{type:"req", id, method, params}` with `{type:"res"}` replies and pushed `{type:"event"}` frames that are not replayed; side-effecting methods (`send`, `agent`) require idempotency keys backed by a short-lived dedupe cache — [OpenClaw architecture](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/architecture)
- The macOS app connects as an operator client and also acts as a node exposing `canvas.*` commands; nodes (macOS, iOS, Android, headless) connect with `role: node`, a device identity and declared capabilities such as `camera.*`, `screen.record`, `location.get`; WebChat is a static UI over the same WS API — [OpenClaw architecture](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/architecture)
- Security model: every WS client carries a device identity; new device IDs need approval and then receive a device token; loopback connections can be auto-approved while tailnet/LAN/non-local connections need explicit approval; clients sign a `connect.challenge` nonce (payload v3 binds platform and deviceFamily); shared-secret auth via `gateway.auth.mode` token/password, plus `gateway.auth.allowTailscale: true` and a `trusted-proxy` mode that authenticate from request headers; `gateway.auth.mode: "none"` is warned against on untrusted ingress; remote access is recommended via Tailscale/VPN or an SSH tunnel (`ssh -N -L 18789:127.0.0.1:18789 ...`), with optional TLS pinning — [OpenClaw architecture](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/architecture)
- Scheduling: the Gateway kernel runs one `GatewayScheduler` driven by a single host timer; stores persist deadlines and schedules are rebuilt at startup; after sleep each due registration runs once, periodic jobs wait for the prior run, and missed ticks are coalesced; plugins receive `PluginServiceSchedulerV1` — [OpenClaw architecture](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/architecture)
- Automations: a built-in scheduler handles recurring and one-shot work (one-shot `--at`, interval, or cron expression) with session context options isolated/current/named/main and delivery to a chat channel, a webhook endpoint, or silent; inbound webhook triggers are supported — [OpenClaw automation](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation)
- Heartbeat is "a system-owned monitor automation" that runs a periodic turn in the main session every 30 minutes by default, uses a small "monitor scratch" context, skips with reason `empty-heartbeat-file` when scratch is empty, and defers while the main queue or other automation work is busy — [OpenClaw automation](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation)
- Hooks: internal event hooks are `HOOK.md` scripts fired on `/new`, `/reset`, `/stop`, session compaction, gateway startup and message flow (managed with `openclaw hooks`); plugin hooks use typed `api.on(...)` handlers to intercept tool calls in-process; webhooks are authenticated HTTP ingress for external triggers — [OpenClaw automation](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation)
- Guidance on cron vs heartbeat: use automations for exact timing, reports, reminders, standalone tasks, a different model, or independent schedules with their own job history; use heartbeat for ambient periodic main-session awareness and quiet alerts; "The inferred commitments experiment was removed in v2026.8.1, so heartbeat no longer extracts follow-ups from conversations. Explicit automations are the replacement." — [OpenClaw automation](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation)
- Heartbeat defaults: `every: "30m"` (becomes `1h` when Anthropic OAuth/token auth is configured and `heartbeat.every` is unset; `0m` disables); `HEARTBEAT.md` is now legacy and was migrated by `openclaw doctor --fix` into database-stored monitor scratch (archived under `backups/heartbeat-migration/`), so the runtime never reads the file; the default prompt body is "Follow the heartbeat monitor scratch context when provided." and instructs the agent not to infer or repeat old tasks from prior chats; heartbeat runs use the same system prompt as ordinary turns; `lightContext` (default `false`) skips workspace bootstrap files; `isolatedSession` (default `false`); quiet replies are `NO_REPLY` (legacy `HEARTBEAT_OK` is accepted at the start/end and dropped when the remainder is at most 300 characters); delivery `target` is `owner` (default) / `last` / `none` / a channel id, with `directPolicy` allow/block — [OpenClaw heartbeat](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/heartbeat)
- Secondary sources (not official; flagged) describe cron jobs and run history persisting in a shared SQLite database inside the Gateway process, with main-session, isolated, custom-session and command-payload execution styles — [ampere.sh](https://www.ampere.sh/blog/cron-jobs-heartbeats-making-openclaw-proactive); note that unofficial mirrors still describe `~/.openclaw/HEARTBEAT.md` as the live task list, which the official heartbeat page now contradicts — [clawdocs.org](https://clawdocs.org/architecture/heartbeat)
- Hermes Agent (Nous Research) is an MIT-licensed open-source agent marketed as "The Agent That Grows With You"; it "auto-generates skills" from its work, keeps conversations, memories and skills in a back-up-able data directory, lets you bring your own provider or use Nous Portal ("200+ Models"), lives in Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Email and CLI, schedules reports/backups/briefings in natural language that run unattended through the gateway, runs isolated subagents, browses the web, and sandboxes execution in local, Docker, SSH, Singularity and Modal backends; a desktop app exists for macOS 12+ and Windows 10/11 and Linux installs via terminal — [Hermes Agent site](https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/)
- Hermes Agent repo: MIT; roughly 255k stars and 55.5k forks at fetch time; mixed Python (`cli.py`, `hermes_state*.py`; Python 3.14) and JS/TS (`ui-tui`, `web`) with Node.js; a `providers/` directory supporting Nous Portal, OpenRouter, OpenAI and custom endpoints switched via `hermes model`; memory is "agent-curated" with periodic prompts to save knowledge, session history searched with SQLite FTS5 plus LLM summarization, user modeling via Honcho, and an OpenClaw migration path that references `MEMORY.md`/`USER.md`; skills are created autonomously after complex tasks, refined during use, and follow the agentskills.io open standard (`skills/`, `optional-skills/`); one gateway process serves all channels plus a cron scheduler with delivery to any platform; sandbox backends are local, Docker, SSH, Singularity, Modal, Daytona and Vercel Sandbox; docs at hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs — [GitHub NousResearch/hermes-agent](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent)
- Secondary reporting dates Hermes Agent's release to February 2026 and a v0.20.0 release on August 3 (2026) adding streaming conversational voice, on-device wake words and an agent-to-agent protocol — [runtimewire](https://runtimewire.com/article/nous-research-hermes-agent-v0200-voice-a2a)
- Khoj ("Your AI second brain. Self-hostable.") is AGPL-3.0 (flag: copyleft for a hosted service), had 37.7k stars / 2.5k forks at fetch time, offers custom agents (own knowledge, persona, chat model, tools), automations ("personal newsletters and smart notifications", automated repetitive research), semantic search over PDFs/Markdown/Notion/Word/org-mode/images, local or online models (llama3, qwen, gemma, mistral, gpt, claude, gemini, deepseek), integrations for browser, Obsidian, Emacs, desktop, phone and WhatsApp, Docker self-hosting, a Python stack (pyproject.toml, uv.lock) and a hosted app at app.khoj.dev — [GitHub khoj-ai/khoj](https://github.com/khoj-ai/khoj); a third-party comparison claims Khoj Cloud was deprecated in April 2026 — [hjarni.com](https://hjarni.com/compare/hjarni-vs-khoj.md), which conflicts with the README still advertising the cloud app; a directory lists the latest release as 2.0.0-beta.28 (2026-03-26) — [opensourcealternatives.to](https://www.opensourcealternatives.to/item/khoj)
- 2026 roundups (vendor-written; treat as opinion) group the self-hosted field as OpenClaw, Hermes Agent, Dify, OpenHands and Agent Zero — [SSD Nodes](https://www.ssdnodes.com/learn/best-self-hosted-ai-agents) — and as Hermes Agent, AnythingLLM, Jan.ai, Leon and PyGPT, while cautioning that many lists confuse local model runners with actual personal assistants — [Vellum](https://website.vellum.ai/blog/best-open-source-personal-ai-assistants)

### Inferences
- The "one React UI, two hosts" goal maps directly onto the gateway pattern: a TypeScript daemon exposing a typed WebSocket/HTTP API, a web SPA served from the same port, and a native macOS client that speaks the same schema. OpenClaw's TypeBox -> JSON Schema -> Swift pipeline is a ready-made recipe for keeping a TS server and a Swift shell in sync.
- Device pairing with signed challenges, loopback auto-approval and Tailscale-aware auth is the pragmatic security baseline for a personal server that is also reachable remotely; both OpenClaw and Hermes assume Tailscale/SSH rather than public ingress.
- Proactive behavior in shipping systems is implemented as a cheap periodic "heartbeat" turn with a tiny scratch context plus explicit scheduled jobs, not as continuous background agents. OpenClaw's defaults (30 min, 1 h under Anthropic OAuth) reflect cost and rate-limit pressure.
- OpenClaw shipping and then removing automatic follow-up extraction ("inferred commitments") is the strongest field evidence found that silently mining goals from chats produces stale or unwanted tasks; the safer design is to surface candidate goals for one-tap confirmation and to store confirmed ones as explicit automations.
- Hermes Agent's SQLite FTS5 + LLM-summarization memory and auto-generated skills (agentskills.io format) show that a file/SQLite-first memory with no vector DB is viable for a personal agent, and that "skill ideation from repeated tasks" is already a product feature to benchmark against.
- Khoj's AGPL license matters if any part of the app is offered as a hosted service; OpenClaw and Hermes (MIT) are safer reference codebases to borrow from.

### Gaps
- OpenClaw's current release number and its memory/browser/skills pages were not retrieved here (the shared web-search budget was exhausted; the memory system is covered in the Memory section and provider abstraction in the Models section).
- Hermes Agent's desktop app framework (Electron/Tauri/native) and exact latest release tag were not visible on the fetched pages.
- Khoj's hosted-cloud status in late 2026 is contradicted between its README and a third-party comparison; unresolved.
